# System of Work Assistant `packages/contracts/` — Build Guide

> **You're in `packages/contracts/`.** This file plus root `CLAUDE.md` both load. The root file covers global project conventions + shared comm rules (track-prefix, escalation taxonomy, messaging budget); this file owns code-area conventions for shared contracts & domain.

## Launch protocol

| Working on... | cwd | Loads |
|---|---|---|
| Planning / docs / commits | repo root (`SoW-build/`) | root `CLAUDE.md` only |
| shared contracts & domain code | `packages/contracts/` | this `CLAUDE.md` + root |

<!-- For a multi-area project, add a row per additional code area. -->

If you find yourself fighting the wrong conventions, check your cwd.

## Session start/end protocol

**At session start:**
1. Read `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (repo root) **by section, not whole** — `grep -n "^##" IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` for offsets, then Read with offset/limit just "Currently in progress" + the active phase. (The file grows; never load it whole.)
2. Confirm with the user what feature this session is targeting.
3. Read the relevant section of `ARCHITECTURE.md` from the lookup table below.

**At session end** (only when the user explicitly says we're done):

1. **Implementer runs `/session-end`.** Implementer writes ONLY:
   - `packages/contracts/` code files (the slice's implementation)
   - test files (the slice's tests)
   - dependency manifest / lockfile (deps the slice adds)
   - `docs/sessions/<NNN>-<date>-<topic>.md` (session doc, created at `/session-end` Step 5)

   **Implementer must NOT touch (all orchestrator territory).** *This list is the canonical statement
   of the territory rule — `/session-end`, the brief template, and the generated
   `scripts/guards/territory-guard.sh` PreToolUse hook (which mechanically enforces it in team mode)
   all point here.*
   - `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`
   - `packages/contracts/LESSONS.md`
   - `packages/contracts/CLAUDE.md` (entire file — both the Cross-doc invariants table AND the Lessons logged index)
   - `ARCHITECTURE.md`
   - `docs/orchestrator-briefing.md` / `docs/tdd-brief-template.md` / `docs/briefs/` / `docs/runbooks/`
   - other top-level deliverable / design docs
   - `.gitignore` and root-level dotfiles (unless adding a new artifact to ignore, flagged at Step 9)

   At Step 10: **explicit `git add <path>` per slice file; never `git add -A`/`.`; never stage an orchestrator-territory file.** Changes to any orchestrator-territory file (a new cross-doc model, a lesson, an arch note) are **flagged at Step 9**, not edited here — the orchestrator writes them hot (root `CLAUDE.md` + the Step-9 matrix).

2. **Orchestrator runs `/orchestrate-end`** for round close-out + Carry-forward triage + round terminal commit + push.

Don't paste these sections into the prompt. Grep the file:section, read only what you need. `/check-arch <topic>` dispatches off this table.

|---|---|---|
| <subsystem A> | `ARCHITECTURE.md` | §X |
| <subsystem B> | `ARCHITECTURE.md` | §Y |

## Stack

<!-- ▼ EXAMPLE BLOCK [id=area-stack]: stack quick-reference for implementer sessions. Canonical stack lives in root CLAUDE.md + ARCHITECTURE.md; this is the cheat sheet. ▼ -->
- **Runtime:** Node 22 LTS + TypeScript 5.x (strict)
- **Stack:** pure TypeScript types + Zod + JSON Schema (ajv) + Drizzle schema source
- **Validation:** Zod + JSON Schema (ajv)
- **Lint / types / tests:** ESLint / tsc --noEmit / Vitest
- **Territory (this track owns):** `packages/contracts/, packages/domain/`
<!-- ▲ END EXAMPLE BLOCK [id=area-stack] ▲ -->

## Standard commands

```bash
# Install deps (run once; re-run when the manifest changes)
pnpm install

# Run the dev server (if applicable)
pnpm --filter <pkg> dev   # e.g. desktop / worker

# Tests
pnpm test

# Quality
pnpm lint
pnpm format:check
pnpm typecheck

# Preflight (use before saying "done" with a feature)
pnpm lint && pnpm typecheck && pnpm test
```

## TDD protocol

**Write the failing test first.** Applies to deterministic code — see the TDD posture in root `CLAUDE.md` for what is test-first vs. exempt.

**Commit per slice when practical.** Never bundle a safety-critical slice with anything else.

## Forbidden patterns

<!-- ▼ EXAMPLE BLOCK [id=forbidden-patterns]: forbidden patterns — 3-5 narrow, enforceable, domain-specific rules. Shape: "Don't <pattern X> because <reason / past incident>; use <alternative Y>." Test-pin them where possible. Starts small; accretes as lessons surface. ▼ -->
Do not:

1. **Write code without a failing test first** (deterministic code — every model, validator, state machine).
2. **Import any app- or adapter-side code** — `packages/contracts` and `packages/domain` are PURE (the §2.5 import-direction root). They depend on nothing downstream; a boundary test pins this.
3. **Change a seam model's field set without editing `ARCHITECTURE.md` Appendix A + its checked-in schema-snapshot in the same round** — seam models are frozen contracts shared across all 6 tracks; a silent field change is a cross-track Finding.
4. **Emit a model without its JSON Schema + a `spec(§3)`-tagged schema-snapshot test** — the schema gate (REQ-S-006) and the no-inference rule (REQ-F-017) depend on the schema being authoritative.
5. **Throw across a subsystem boundary** — return a typed `Result<T,E>` with enumerable failure variants (§16 error convention).
6. **Re-define a safety predicate/sanitizer that already exists in a lower package** — RE-EXPORT it. A second definition diverges silently from the grammar or guard it tracks (L39/L9: the region-marker neutralizer must live where `REGION_MARKER_RE` lives). Pin it three ways, because a name-grep pins the NAME and not the DEFENSE: referential identity to the canonical symbol, a DEFINITION census (`function` AND `const` forms — an `export const` re-fork is the shape that actually happens), and a MATCHER-LITERAL census (a re-forked regex with the same name is the real hazard). `pattern: [ "$(grep -rlE 'export (function|const) neutralizeRegionMarkers' --include='*.ts' packages apps | grep -vE '/(test|dist|node_modules)/' | wc -l | tr -d ' ')" = 1 ]`
7. **Assert a safety posture from a constant, a default-seed, or a hardcoded string** — DERIVE it from the governing durable state (L56: four rule-5 issues in one round shared this shape). The test: change the governing state and assert the claim moves; a claim that cannot move is a decoration that reads as a guarantee. `pattern: [ -z "$(grep -rnE '\"(local-only|zero-egress)\"|>[^<]*(local-only|zero-egress)' --include='*.tsx' apps/desktop/renderer 2>/dev/null | grep -v /test)" ]`
8. **Cite a plan task or another document's section as a bare `§N.x`** — `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s real numbered subsections are **`§19.x` AND `§2.5`**; every other `§N.x` is shorthand (L101). ⛔ **CLASSIFIER CORRECTED 2026-08-11 (24.6 round 4 / `LES-1`) — `§19.x` **AND `§2.5`** are real anchors; every OTHER `§N.x` is shorthand.** ⚠ **The prior wording — *"real numbered subsections ONLY under §19 … by construction NOT an architecture anchor"* — was FALSE, and it was LOAD-BEARING: `ARCHITECTURE.md:80` is `## §2.5 — Subsystem dependency DAG & parallelization seams`, corroborated three ways (Phase 9's declared `Spec anchors:` line · this file's own *"the §2.5 import-direction root"* · `docs/tdd-brief-template.md`'s seam bullet).** ⛔ **Applying the old rule would have STRIPPED the one cross-cutting seam anchor the brief template mandates.** ⭐ **The correction landed in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (`25acd598`, 2026-07-30) and NEVER PROPAGATED HERE — and a brief dated one day later cited the falsified universal as its justification.** *(contracts L94: a correction that reaches the channel STATING a claim and not the channel REPEATING it leaves the repeating channel authoritative.)* Architecture ⇒ bare `§N`; plan task ⇒ `task N.M`; another doc ⇒ path + its own numbering. A dangling citation gets investigated; an **ambiguous** one gets believed. `pattern: grep -nE '§(1[0-8]|[0-9])\.[0-9]' IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — hits are CANDIDATES to classify (87 at L101's writing), not defects.
9. **Retain a struck/superseded block with its STATUS or NEXT-ACTION lines intact** — striking a block does NOT tense-shift the sentences inside it (L195). ⛔ **`### 24.84` retained a voided owner ruling ending `STATUS: RE-PUT TO THE OWNER, UNDECIDED`; an implementer read it as live and nearly reopened a settled cat-4 decision** — the live `FINAL OWNER RULING` sat ~4,800 chars ABOVE it, so the dead status line was the LAST and most recent-looking state in the entry. **Keep the REASONING (that is why we retain); strip or past-tense the STATE.** Narrative survives striking; a status claim does not. `pattern: grep -nE '^>?[^A-Za-z0-9]*(STATUS|NEXT|PENDING|UNDECIDED|IN FLIGHT|AWAITING)' IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — hits inside a struck/retained block are defects; elsewhere they are CANDIDATES to classify.
<!-- ▲ END EXAMPLE BLOCK [id=forbidden-patterns] ▲ -->

## Cross-doc invariants — schema/docs mirroring

Several typed models in this codebase are **contracts** mirrored in `ARCHITECTURE.md` and indexed in the table below. The architecture doc is the canonical contract; the model is the executable enforcement. Drift produces silent disagreement.

**Authoring discipline (orchestrator owns this table).** The implementer never edits this table or `ARCHITECTURE.md` directly — it flags a field add/remove/rename at Step 9 as a `Cross-doc invariant change`; the orchestrator writes the row + the arch edit hot the same round (see root `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/orchestrator-briefing.md`). Commits stagger; the working tree stays aligned within the round.

| Model | `ARCHITECTURE.md` section | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| EgressPolicy | §3, §5 | workspaceId, allowedProcessors[], rawContentAllowedProcessors[], employerRawEgressAcknowledged, acknowledgedAt? — refine: acknowledgedAt ⇔ acknowledged. |
| ToolPolicy | §3, §5, §7 | mode(read_only\|scoped_write), allowedTools[], deniedTools[], allowsMutating — read_only ⇒ !allowsMutating; deniedTools-precedence helper. |
| Capability / ProviderRoute | §3, §7 | Capability = open branded id (zod-brands). ProviderRoute = union {runtime}⊕{provider} + model, endpoint, egressClass. |
| ProviderProfile | §3, §4, §7 | provider, endpoint, model, capabilities[], egressClass, costCaps, conformanceStatus — NO inline-secret field (REQ-S-003). |
| ProviderMatrix | §3, §5, §7 | workspaceId, allowedProviders[], capabilityDefaults: Record<Capability,ProviderRoute>, rawCloudEgressEnabled, localProviderPreference? — provider-routes ⊆ allowedProviders. |
| ProviderId (enum) | §7, §5 | **+`perplexity` +`xai` (RES-1, 13.13, 2026-07-25)** — egress-cloud research processors, each its OWN processor (never an OpenAI/alias). Frozen-contract round: the enum expansion regenerated **5** embedding schemas (provider-route + provider-profile + provider-matrix + agent-job + workspace) + snapshots + membership pins. NOT a §5-veto edit — the rule-5 veto is ProviderId-AGNOSTIC (`LOCAL_PROVIDERS` opt-in allowlist; a new provider absent from it is egress-classed fail-closed BY CONSTRUCTION). L25. |
| Workspace | §3, §6 | id, name, type, dataOwner, markdownRepoPath, gbrainBrainId, defaultVisibility, egressPolicy, providerMatrix — id≡egressPolicy.workspaceId≡providerMatrix.workspaceId; defaultWorkspace() safe-default factory. |
| AgentJob | §3, §7, §9 | +trustLevel, +carriesRawContent; COST-1 budget pins; embeds ToolPolicy+ProviderRoute; isRegisteredOutputSchema() registry predicate. |
| AgentExtractionCandidate | §7, §9, §19.5 | NEW (18.11/CP-1 GATE-1): { fields: Record<name,{value: string\|number(finite)\|boolean\|"TBD", evidenceRef?}> }; `sow:agent-extraction` schema — strict outer+inner + `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype` propertyNames blocklist, value `anyOf` (excludes null; finite number), empty `{fields:{}}` structurally valid. Evidence-preserving so `validateNoInference` sees the model's real `evidenceRef` (anti-KMP-stand-in, REQ-F-017). TS type `AgentExtractionCandidate` (avoids @sow/workflows `AgentExtraction` collision); schema id `sow:agent-extraction`. Union member + producer/consumer legs = CP-2/CP-3; charset + maxProperties/maxLength bounds = Future-TODO (§9 catalog). ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-07-31 (13.8g-C leg A, `5eaf33f5`) — a SCOPED, DECLARED, DEFAULT-CLOSED RELAXATION of this rule-2 gate, NOT a reclassification:** exactly the fields in `LIST_VALUED_EXTRACTION_FIELDS` (`attendees`, `decisions`) may additionally carry a `string[]` capped at 200, elements scalar-validated, **nesting rejected**; scalar/`TBD` stay legal everywhere and **every undeclared field keeps today's scalar-only rejection.** ⭐ Default-closed falls out of JSON Schema's own precedence (`properties` for declared keys over `additionalProperties` for the rest), which answers *"can a candidate declare its own list-ness?"* **BY CONSTRUCTION** — a payload cannot add itself to `properties`. ⚠ The container swap drops **three** guards (ajv `propertyNames`; Zod's own key check; and Zod SILENTLY DROPPING a `__proto__` own-key during reconstruction) — all restored via a `z.preprocess` key guard + a `guardCatchallPropertyNames` policy in `emit.ts`; `propertyNames` **verified by READING the regenerated JSON**, and the three reserved-key assertions confirmed **byte-unchanged**. |

(§13.10a gate 1 — verification-only raw projectId; executor rejects a NotePatch whose target frontmatter projectId ≠ this). REQ-F-006 reject-on-empty sourceRefs. |

> **All 27 frozen 2026-06-30 (Phase 1, tasks 1.2–1.9); +Project (§13.5, 2026-07-06) = 28; +Task (§13.15, ARC-2 2026-07-25) = 29.** Authoring = ADR-008 Zod-as-source: each model is `XSchema` (`.strict()`) → `z.infer` type → generated `schemas/<kebab>.schema.json` → frozen `__snapshots__/<kebab>.snap` (top-level field-name set) → registered in the ajv-strict registry. **A field add/remove/rename requires editing `ARCHITECTURE.md` Appendix A + the model's schema + its `.snap` in the same round** — `registry-all.test.ts` + the per-model snapshot test fail otherwise. Shared sub-shapes (`ContextRef`/`SourceRef`/`NoteCreate`/`NotePatch`/`LinkMutation`/`FrontmatterPatch`/`CanonicalSourceRef`) live in `src/models/shared-shapes.ts`; shared enums in `src/models/shared-enums.ts`; brands in `src/primitives/zod-brands.ts` — never re-declare them inline.

> ⭐ **UI-SAFE PROJECTIONS ARE INDEXED IN `ARCHITECTURE.md` APPENDIX A BUT ARE DELIBERATELY *NOT* ROWS IN THE TABLE ABOVE — and the distinction is load-bearing, not bookkeeping.** The table above is the **29 frozen seams**: Zod-as-source → generated `schemas/<kebab>.schema.json` → frozen `.snap` → ajv-strict registry. A **UI-safe projection** (`src/api/ui-safe.ts`) has **none of those** — no generated schema, no snapshot pin, no registry entry — so listing one here would assert a freeze that does not exist and would put the "29 frozen" count in doubt. **Current projections, both carrying Appendix-A rows:** `UiSafeCopilotAnswer` (§9.6 — the Copilot sidebar's candidate-gate / leakage contract) and `UiSafeAuditDrillSummary` (§9.41 leg A — the audit drill-down; `auditRef` never leaves the worker). ⛔ **They are still ORCHESTRATOR-WRITE territory and still cross-doc invariants** — a field change needs its Appendix-A row edited in the same round, and an implementer flags it at Step 9 rather than editing it. ⚠ **This note exists because the gap it closes was found the hard way:** `UiSafeAuditDrillSummary` shipped with consumers on **both** the worker (`api/procedures/queries.ts`) and desktop (`renderer/lib/audit-drill.ts`) legs while having **no** row in either doc, and the only mechanism that surfaced it was an implementer noticing an absence in territory it is forbidden to edit.

## Module organization

<!-- ▼ EXAMPLE BLOCK [id=module-layout]: module layout + layer dependency rule. Replace with the project's real directory tree and import-direction DAG. ▼ -->
```
packages/contracts/   runtime-safe types · JSON Schemas · tRPC router types · event catalog · model snapshots
packages/domain/      pure rules · the 6 state machines · validators (schema gate, no-inference) · canonical-key/idempotency builders
```

Layer dependency direction (top depends on bottom, never reverse):

```
(everything downstream) → packages/domain → packages/contracts
packages/contracts + packages/domain import NOTHING app- or adapter-side (pure root of the DAG)
```

## Lessons logged from prior sessions

The full prose for each lesson lives in `packages/contracts/LESSONS.md`. This index is the compact orientation surface.

**Lesson numbers are stable IDs** — once assigned, they don't change. New lessons get the next sequential number. `/session-end` proposes additions when it detects them; the user approves before the entry is written and a row is added here.

Lessons start at §1.

| # | Date | Topic | Rule (one-liner) |
|--:|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2026-06-30 | Branded `z.infer` + `declaration:true` → TS4023 | A model whose Zod schema embeds a branded ID must export an explicit `interface` + annotate the schema `z.ZodType<Out, ZodTypeDef, In>` — never rely on bare `z.infer` for the exported type. |
| 2 | 2026-06-30 | Zod-as-source contract recipe (ADR-008) | Each Appendix-A model ships 4 files (`.ts`/`schemas/*.schema.json`/`__snapshots__/*.snap`/test); JSON Schema is generated (never hand-written); import shared brands/enums/sub-shapes — never re-declare them inline. |
| 3 | 2026-06-30 | ajv `validate()` is structural-only — gate is a composition | `zod-to-json-schema` drops `.refine`; the candidate-data gate = ajv `validate()` + the model's Zod parse + the §3 universal rules + the §5/§6/§7 predicates — never ajv alone. |
| 4 | 2026-07-01 | Isolate the URL authority before extracting host in a security predicate | A loopback/SSRF/allowlist gate parsing an untrusted endpoint must strip path/query/fragment + backslash to isolate the authority BEFORE stripping userinfo/host — userinfo-first is spoofable (`http://evil.com/@127.0.0.1` → fake loopback → Phase-3 egress-veto bypass). Green unit tests ≠ safe; gate it with an adversarial-verify pass + regression tests. |
| 5 | 2026-07-02 | Redact secrets/raw-content by per-field TYPE, never a length/shape heuristic | A mandatory redactor cannot decide "is this raw?" from a value's own shape — a short whitespace-free raw word (codename, OTP, opaque token) is indistinguishable from a safe token. Emit a string only if it's a frozen-enum member / id-under-id-field / number / ISO timestamp; fail-safe redact everything else. Run dedicated field validators BEFORE any generic name-suffix rule (an `Id`-suffix shadowed the `providerId` enum case). Document + pin the accepted residual; re-verify each fix until a skeptic can't leak (took 3 rounds). |

| 61 | 2026-07-26 | When a finding names one call site, grep for the CONSTRUCTION — the second copy is the one that ships | The 13.8g-A structural-surface finding was reported at `meeting-rewrite.ts:185`; the identical `${proposedSlug}.md` construction also sat at `planner.ts:288` on the SOURCE path — which was already bound dormant in production, making the UNREPORTED copy the more exposed one. | 62 | 2026-07-26 | A "makes no unsafe claim" pin must be DIRECTION-AGNOSTIC and subtree-scoped — ban the TOPIC in the surface that cannot know it, never blocklist the wrong phrasing | #10's approved pin was a 3-phrase blocklist; the reviewer ran 25 plausible restorations and **20 slipped** ("Local only" with a space, U+2011 hyphen, "Nothing leaves this Mac") — and `Egress: cloud-allowed`/`Egress: none` passed GREEN despite being the identical defect (a chrome element asserting a posture it cannot know). | 64 | 2026-07-26 | Any search for INSTANCES of a defect must enumerate the CONCEPT, never the known string forms — the known forms are by definition the ones already found. Loop until dry. | The general form of L62 (which was written too narrowly as a test-pin rule). Same failure in every medium: a 3-phrase pin let 20/25 restorations through incl. the OPPOSITE-direction claim; a literal-string doc sweep found 4 of 9 sites and a CONCEPT sweep (shield·pill·ambient·always-visible) found the rest in THREE iterations before coming back dry; and the most dangerous site surfaced LAST — a normative principle ("always-on governance signals stay first-class visible") that would have re-authorized the removed element on its own authority, feeling like good governance while doing it. Structural reason: the string forms you know are precisely the ones you already found, so searching them can only re-find what you have. Search by CONCEPT (what must not be true / what topic this surface may not address), cover opposite-direction phrasings + alternate unicode + diagrams + NORMATIVE re-authorizations, and stop on a pass that ADDS NOTHING — never report an all-clear from one string-shaped pass (a confident wrong all-clear closes the question, worse than no sweep). Applies to pins, greps, code review, security audits, doc reconciliation. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` (mitigation: state the search KEY + dry-iteration count in the report). [full](LESSONS.md#64) ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-08-18 — FOURTH MEDIUM, STRICTLY STRONGER: in a CODE census the instance may have NO string form at all (`id: workspace.type` contains none of the type literals), so better vocabulary cannot reach it and "a pass that adds nothing" terminates CORRECTLY on a false all-clear. ⭐ CHANGE MEDIUM, NOT VOCABULARY — string search → reference/type graph; state which medium produced the answer. ⚠ Spend census effort in proportion to how SILENT the failure is: a loud defect announces itself, a silent one is only ever found by a deliberate look.** |
| 65 | 2026-07-26 | A fail-safe DEFAULT that resolves through a prototype chain is not a fail-safe — use a `ReadonlyMap` whenever the key is untrusted | 13.8j: `LOOKUP[key] ?? FALLBACK` over an OBJECT LITERAL looks total with a safe default but isn't — literals inherit `Object.prototype`, so `__proto__`/`toString`/`constructor`/`valueOf`/`hasOwnProperty` return NON-undefined, `??` never fires, and the caller gets a function/object where a namespace was expected (here: `"[object Object]index.md"` — paths back at the vault ROOT, the exact collision the slice existed to prevent, reintroduced by the mechanism meant to prevent it). Worse than an ordinary bug: it silently converts complete-by-construction into "complete except ~9 magic strings" (the denylist posture the design rejected), and a reviewer checking only "is the default applied?" passes it. Use `ReadonlyMap` over `Object.create(null)` (survives a refactor back to a literal + `Object.prototype` pollution); type the param `K | undefined` so the fallback is reachable BY THE TYPE SYSTEM, not via a cast; pin with HOSTILE keys, not just absent. Distinct from L41 (lookup COMPLETENESS) — this is the DEFAULT being defeated. Pin: `knowledge synthesis-entity-resolver.test.ts`. [full](LESSONS.md#65) |
| 66 | 2026-07-26 | When an absence is safe only because of an invariant enforced ELSEWHERE, the pin must say so — it encodes a conclusion, not a law | Second half of L56, about the TEST rather than the code. 9.24 concluded a missing Copilot egress notice is safe — but that rests on four legs living elsewhere (`.optional()` not `.catch()` so it can't be stripped · failed ask renders explicit failure · both synthesis adapters fail closed on a non-Claude route pre-egress · `buildCopilotDeps` pairs adapter+routeSelector under one ternary). A consumer-side pin asserting "absent ⇒ silent" therefore encodes a conclusion whose PREMISES IT CANNOT SEE: if any upstream leg changes, the pin still passes and the conclusion is quietly false. So name the dependent invariants in the pin, and record the durable WHY where the legs live — parking it in a desktop test file makes it invisible to the worker track that owns them. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. | 67 | 2026-07-26 | A test whose NAME asserts more than its body can observe is worse than no test | 9.24: `non_employer_cloud_egress_behavior_unchanged` could not see workspace type AT ALL — the renderer keys the notice solely on field presence, so the scope in the fixture was decorative and the test was mechanically identical to the existing absent-notice case. Worse than absent: a reader or audit counts it as protection, which suppresses writing the real check. Fix was to RE-AIM, not delete — `notice_is_scope_blind_at_the_renderer` pins what the layer CAN observe and fails precisely if someone "fixes" case 2 with renderer-side suppression (an owner-decision reversal AND a rule-5 decision migrating out of the worker). | 68 | 2026-07-26 | Verification that flows only DOWNWARD leaves a blind spot exactly the size of whoever sits at the top of it | All round the orchestrator read source to check implementers' rule-5 claims and it kept catching real defects — but nothing checked the ORCHESTRATOR's output. Evidence: an orch-authored finding claimed commit messages reached git degraded and framed the durable audit trail as at risk; it sat in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` as fact until the lead READ THE STORED BYTES (3740, intact). An overstated finding in a durable file is the same defect class as an overstated safety claim — it asserts more than the evidence supports and someone later acts on it. Generalizes: in any review hierarchy the top node's claims are checked by nobody inside the system (the lead named their own instance — the owner is the only check on their reports). Mitigation is structural, not diligence: state claims with RE-RUNNABLE evidence attached (`file:line`, counts, command output) and spot-check UPWARD by reading the artifact rather than the report. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. | 69 | 2026-07-26 | A TESTED false assurance is an active defense of the defect — it inverts the review gate from a check into a lock | 9.22: `egressCommands.test.ts:83` and `:178` both asserted `zeroEgressOnly === true` after a revoke — false whenever the ws allowed a cloud processor. Worse in KIND than an untested gap: the suite made the CORRECT fix look like a regression, so the next person backs out **with a failing test's confidence**, turning the assertion from a check on the code into a LOCK on it. The reflex to make red green is strong and mostly right, which is why stopping to ask whether the TEST was right must be trained. Corollaries: (a) after fixing a construction, re-sweep TESTS — one report yielded four artifacts (worker found 1 pinning assertion, lead found the 2nd full-object `toEqual` that survived the first fix, an orch sweep found 2 stale FAKES modelling a now-impossible state); (b) **an audit reading only production code cannot find this class** — it lives in assertions/fixtures (24.6 scope constraint 5). Prefer independence/invariant assertions over value assertions; flag test-semantics changes on safety pins explicitly. Pin: `worker egressCommands.test.ts`. [full](LESSONS.md#69) |

| 71 | 2026-07-27 | [A durable claim must carry the conditions under which it was true — and a correction is a claim too](LESSONS.md#71) | Five retractions in one round, two distinct mechanisms — and the tempting one-liner ("diagnose by RUNNING, not inferring") is WRONG because it misdescribes the costliest instance. **(a) INFERENCE where execution was available:** a remembered crossing recorded as "landed" when one grep showed the symbol had zero hits repo-wide (it lived only on an unmerged branch); a mechanism asserted about someone else's error ("they never ran the suite") that was itself never checked. **(b) A REAL execution attributed to the WRONG STATE** — the costly one (a)'s framing hides: a measurement taken in a mutated tree, the edit reverted, the result recorded as a property of committed HEAD; the follow-up `git status`/`git log` both PASSED and were both TRUE — of a different tree-state. Two correct observations fused into one false composite; checking their work the obvious way confirmed both halves. ⭐ **Rule: when a diagnosis and its git verification are separated by a REVERT, the verification does not cover the diagnosis** — re-run clean, or record the tree state beside the observation. **Propagation half (corollary to L68):** a credible upstream source is not evidence; an implementer's hypothesis became durable via an unverifying orchestrator, and a lead began banking a lesson off it — broken only when the implementer re-tested and retracted their OWN hypothesis against two people above them. **Fourth instance — a correction is a claim:** a half-applied fix left "ONE issue, not two" in the head and "TWO ISSUES ARE STACKED HERE" in the tail (worse than uncorrected — authoritative in both directions); a second over-swung to "none of that is true" when the observation WAS true, of another tree. Re-read the whole entry after correcting; say precisely which part was wrong — observation, attribution, or scope. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` (enforcement points: `/orchestrate-end` Carry-forward triage + `/team-end`). | 75 | 2026-07-27 | [Prove a new guard by SIMULATING the compromise it claims to catch](LESSONS.md#75) | The L74 guard had ALREADY passed review as "load-bearing" when its author found it bypassable — what settled it was running the attack, not more review. Simulation: compromise the planner to genuinely mint at the vault root, launder the corpus to agree, re-stamp the hash. Result: **all 10 pre-existing tests PASS** (incl. `safety_floor_100pct`, `faithfulness_no_fabrication`), only the 2 new KN-12 guards fail ⇒ a root-collision regression laundered through the corpus **would have shipped fully green**. That is a measurement; "load-bearing" had been an opinion that already survived one review while being false. **A negative guard that has never failed is indistinguishable from one that cannot fail** — make it fail on purpose against a genuine compromise, then revert, and report what stayed GREEN (the passing set is the finding). Generalizes L62's throwaway probe to any safety guard. **Byproduct (knowledge/13.8j):** deleting the `concept` map entry still emitted `entities/widgets.md` — a root mint required ALSO blanking `FALLBACK_NAMESPACE`, so that module's "⚠ LOAD-BEARING — do not tighten into a rejection" comment is now demonstrated, not asserted. ⛔ **PRECONDITION (added 2026-07-28, same author, next round; wording is theirs):** a simulation is evidence ONLY if you **prove the compromise was REACHED — by seeing the guard FAIL before you trust it passing**. TWO distinct ways to get a false green, and only the fail-first check covers both: (i) the compromise never reached the code under test (patched `@sow/providers` where the test imports `@sow/domain`); (ii) it reached the right function but **no test fed it the triggering input** (oracle fixed, corpus hand-picked 3 values, none the PEM). ⛔ **AND CHECK THE COUNT — a green suite is not evidence a file RAN:** literal control chars made a test file a SYNTAX ERROR, which reports **`PASS (0) FAIL (0)`** — every normal signal said fine; caught only by the count dropping **466→334**. **A file that did not run and a file whose tests all passed are indistinguishable from the pass/fail line alone**, so state 1 (compromise applied, guard passes) is indistinguishable from "the file did not execute." Record the COUNT with every simulation state. Run the **three-state protocol**: compromise+old guard PASSES (blindness reproduced) → compromise+new guard FAILS (it discriminates) → clean+new guard PASSES (no false positive). **State 2 is what a fix without simulation skips** — it caught a stacked second defect the other two missed — their first attempt patched `@sow/providers` while the test imports `@sow/domain`, yielding a confident "24/24 passed" that measured an untouched path (L71 wearing this technique's clothes). Confirm the mutation was OBSERVED: break something the suite demonstrably DOES catch, watch it go red, then apply the real mutation — a compromise that changes nothing is indistinguishable from one never applied. ⭐ **AMENDED 2026-07-29 — the trap was hit again INSIDE this technique, and the new half is WHY: a mutation target can look live and be DEAD because a downstream consumer RE-HARDCODES the value.** `budget-enforcer.ts` computes `branch` on its `GateDeny`; `broker.ts:377` **discards it and re-hardcodes its own `"cancelled_budget"` literal** — so mutating the producer's field changed nothing observable, though the import path, module and function were all correct. A distinct sub-case of (i): **the field itself was unreachable.** ⇒ **confirm the mutated VALUE is actually CONSUMED, not merely that the file/function is on the path** — two independent literals that happen to agree read exactly like one shared literal until you mutate one (and that duplication is itself a live drift risk). **Process half, standing alone:** the reported result (7/1) was stated BEFORE the command ran; the real run was 8/0 — inside the lesson that exists to prevent that. It cost nothing only because the author corrected it within minutes, unprompted, against their own prior message; **self-correction at that speed is what keeps a verified-findings list worth reading.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — mitigation: state the simulation, the import path it reached, **that the mutated value is consumed downstream**, and the passing set; never state a result you have not run. | 79 | 2026-07-27 | [Adding a conjunct silently retires the discrimination of every existing test built on the state it now rejects](LESSONS.md#79) | 9.22's third conjunct (matrix must be NON-EMPTY) was one line and the suite stayed GREEN — **and green was the wrong signal.** All seven single-fault fixtures (`["claude"]`, `rawCloudEgressEnabled:true`, sparse/hole, proxy, malformed-field) had been built on an EMPTY matrix, the minimal fixture under the old arity — so they all still passed **on the new unconfigured conjunct, not on the condition each was named for.** Two were worse: the totality pins **short-circuited at the new check and never reached the route scan they exist to exercise** — a safety pin that no longer executes the code it guards is absent while reporting present (L69's hazard by accident). **Widening a precondition narrows the input space the existing tests explore.** Check on every conjunct addition: *does this fixture still REACH the condition its name claims?* Mechanically, re-base each case onto a fixture satisfying the new conjunct (a `localMatrix()` builder) so it fails for its own reason again. ⭐ **SHARPENED: re-basing correctly is NOT sufficient** — two pins already mutation-verified in the base slice still silently re-pointed at the new conjunct (an under-reported-`length` proxy tripped the new check instead of its own guard; non-enumerable/symbol fixtures cleared the conjunct only because it enumerated keys the same way). | 80 | 2026-07-28 | [A suite must assert that a gate DECIDES, not that a gate SAID NO](LESSONS.md#80) | ⭐ The round's strongest line, verbatim: **these suites assert that a gate SAID NO, not that the gate DECIDES.** Two guards proven blind by simulation, each leaving ALL 610 tests GREEN under compromise: `redactRecord` emitting a **PEM private key verbatim** (rule 7), and `validateProjectionVisibility` replaced by a **constant DENY** (rule 4/WS-8). **A guard with no allow-side control and no reason-code pin cannot distinguish a working gate from a brick wall** — every deny-side assertion still passes when the gate stopped deciding, because "it said no" is what a brick wall says. **Two requirements:** (1) an allow-side control differing from the deny fixture in EXACTLY the field the gate decides on (the WS-8 sibling leg already had one — same file, one leg protected, one not; the asymmetry WAS the defect); (2) a reason-CODE pin, not a boolean (`MALFORMED_POLICY_INPUT` and a real refusal are the same `isDeny`). | 81 | 2026-07-28 | [An implementer who contradicts a brief after reading the code is functioning correctly](LESSONS.md#81) | ⭐ **STANDING EXPECTATION, not an anecdote** (lead's instruction). **FIVE** orchestrator framings corrected in one round, every one caught by **DOING THE WORK**, **none** by review — not by the author, the endorsing lead, or a reviewer subagent. | 82 | 2026-07-28 | [On finding a false assurance: make the claim true, or retract it — never split the difference](LESSONS.md#82) | Two false coverage claims, **opposite dispositions, one rule**: `WORKSPACE_LEAKAGE dodPass=true` was one pin from true ⇒ **make it TRUE**; four dead DoD pointers (incl. safety-classed KNOWLEDGE_WRITE / WORKSPACE_ROUTING / TOOL_GATEWAY_IDEMPOTENCY) would need three real suites ⇒ **mark UNCOVERED + task the suites**. ⛔ **THE FORBIDDEN MIDDLE — re-point the claim at whatever evidence is handy** — is attractive precisely because it is cheap, looks like a fix, and turns the build green; it **manufactures the appearance of coverage, committing the defect while repairing it**. "Nearby" is not coverage; **proximity is not evidence** — re-point ONLY with proof the suite covers the criterion's ACTUAL claim. **Why uncovered beats falsely-covered: an uncovered criterion is VISIBLE and invites work; a false-green one CLOSES THE QUESTION** — nobody audits what reports covered, so the gap becomes permanent AND invisible. L56's **disposition half** (L56: derive, never assert; L82: what to do once you find one asserted). Three options, two legitimate: supply the evidence (cheap+bounded) · withdraw + record what would restore it (evidence is real work) · ⛔ re-aim at handy evidence (never). ⚠ **The pull toward the forbidden option scales with deadline pressure and with how safety-classed the criterion is — exactly inverted from where it is safe.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` (mitigation: an `existsSync`-class tripwire making a dangling claim RED, not invisible). | 83 | 2026-07-28 | [In a shared working tree the INDEX is shared state — per-file `git add` is not isolation](LESSONS.md#83) | Hit from BOTH sides in one round, neither at fault. Desktop staging its own 4 files found **3 of evalsec's already staged** — a plain `git commit` would have swept another track's work into a **rule-5 commit** (the `225c10ca` mixed-commit class). evalsec staging only its session doc had that doc **swept into the worker's commit**. **`git add <my-file>` isolates what you PUT IN the index; it does not isolate the index** — anyone's broad add/commit between your add and your commit carries your file with it. **Defenses:** (1) **pathspec-limited commit** `git commit -- <paths>` makes capturing another's work STRUCTURALLY impossible, not a matter of noticing — ⚠ side effect: it resets the index for uncommitted paths, so a teammate's staged files silently unstage (content safe — TELL THEM); (2) stage immediately before committing and verify `git diff --cached --name-only` **at commit time**, not add time — the window between is the exposure; (3) **do NOT rewrite history to fix a mis-attribution** — a rebase in a shared tree with live agents is far more destructive than a mis-attributed file; record it in the seal. Same root as L75's mutation window: **a shared tree has more shared state than the files — index and working tree are both global, and every agent's CORRECT workflow perturbs them.** Structural, not discipline. ⭐ **THE PROCEDURE — per-file `add` is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT** (it guards neither files already in the index nor the index changing between add and commit): **(1) BEFORE** `git diff --cached --name-only` verify the exact set · **(2) DURING** chain `add && commit` in ONE invocation so nothing lands between them · **(3) AFTER** `git show --stat` catch what still got through. **Step 3 is the one that matters** — in one occurrence a commit had ALREADY carried another area's session doc and was caught only afterwards (corrected with `reset --soft`, which is recovery, not history rewriting, on an unshared commit). Steps 1–2 reduce the odds; **step 3 tells you they failed.** ⭐ **AMENDED 2026-07-29 — the working tree is a moving target for READERS too, not just writers**, and that produced three lead-level framing errors in one day. Sharpest: a reviewer checking whether 9.22 had touched all three sites read `boot.ts` AFTER the implementer's edit (their quoted `:543`/`:588` reflect the edited file) and `egressRevoke.ts` BEFORE it, then reported site 3 untouched and asked for rework — it was already derived correctly. **The proof was self-contained: they quoted a comment as evidence of absence, and that comment had been DELETED by the very edit they were looking for.** Worse than an ordinary stale read because it arrives as a *finding* with file:line evidence, addressed to whoever can act — so it is maximally likely to be relayed, and would have sent a correct implementation back mid-slice with a reviewer's authority behind it. ⇒ **Mitigation, one line: while an implementer is mid-slice, review the COMMITTED diff, not the live tree** (`git show`/`git diff base..head` are immutable; `git status` and a file read are not). If you must read live, take every observation in ONE invocation so they share a snapshot, and say which snapshot — same discipline as the L75 mutation window. **Corollary for the relaying orchestrator: verify a review finding yourself BEFORE forwarding it**, exactly as you would a brief premise (L81) — three times in one day the thing that prevented rework was checking first, and checking costs one `git diff`. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. [full](LESSONS.md#83) |
| 84 | 2026-07-28 | [A green suite under a mutated guard has TWO readings — unasserted, or unreached — and they demand different fixes](LESSONS.md#84) | E1/#40: the prior round wrote *"delete that branch in production and the eval stays green"* into the seal **as fact**, then corrected itself — three of four claims were read off the source, the fourth was not, all stated at one strength. Executed: terminal branch removed from `operational-truth.ts:254-267`, §12 approval-exactly-once suite (**a phase-exit-8 DoD criterion**) stayed **3/3 green**, branch restored, `packages/db` clean. **The claim was true; its obvious reading was wrong.** The tempting conclusion is *"the eval fails to ASSERT the guard"*; the finding is stronger and different — **the eval's call graph never REACHES that code at all.** ⭐ Not academic, because it changes the fix: **unasserted** ⇒ add the assertion, closed; **unreached** ⇒ adding an assertion to the stand-in achieves **nothing about the real code** (correct work — it removes drift — but it must not be recorded as closing the coverage gap). That is why #40 shipped **and #44 stayed open**. **Opposite corner from [L75](#75)**, which governs false greens in the *simulation* (compromise never landed / no test fed it the trigger; remedy = fail-first): here the compromise genuinely landed and the green is genuine — L75 says the measurement is real, L84 says a real measurement still has two readings. **Sharper than [L80](#80)'s** *"replace the gate with a constant DENY — anything red?"*: this is *"delete the gate — anything red, and if not, WHICH reason?"* L80 catches a gate that stopped deciding; this catches a suite that was never watching. **Do:** determine the reading BEFORE writing the fix (cheapest discriminator — do the suite's imports reach the mutated module at all?), state it in the report, and name the test that WOULD have gone red. `pin: packages/db/test/invariants/operational-truth.test.ts` (30/30 — the only real pin on `decideApprovalCas`; load-bearing) · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. | 85 | 2026-07-28 | [A fake that mirrors a real guard covers the fake — mirroring buys discrimination, never coverage](LESSONS.md#85) | #40's residual → #44. The fix made the eval's fake CAS enforce the terminal-state rule importing the **real** `isTerminalApprovalStatus` from `@sow/db` rather than re-declaring the set, so the two cannot drift — right, and better than a duplicated literal ([L39](#39) applied to a double). **And it does not make the suite cover `decideApprovalCas`**: a future regression in the real function stays invisible, because the suite still executes the double. ⭐ **Mirroring a real guard's semantics buys discrimination for the double's own logic; it buys nothing about the code the double stands in for.** **Why it's easy to miss, and worse than the original defect:** "the fake was missing the guard; we added the guard" reads as a closed loop — both halves true, suite green, finding ticked — but the fake merely stopped being *wrong*; nothing changed about what the suite *watches*, so the remaining exposure is now HARDER to see than before the fix ([L82](#82)'s hazard arriving through a legitimate repair rather than a re-pointed claim). **Corollary — know which test is load-bearing:** once a criterion is nominally covered by a suite running a double, the real assurance lives elsewhere, and nothing in the suite's name or output says so. Name it, in the suite and in the plan. **Do:** when a double is corrected to match production, state what the suite covers AFTER the change (usually: unchanged) and where the real code is actually pinned; where a real adapter exists, prefer driving it — a double is for what you cannot reach, not what is inconvenient to wire. `pin: packages/db/test/invariants/operational-truth.test.ts` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` (enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 9 — a "fixed the fake" flag must state post-fix coverage, not only drift closure). | 86 | 2026-07-29 | [A refusal channel is only real once a refused run is distinguishable from an empty one on EVERY exit path — including the ones that reject afterwards](LESSONS.md#86) | 13.8m-A/B: the point of a refusal channel is that "we refused something hostile" stops being byte-identical to "there was nothing to do" — and that is a property of **every** exit, not the happy one. Producer hoists its `refusals` accumulator **above the `try`** (`ingest-rewrite.ts:112-115`) and carries it on the fail-safe `empty()` receipt (`:116`): *a fault AFTER admission must not discard what was already refused, or a hostile run that hijacks paths then trips a throwing port becomes byte-identical to a benign empty one.* Consumer had the same shape a layer up — `createLivingVaultPort` can reject AFTER reading the receipt (`path_escape`, WS-8 mismatch, unresolvable root), so the audit fires **once, immediately after the receipt, BEFORE root resolution and containment.** ⭐ **Ask: enumerate every `return` between the refusal and the caller, and check each still carries it** — a channel surviving only the success path documents the case nobody attacks. Constraints: **code-only** (closed 2-member union; a channel carrying the refused PATH becomes the exfiltration route it exists to report, rule 7 — a count is fine); **best-effort at the SINK, never at the accumulator** (a throwing sink must not alter the `Result` nor escape, L25; accumulation is not best-effort); **a benign run must invoke the sink ZERO times** (firing always destroys the distinction from the other direction, and is the easier mistake). Known bound recorded in-code, not hidden: `refusals` OPTIONAL on the seam (13 pre-existing fakes stayed valid, L11) ⇒ a future adapter could omit it and the sink silently never fires; acceptable only because the single production producer always forwards, pinned. ⚠ The plan claimed it landed on BOTH receipts; it landed on ONE — briefing that would have sent an implementer after a nonexistent field and left the meeting path byte-identical between poisoned and empty, on an `§ARM-RESEARCH` precondition. `pin: living-vault-refusal-audit.test.ts (refused_then_containment_rejected_still_surfaces + benign_empty_run_invokes_no_sink)`. | 87 | 2026-07-29 | [Close a structurally-satisfiable safety type with a NOMINAL brand — and give the genuine candidate boundary its own UNBRANDED type](LESSONS.md#87) | 9.34: `CopilotTurnView.reply`/`CopilotAnswerView`'s prop were `UiSafeCopilotAnswer`, and TS is **structural** — any right-shaped object satisfies it, so with the rule-5 disclosure field `egressProcessor` being `.optional()`, a hand-built literal omitting it compiled and **silently dropped the disclosure**. The type asserted "validated" while accepting anything shaped like it. Fix: a **nominal brand** (`AdmittedCopilotAnswer = UiSafeCopilotAnswer & {readonly [uniqueSymbol]: true}`) mintable only by `admitReply()` (internal cast after `.safeParse`), required at both chokepoints ⇒ `reply:` uninhabitable by a literal. ⭐ **The half that makes it honest and is easy to skip: a SEPARATE unbranded `CopilotTurnSeed` for the mount-time seed prop** — that boundary genuinely receives candidate data, so branding it (or casting into the brand there) would make the brand assert something false exactly where it matters; admit INTERNALLY at the door that validates. **A brand is a claim about provenance: if you are casting into it at a boundary that has not validated, that boundary needs its own unbranded type, not a cast.** Verification: **RED for a type-level pin is a `@ts-expect-error` going UNUSED** (both compiled pre-brand ⇒ unused directive ⇒ typecheck RED — a real red-green on a purely static change); both reviewers independently mutation-tested by aliasing the brand back to the bare type (L75 applied where the instinct to skip simulation is strongest — "it's just types"); **no contract change** (renderer-local nominal type over the existing model, not a frozen round). `pin: copilot-panel.test.tsx`. | 88 | 2026-07-29 | [Single-sourcing is for literals that must AGREE — two caps bounding different THREAT MODELS must stay independent](LESSONS.md#88) | 13.8h: the task's own `Done-when` said *"keep the cap a single shared constant so a caller-side and planner-side cap can't drift"* — **overridden deliberately.** `MAX_ENTITY_REFS` bounds **deterministic input the meeting path owns**; the new `MAX_MODEL_ENTITY_REFS` bounds **adversarial model output** (a degenerate REASON emission driving an unbounded sequential GBrain read loop). Both are 200, so sharing looks free — but **coupled, a future tuning of one silently retunes the other's THREAT MODEL**: someone raising the meeting cap for a legitimately large attendee list would unknowingly widen the adversarial fan-out bound. The values coincide; the reasons do not. ⭐ **L5/L37 governs a literal that must AGREE with another** (a grammar, path convention, matcher, enum both sides parse) — **not two values that merely happen to be equal.** Ask: *if one changed for its own reason, must the other change with it?* No ⇒ different constants sharing a number, and merging couples two decisions that should move independently. **The discipline is symmetric and both directions have bitten this project:** a duplicated MATCHER diverges silently ⇒ single-source it (L39; the `branch`-literal duplication in the L75 amendment); a shared POLICY THRESHOLD couples unrelated decisions ⇒ split it. Naming + an in-code note are what stop the split reading as an oversight. ⚠ **A plan's `Done-when` is a hypothesis too** (L81 aimed at the tracker, not the brief) — written before anyone checked whether the caps shared a reason, and a circular import made sharing infeasible anyway; the divergence is recorded ON the task, because a shipped implementation contradicting its own Done-when is what a later reader "fixes." `pin: synthesis-planner.test.ts` (cap asserted via the ACTUAL call count, not the reported number — property not mechanism, L70) · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. [full](LESSONS.md#88) |
| 89 | 2026-07-29 | [A gate named for a check it does not perform is worse than an absent gate — its passing is read as evidence](LESSONS.md#89) | An implementer's `/session-end` reported repo-wide `pnpm lint` as FAILING. Running it gave the opposite, and the opposite is worse: **`Tasks: 11 successful, exit 0`** — while **every** package's `lint` script is **`tsc --noEmit`**, **ESLint is not installed** (`node_modules/eslint` absent) and **has no config anywhere** (no `.eslintrc*`/`eslint.config.*`), and **no package or root defines `format:check`** (so `pnpm format:check` fails as an unknown script). ⇒ **`pnpm lint` is `pnpm typecheck` under another name; every `/preflight clean` and `lint clean` claim in this project's history means "typecheck passed" — there has never been ANY lint coverage.** ⭐ **The failure mode is not that the gate is broken — it is that the gate PASSES.** A broken gate is fixed the first time it runs; a gate that passes while checking nothing it names keeps producing green that gets consumed as evidence by implementers, orchestrators, and every round seal. ⛔ **CORRECTION TO THIS LESSON'S OWN FIRST DRAFT — it was NOT undiscovered, and that makes it sharper.** `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` `#### Residuals (1)` has carried **three** bullets on it since **2026-07-26**, one already stating the consequence in almost these words *and already drawing the L56 analogy*: *"`/preflight` currently CLAIMS a lint gate it does not run … A gate that reports success without executing is the same false-assurance shape as this round's four rule-5 findings, one layer up in the tooling."* It was found, explained, correctly analogized, and re-confirmed by a second area — and then five implementers, an orchestrator and a lead spent a round reading "lint clean" as evidence, after which I re-discovered it as novel. ⭐ **So the actual lesson is: recording a defect does not stop its false signal being believed** — a finding in a file competes with a green check in a terminal, and the green wins, because it arrives at the moment of decision and the file does not. ⭐ **Mechanism is procedural:** that bullet says it was *"moved out of Carry-forward — phase-owned tooling debt, not next-1-2-brief working set."* **That move was CORRECT and it is what stopped the item being read** — Carry-forward is triaged every `/orchestrate-end`; phase `Residuals` are not. Correct bookkeeping produced the blind spot. ⇒ **Second rule: when you de-prioritize a known false-green out of the triaged set, DISARM the signal at the same time** (rename the script, or make `/preflight` announce "lint = typecheck only") — moving the record without disarming the green is the trap. **What made it durable: six documents describe a tool that is not installed** — root `CLAUDE.md`'s `Lint \| ESLint` stack row, all six area `CLAUDE.md` "Standard commands" blocks, and `/preflight`'s `lint && typecheck && test` — the same *three-surfaces-agree-one-has-it* shape as the approval terminal guard, arriving in the toolchain instead of the code. **Mirror of [L80](#80)** (*"replace the gate with a constant DENY — anything red?"*): this is a **constant ALLOW** for the property it names, and a constant-DENY gate at least announces itself by blocking. **[L82](#82) governs the disposition** — make it true or retract it; ⛔ do NOT re-point it as "typecheck is a kind of linting," which is L82's forbidden middle. **Do:** run any gate you rely on once and read **what it executed**, not whether it passed; for a composite gate confirm each leg invokes a DISTINCT tool (two legs on one binary means one leg is decoration); and when a tooling claim arrives second-hand, run it — the reporter's instinct was right and their conclusion inverted. ⛔ Recorded NOT fixed (installing ESLint across 11 packages is a new arc, forbidden by the teardown boundary) and left visible per L82. `pattern: [ -d node_modules/eslint ] \|\| echo "WARN: lint gate claims ESLint; ESLint is not installed"`. [full](LESSONS.md#89) |
| 90 | 2026-07-29 | [A non-vacuity guard must be satisfied by the test's SUBJECT, not by the environment — delete the feature and see if the guard still passes](LESSONS.md#90) | #8, the round's final slice, **both halves self-caught by the author**. `no_naming_attribute_overclaims` sweeps `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`/`aria-describedby`/`title` on the posture subtree for banned claim tokens. Knowing a filter-then-assert-nothing-matched test passes vacuously over an empty set (L54), they added a non-vacuity check: *assert the attributes exist.* ⚠ **The non-vacuity guard was itself vacuous** — pre-existing unrelated `aria-label`s elsewhere in the page satisfied "attributes exist", so **the test would have passed while never touching the new pill at all.** It guarded the DOM's general habit of having labels, not this feature's labels. ⭐ **The check that finds it: DELETE YOUR FEATURE ENTIRELY — does the non-vacuity guard still pass?** If yes it is anchored to the environment, and *"something matched"* was never the same claim as *"the thing I am testing matched."* Fixed by giving the pill its OWN populated `title` (the scope sentence) and anchoring non-vacuity to that specific node's attribute, so the guard cannot be satisfied by anything the feature does not own. **Second half, same slice:** they had confirmed the banned-token regex **passes on clean text** but not that it **FIRES on a real restoration** — L75 landing on the pin guarding this round's signature defect, i.e. a negative pin verified only in the state where it should be silent; closed by reintroducing `"nothing leaves this machine"` and confirming FOUR tests caught it. **Why it is the right note to end on:** every earlier instance this round was found by someone else (reviewer, orchestrator, lead); these two were found by the author asking what their own guard would fail to notice — **the only version that scales, because the reviewer reads the assertion and finds it reasonable, which it is. The defect is never in what the assertion SAYS; it is in what SATISFIES it.** **Do:** for any anti-vacuity guard, state what anchors it to the subject; for any absence pin, verify it FIRES on a genuine restoration. Both are one extra run inside the slice. `pin: egress-settings-page.test.tsx` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. [full](LESSONS.md#90) |
| 91 | 2026-07-29 | [A clearance that stops at the routing layer is indistinguishable from a clearance never given — whoever imposes a hold owns releasing it](LESSONS.md#91) | The orchestrator's OWN failure on the round's last slice, caught by the lead. #8 was rule-5, so the lead asked its Step-10 commit HOLD until they cleared the Step-9; the orchestrator relayed the hold, the lead cleared #8 **to the orchestrator** (the lead does not DM implementers) — **and the orchestrator did not pass it on**, spending the next three commits banking lessons while **the team stalled**: implementer idle, slice complete, files uncommitted, correctly obeying a hold nobody lifted. ⭐ **From the implementer's side a clearance stopped at the relay is byte-identical to a clearance never given** — everyone waits, nobody is blocked by anything real, and **no signal anywhere reports a problem**: the same false-green shape this round spent itself closing, relocated from the code into the COORDINATION layer. **Two actionable halves:** (1) **imposing a hold creates a blocking edge and the holder owns releasing it** — "hold until X clears" makes the RELAY of X load-bearing, and it was treated as ordinary correspondence rather than the thing a teammate was parked on; (2) **the status board would have shown it and NOBODY read it** — the task sat `in_progress` with an idle owner and a dirty tree the whole time, visible to **both** roles: the orchestrator was reading its own commit queue, the lead was reading idle-notifications as *normal quiet*. Two roles, same channel available to each, neither looking ⇒ not one agent forgetting a check, but **nothing polls the board** while both parties consume event streams instead of state. ⛔ **WHO ACTUALLY CAUGHT IT: THE OWNER, by noticing every pane had gone quiet.** Not the orchestrator, not the lead, not `TaskList`, not a heartbeat, not `/context-check`, not a tier threshold — **the entire monitoring apparatus reported normal while nothing moved for twenty minutes.** ⭐ **A stall is the only failure mode whose signature is the ABSENCE of a signal:** every other defect this round announced itself (a red test, a failing gate, a doc contradiction, a mutation staying green), whereas **a stall announces itself as calm** — our instrumentation detects events and has nothing watching for their absence, and an idle notification is byte-indistinguishable from "correctly finished, nothing to do." Recorded as an **unmet monitoring gap, not a save**, since a human noticing an absence is exactly the detection method a governed autonomous system cannot rely on. The missing check, answerable from data we already have: *is any task `in_progress` whose owner has been idle longer than a slice takes, with uncommitted files in its territory?* ⚠ **Kept because it is the useful part: the lesson being written during that window was L90 — about a guard that passes while checking nothing.** The lapse and the lesson occupied the same twenty minutes. **Knowing a failure mode in the abstract does not make you notice it in your own workflow**, which is exactly why these are written as mechanical checks rather than things to be mindful of. **Do:** write an imposed hold down as an owed release (same rule as L51 — if it lives only in your head or in a message, it is not tracked), release it before any other work, and in a hub-and-spoke team treat the relay as a critical path. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — mitigation: check `TaskList` for `in_progress` tasks with idle owners before starting unrelated work. [full](LESSONS.md#91) |
| 92 | 2026-07-29 | [A certified phase's acceptance statement is IMMUTABLE — new work anchored to its spec goes to an ACTIVE phase; the Spec anchor carries traceability, the placement carries ownership](LESSONS.md#92) | Scheduling §DEC-CANDGATE leg 1 (task 13.18) needed a numbered home, and the anchor-native one was obvious: the slice adds a `packages/contracts` model and **Phase 1's Spec anchors are exactly `§3 · Appendix A · §16/REQ-S-006`** ⇒ `### 1.16`. But Phase 1 is `✅ done · CLEAR 06-30` and its acceptance line is `- [x] DONE · a039e86e · phase-certified` + *"All 1.X checkboxes ticked."* The orchestrator noticed an unticked `1.16` would flip a `✅` row the owner reads, judged that not theirs to do quietly, and **surfaced it rather than absorbing it — right instinct, too-small reasoning.** ⭐ **The lead's ruling: a phase certification is an AUDIT ARTIFACT, not a status field** — adding an unticked task under *"All 1.X checkboxes ticked"* does not flip one row, it **retroactively redefines every past certification in the project** to mean *"complete, except for whatever we added later"*; that integrity loss dwarfs a phase-boundary smudge. ⭐ **The objection dissolves once PLACEMENT and ANCHORING are separated:** the `Spec:` anchor carries **traceability** (13.18 still declares `§3 · Appendix A · §16/REQ-S-006`, so `/phase-exit`'s auditor reads those anchors and the audit surface follows the task anywhere), while the phase carries **ownership** (who works it, which round closes it, whose gate it blocks). So it went to **Phase 13** — active, and where all four instances of the defect arose — with `spec-lint`'s sanctioned `widens phase scope because…` declaration. ⚠ **The near-miss worth naming: the alternative was LOCALLY correct on every visible rule** (right anchors, right package, right phase for the spec) **and would have quietly damaged an audit trail spanning fourteen certified phases — a rule that is right about one row can be wrong about the artifact the rows compose into.** **Do:** work whose anchors point into a **certified** phase goes to an **active** phase with an explicit widening declaration, recorded in-task; never add an unticked box under a `[x]`-certified acceptance statement; if no active phase can own it, escalate rather than edit the certification. `pattern: awk` over `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` for an unticked task under a certified acceptance line (real fix = a `plan-lint` rule, recorded as a scaffolding follow-up). [full](LESSONS.md#92) |
| 93 | 2026-07-29 | [Code citations in durable prose fail in BOTH directions — they rot silently, AND a wrongly-scoped audit condemns healthy ones; the audit is the more dangerous half](LESSONS.md#93) | Durable prose here cites `file.ts:NN` constantly and **nothing lints those citations.** This round produced what looked like **three** rot instances; it was **two**, and the third taught more than both. **Real rot:** (1) **L69** cited `:83`/`:178`, both drifted — found by an implementer reading the file while briefing 9.22 (*"an L71 instance inside L69"*); (2) **`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:90`**'s §DEC-CANDGATE ledger cited `planner.ts:180-182` and *"consumed at `:197`"*, both stale post-13.8h — **inside the paragraph written explicitly for a cold pickup** (corrected to `:197-200` and `:220`). ⛔ **The FALSE POSITIVE:** a premise-check reported **L49** cites a `ZOD_BY_ID` registration + membership-guard rows that **do not exist** — grep confirmed zero hits, and a brief was drafted saying *"follow the code, not L49's stale prose."* **L49 was correct:** `ZOD_BY_ID` is in `packages/domain/test/fixtures/fixtures.test.ts`, membership rows in `packages/contracts/test/primitives/shared.test.ts`. The search covered only `packages/contracts/{src,test}` and **generalized "not in this package" to "does not exist."** ⭐ **It reproduced the exact blind spot the lesson exists to close** — L49's content IS *"the checklist reaches OUTSIDE `packages/contracts`; missing the fixture/`ZOD_BY_ID` breaks the DOMAIN meta-test, not the contracts suite — **green-in-contracts ≠ done**"* ⇒ an audit scoped to contracts was **guaranteed** to declare it stale. **Had it shipped:** a green `packages/contracts`, a clean report, and `@sow/domain` red with a half-frozen seam — **L49's precise failure mode, caused by a brief that cited L49 while contradicting it.** ⚠ **The two directions demand OPPOSITE responses, which is why one-directional framing makes the next reader worse:** rot invites *"trust cited lessons less"* — and that reaction is exactly what would have landed instance 3, whose correct response is *"do not delete the guard the citation defends."* ⇒ **a citation that fails to resolve is a QUESTION, never a verdict:** ask *"did I look everywhere it could live?"* before *"is the prose wrong?"*, and in this repo the answer is usually another package, because the seams deliberately span them. **Do:** search the **whole repo** before declaring a citation stale; land **one** number in **every** place it appears (a half-corrected citation is a new instance); a lesson gets the benefit of the doubt until the search is proven exhaustive — it was written by someone holding the code. ⛔ **Deliberately NOT done: a repo-wide citation sweep** (task 24.6 territory, out of scope; and this lesson argues a cheap sweep yields false positives faster than fixes). `pattern: grep -oE '[a-zA-Z0-9_/.-]+\.ts:[0-9]+' packages/*/LESSONS.md` then verify path exists + has ≥ that many lines — ⚠ **states its own limit: catches a citation past EOF or at a deleted file, CANNOT catch a line that still exists but now says something else, which is the common case (both real instances).** Stronger form: cite a stable symbol name alongside the line number. ⛔ **NAMING VARIANT (added same day):** `packages/domain/test/fixtures/fixtures.test.ts:128` is named *"provides exactly one VALID fixture for every registered **Appendix-A** schema"* while its mechanism (`:129`) measures against `defaultSchemaRegistry.ids()` — an **unconditional glob** over `schemas/*.schema.json`. **No Appendix-A filter exists; the name asserts a narrowing the assertion does not perform.** It caused a real error the same day: the orchestrator read the name, inferred that L49's new-model checklist attached only to Appendix-A models, and wrote that conditionality into a brief + task description; the implementer traced the registry in source and overturned it (the checklist is mandatory the moment a schema file exists) — had it been followed, `packages/contracts` would have gone green with `@sow/domain` red, L49's exact failure mode. ⭐ **Why it earns its own line: an overstated name is INVISIBLE TO ITS AUTHOR and load-bearing for everyone else** — the author knows what the assertion does, so the name never misleads them and no test failure ever surfaces the gap; it misleads the NEXT reader, who has only the name. **A name is documentation with none of documentation's review.** ⇒ **a test name is a claim about scope; if it names a filter the assertion must perform it** — when they disagree fix the NAME (the assertion is usually the correct half, and renaming is free). **Do:** read the assertion before inferring a rule from a name; prefer a name that under-claims to one that over-claims. [full](LESSONS.md#93) |
| 94 | 2026-07-29 | [A correction must land in EVERY channel that carries the claim — and channels rank by what a stale value CAUSES, not by how authoritative they look](LESSONS.md#94) | The orchestrator retracted a wrong finding (that **L49** was stale — see [L93](LESSONS.md#93)), fixed brief 219, and **told the lead the correction had landed. It had landed in one channel of four.** ✅ brief correction-3 section · ✅ task metadata · ⛔ **task #3 DESCRIPTION still carried the retracted version verbatim** (found by the LEAD, and being read by an implementer **already working the slice**) · ⛔ **three downstream references inside the same brief still pointed the old way** (found only on a re-audit), including the *"verify the premises"* instruction — the first thing the implementer reads — which still said *"a probably-stale L49 citation."* ⚠ **`metadata ≠ description`, and correcting the section that STATES a claim does not correct the sentences that REPEAT it** — the argument was fixed while the conclusions drawn from it stayed, in the same file, just re-stamped and declared fixed. ⛔ **A correction landing in one place and not another is WORSE than no correction: two authoritative sources now disagree and neither announces it** — before, one source was wrong; after, the reader picks. ⭐ **Round's signature shape, THIRD instance** — same as the seven Phase-9 checkboxes reading `[ ] OPEN` while the phase's own acceptance prose credited every landing commit (*correct where nobody looks, wrong where it gets read*), and same as **L91**'s clearance (correct at the routing layer, absent at the consuming one). ⭐ **The new, mechanical half: NOT ALL CHANNEL DRIFT IS EQUAL — rank by consequence.** A stale **instruction** fails **OPEN** (the retracted L49 text pointed at *"skip the domain checklist"*, i.e. straight at the half-frozen seam); a stale **spec-lint stamp** fails **SAFE** (Step 0 just re-lints, costing one run). Both were stale in the same description; **only one could hurt.** And the stamp rotted **three times** (`@9632c07d→@ed597a6a→@1469120a→@8fe4b89d`) because **every content edit re-hashes the brief** ⇒ a hash in durable prose is guaranteed to rot. ⇒ **fix is STRUCTURAL not diligence:** the stamp was removed from the task description and replaced with a metadata pointer + the regeneration command — **a volatile value does not belong in durable instruction prose**, because its drift trains readers to distrust the text around it, which is where the load-bearing content lives. **Do:** enumerate a claim's channels BEFORE declaring the fix done (brief incl. every repeating sentence · task description · metadata · messages already sent), fix the highest-consequence channel first, grep the retracted wording to prove none survives, and when a correction REVERSES an instruction someone may have acted on, **say that it reversed** rather than just publishing the new version. `pattern: grep -rniE '<retracted wording>' docs/briefs/ IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md packages/*/LESSONS.md` as the **LAST** step — a correction is done not when the right text exists but when the wrong text does not; ⚠ task descriptions/metadata are session-scoped and NOT greppable, enumerate by hand (L51). ⛔ **AMENDMENT (+1h): applied to the INSTANCE, not the CLASS — fourth instance.** The author wrote *"a hash in durable prose is guaranteed to rot"* (**a claim about every task description**) then de-inlined the stamp from **task #3 only**; the lead enumerated the rest — **#1 was stale since dispatch** (`@f9bdfa76` vs the brief's actual `@e23ff413`, **the very hash the orchestrator quoted in #1's own dispatch message**), #2 current only because that brief had no edits yet. ⭐ **The distinction that rescues the consequence-ranking instead of undermining it: "which do I fix FIRST" and "what is the FULL SET" are DIFFERENT QUESTIONS, and answering only the first is how *fix first* becomes *fix only*.** The lead's de-prioritisation of #1 was sound (stale stamp = fail-safe); ranking gives order, never extent — **ask both, in that order.** ⇒ when a lesson names a class, the fix is not done until the class is **enumerated**, including members whose staleness is harmless, **because the harmless ones are where the habit forms.** ⚠ Committed by the lesson's own author within an hour of writing it — knowing a failure mode does not make you apply it at the right scope. **Do (added):** after fixing an instance ask *"what SET does this belong to, and have I enumerated every member?"* and record the enumeration, not just the fix. [full](LESSONS.md#94) |
| 95 | 2026-07-29 | [A type consumed as if its TS annotation constrained runtime-untrusted data needs a BOUNDARY schema — which rejects-or-passes, never rewrites](LESSONS.md#95) | **The §DEC-CANDGATE thesis, and why four fixes in one round did not add up to one fix.** `EntityRef` was declared only in `packages/knowledge`, self-described *"knowledge-local, not a frozen contract"*, with no schema anywhere, while `planSynthesis`'s guard validated only `workspaceId`/`sourceRefs` — so `kind: EntityKind` was a **COMPILE-TIME claim about RUNTIME-UNTRUSTED data**, defended ad hoc per consumer. One shape, four bugs: the uncapped `entityRefs` fan-out (13.8h) · `requiresApproval` enforced nowhere, the §9.8 Approvals bypass (L57) · attendee name shapes no validator rejected (L60) · `kind` indexing an object literal, the prototype-chain hole (L65). **Every fix closed an INSTANCE while the shape stayed open, so instance five looked novel.** ⇒ **the fix belongs at the BOUNDARY schema, not at each consumer** (a consumer-side guard protects that consumer; the next starts from zero). ⚠ **The gap is usually already wider than it looks** — `EntityRef` had ALREADY crossed into `packages/evals` via the knowledge barrel, i.e. a de facto cross-package contract with zero runtime validation; check who imports it before scoping. **Two load-bearing schema properties:** (a) **rejects-or-passes, NEVER rewrites** — no `.trim()`/`.transform()`/coercion, since `name` feeds `entitySlug()`/`faithfulKey()` path+key derivation and **a schema that mutates its input is a SECOND PRODUCER** (the one-writer rule violated at the validation layer); (b) **`.strict()` closes a smuggled field at BOTH tiers** — `additionalProperties:false` SURVIVES JSON-Schema translation (unlike cross-field refines, L3) so ajv gates it independently of Zod, closing the model-supplied-`path`-reaching-a-writer-owned-surface shape (§ARM-RESEARCH 13.8j/k/l) twice; ⭐ established EMPIRICALLY by the reviewer, which is what separates it from `.strict()`-as-tidiness. Prefer `z.enum` to an object-literal lookup (real Set-membership ⇒ L65 cannot recur through it). ⛔ **A boundary schema with no caller closes nothing** — leg 1 ships the gate, leg 2 CALLS it; "the candidate-data gap is closed" after leg 1 would be the half-gate-that-reads-as-coverage the owner warned about. `pin: test/models/entity-ref.test.ts + schemas/entity-ref.schema.json freeze`. [full](LESSONS.md#95) |
| 96 | 2026-07-29 | [A `CLAUDE.md` claim that an invariant "is pinned" is ITSELF a claim needing verification; a scanner adds a companion guard for vectors it cannot classify](LESSONS.md#96) | Forbidden-pattern #2 asserted the §2.5 pure-root invariant was pinned by "a boundary test"; **a repo-wide search found none**, and nothing asserted the dep sets ⇒ **an unbacked claim of coverage is WORSE than an acknowledged gap, because nobody re-checks what a convention doc says is already enforced** (same class as `lint`-is-typecheck). ⭐ **The slice then reproduced the defect in miniature and fixed it properly:** its own scanner header claimed to catch a tsconfig `paths` reach-around — **it did not** (a path-aliased specifier is neither `@sow/`-prefixed nor relative, so it passes both branches unscanned). Fix was NOT deleting the sentence: **narrow the comment to what is detected AND add a companion guard asserting no `paths` map exists**, so if one appears it goes red and the scanner needs a real third branch. ⇒ **a specifier-classifying scanner cannot classify what doesn't look like a specifier — for every such vector, a guard on its ABSENCE or an explicitly named blind spot, never a comment implying coverage.** `pattern: grep -rn "pins this\|is pinned" packages/*/CLAUDE.md` — every hit is a claim to verify. [full](LESSONS.md#96) |
| 97 | 2026-07-29 | [A BARE IDENTIFIER IS A POINTER TO NOTHING THAT READS AS A POINTER TO SOMETHING — qualify every cross-namespace reference](LESSONS.md#97) | Three independent instances in one round. **(1) `#13`** in a handoff's desktop queue — no tracker entry, no description, detail dead with a prior session's task list; **ambiguous across THREE numbering systems**, and the only resolvable `#13` is the **owner-ENABLE HARD LINE** ("lead-run with the owner — NOT an orchestrator step") ⇒ **briefing it would have pointed at an owner crossing while looking routine.** **(2) `L26`** cited in a CONTRACTS brief while meaning WORKER's — lesson numbers are per-area and independent, so a bare `L##` resolves differently in four namespaces and **looks valid in all of them**; the implementer inherited and copied it, correctly given the context. **(3) `L49`** plan references that were worker's, not contracts'. ⇒ **the failure is not staleness — it is that a bare identifier LOOKS FINE**: a stale citation fails to resolve and gets investigated, an ambiguous one resolves to the wrong thing silently. ⚠ **Worse for `#13`: it sat in a DURABLE FILE, so it read as recorded** — L51 says close-out debt goes in a file, and **the corollary is that a file entry must carry enough scope to be actionable COLD. A number is not scope.** **Do:** qualify every cross-area citation (`worker L26`); never queue a bare `#N` without a one-line description; resolve before acting, and if it cannot be resolved SAY SO rather than inferring. [full](LESSONS.md#97) |
| 98 | 2026-07-29 | [An owner of a shared doc holding UNCOMMITTED state makes HEAD lie to everyone who verifies against HEAD — the counterpart to L83](LESSONS.md#98) | **L83** says verify at a COMMIT, never the live tree.
