The lead did exactly that — checked task 9.24's checkbox **at HEAD**, read `[ ] OPEN`, and correctly concluded an orchestrator claim was false. **9.24 was DONE** (shipped a prior round; this round's seven-stale-checkbox reconciliation had ticked it) — **in the orchestrator's working tree, UNCOMMITTED, for thirteen commits.** ⇒ **verifying at HEAD is right and returns a WRONG ANSWER while a doc-owner holds uncommitted tracker state**; L83 protects you from a moving tree, nothing protected the lead from a STALE HEAD. **Both halves of "don't trust the tree, trust HEAD" can fail at once.** ⚠ **The asymmetry making this the ORCHESTRATOR's problem:** implementers commit per slice so their state converges every cycle, but the orchestrator owns the tracker and the cadence rides it on the ROUND-TERMINAL commit — **so tracker truth is DESIGNED to be stale for a whole round, for the one file the entire team verifies against by policy.** **Do:** commit tracker reconciliation PROMPTLY, not at the seal — a checkbox tick is not round bookkeeping, it is the answer to a question teammates ask HEAD; and if you must hold plan state, say so when anyone reports a task's status. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — mitigation: after any batch of ticks, commit before the next dispatch. [full](LESSONS.md#98) |
| 99 | 2026-07-29 | [Enumerate the occurrence set, then CLASSIFY each member — an occurrence of a stale citation is not always a stale citation](LESSONS.md#99) | A stale pin name sat in FOUR places; the instruction "fix all four" was right about extent, wrong about treatment. Three were **citations** (`pin:` lines that must resolve); the fourth was a **QUOTATION** — `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`'s own finding record naming the stale string as its subject (*"L76 cites a pin named X that exists in NO TEST FILE"*). Substituting there makes the record assert the LIVE guard doesn't exist. ⇒ **a citation-rot record has to keep quoting the rotted citation**; enumerate the set (L93), then classify each member before touching it — "enumerate" does not imply "repair." **Larger half:** 9.30 had DELETED the mechanism L76 describes, so a pure rename would leave prose asserting a deleted re-gate while pointing at a live test — **worse than the stale name**, which at least fails visibly under grep. Resolution: rule stands, mechanism is history, closure is by construction (L71 — a correction is a claim, so verify the cited thing still DOES what the citing prose says). Checked L72's two unflagged sibling names too (live at `:75`/`:118`) — that is what closes a class instead of an instance. ⭐ **Mechanizable, and it found a FOURTH instance by execution:** `revoke_return_makes_no_unearned_local_claim` (L69's pin) names no test in the file it cites. ⚠ **Known false-positive mode, because it fired on itself** — the corpus-wide form flagged 2 legitimate quotations; even pin-scoped, prose mentioning `pin:` while quoting a stale name trips it. Output = candidates to classify, never defects (L89: a check with permanent false positives gets disbelieved). `pattern:` pin-line-scoped grep (in the full entry); `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` for the classification half. [full](LESSONS.md#99) |
| 100 | 2026-07-29 | [A negative claim from a scoped search is only as strong as its scope — so state the scope WITH the claim](LESSONS.md#100) | *"There is NO `WorkspaceSchema.parse` on any write path"* was FALSE — `defaultWorkspace()` parses at `packages/contracts/src/models/workspace.ts:112` and the create path writes its output (`provisionWorkspace.ts:294`). The search covered `apps/worker/src` + `packages/db/src`, which **structurally cannot contain** `packages/contracts/src`. **Third instance of one defect by a third mechanism in one round:** wrong **pattern** (regex couldn't match the form) · merged **output** (two results read as one) · wrong **scope** (paths excluded where it lives). ⇒ **The rule is NOT "widen the pattern"** — that fixes only mechanism one. **A negative existence claim inherits its search's boundaries, and they must travel with it.** *"I grepped and found nothing"* is not a finding; *"no hit for `<pattern>` under `<paths>`"* is, because a reader can see the hole and the person holding the file can contradict it cheaply. **Why negatives specifically:** a positive carries its own evidence and self-falsifies; a negative's evidence is an ABSENCE, indistinguishable from an absence of looking — so the scope IS the evidence (kin L91, L75). ⚠ **Expensive here because the false negative fed a CORRECTION**, which would have replaced a stale citation with a confidently-wrong one (L99). ⭐ **Counter-example worth copying:** "both dialects × `get`/`list` = four sites" was stated WITH its scope, which is what let the next reader find `updateProvisioningFields`'s `.returning()` cast too — **six sites, three per dialect**. A scoped claim gets corrected; an unscoped one gets believed. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — a negative claim without a stated scope is treated as unverified, not as evidence. [full](LESSONS.md#100) |
| 101 | 2026-07-29 | [`ARCHITECTURE.md` has real numbered subsections ONLY under §19 — every other `§N.x` in project prose is shorthand wearing an anchor costume](LESSONS.md#101) | Phase 9 cites `§4.5`(8×) `§14.1/3/5` `§13.5` `§9.4/9.5/9.32` — **none is an `ARCHITECTURE.md` section.** Referents: `§4.5`→`docs/design/ui-ux/ui-ux-spec.md:206` (another doc's own numbering) · `§14.x`/`§13.5`/`§9.x`→**plan task numbers** · and `USER_FLOWS 3/5/6/13` — **Flow 13 doesn't exist** in `USER_FLOWS.md`. ⭐ **Checkable structural fact: only `### §19.1`–`§19.13` are real subsections, so any `§N.x` with N≠19 is BY CONSTRUCTION not an architecture anchor.** One lesson, not eight repairs: the project uses `§` for ≥4 numbering systems and the token doesn't say which — **the failure isn't a broken link, it's a token that resolves PLAUSIBLY in the wrong system.** A dangling citation gets investigated; an ambiguous one gets believed (same asymmetry as false-CLEAR vs false-RED). ⚠ **Cost a real escalation** — `§4.5` anchors the leg that makes Phase 9 un-exitable, so "the blocker is pinned to a nonexistent section" went toward the owner and was withdrawn once the referent was found: **a formatting choice manufactured a scope question.** ⚠ **`spec-lint` catches this in BRIEFS only** (verified: it rejected a draft carrying `§9.36` + a stray `§13`, authored by the person who'd reported the finding an hour before) — **nothing lints the plan prose where all the instances live.** ⛔ **Scale measured, not estimated: 87 candidate lines** in the plan (vs 180 legit `§19.x` excluded); Phase 9 was 8 — budget against 87, report how many were real. **Do:** architecture ⇒ bare `§N`; plan ⇒ `task N.M` (no `§`); other doc ⇒ path + its numbering. `pattern: grep -nE '§(1[0-8]\|[0-9])\.[0-9]' IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`; inherits L99's limit. [full](LESSONS.md#101)  ⛔ **CLASSIFIER CORRECTED 2026-08-11 — `§19.x` AND `§2.5` are real; every other `§N.x` is shorthand.** ⚠ **The "only §19 / by construction" universal was FALSE** (`ARCHITECTURE.md:80` is a real `## §2.5` heading, corroborated three ways) **and applying it would have STRIPPED the one cross-cutting seam anchor the brief template mandates.** ⭐ **Corrected in `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` 2026-07-30 and never propagated here until now — L94's shape, found by `LES-1`.** |
| 102 | 2026-07-29 | [Before recording a rule as unpinned, ask whether it is the KIND of rule a test can pin at all](LESSONS.md#102) | The L69 pin-repair ruling was *"either a test pins the rule, or record the pin ABSENT — and absent ⇒ a candidate slice for a later round."* Both halves wrong. **(a) FALSE DICHOTOMY** — the pin's subject existed in the cited file under **two different names**, evidenced by an in-code `9.22 ⚠ SEMANTICS CHANGE (L69)` comment naming the lesson: **author-declared linkage is not name resemblance**, so citing it smuggles nothing — the very distinction the ruling meant to protect and its binary made unreachable. ⇒ when a ruling enumerates outcomes, check the enumeration is exhaustive before reading "neither" as absence. **(b) L69's rule is a PROCESS rule about test-authoring judgment — NO test can pin it**, so "a later slice writes the pin" created an obligation **no slice could discharge**, in the same message that ruled against phantom pins. ⭐ **A phantom obligation is the same defect as a phantom pin, facing forward**: one says "this is checked" when nothing does, the other "this will be checked" when nothing can — and the second accretes backlog closable only by re-deriving that it was impossible. **The check, asked BEFORE writing the enforcement line:** property-of-the-code ⇒ `pin:` + mutation-verify (L75) · mechanically detectable shape ⇒ `pattern:` · **judgment rule ⇒ `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`, and NO slice owed** (name the enforcement POINT instead, as L71 does). ⚠ Quiet failure mode: `accepted:` reads as the weakest option so there's a pull to promise a pin — **that is less rigorous**, substituting an unproducible deliverable for an honest statement about the rule's nature. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: the moment a `pin:`/`pattern:`/`accepted:` line is authored at Step-9 routing. [full](LESSONS.md#102) |
| 103 | 2026-07-29 | [**HOUSE PATTERN** — make the violation UNREPRESENTABLE; a detector is belt, never the mechanism](LESSONS.md#103) | A **convention**, named because the team converged on it unprompted — **four-for-four in one round, four independent authors, three areas**: 9.35's `fallback: (reset) => ReactNode` with **no error param** (rule 7 by type) · `ProvisioningOwnedFields` making a posture write **untypeable** · 9.21-B's partial variant carrying only a **closed literal**, no message field · 9.36's parse as the **only** constructor of the repo's return. ⇒ **Prefer a design where the bad state does not typecheck over one where it is detected.** A detector answers "did it happen?"; unrepresentability answers "can it happen?" — and only the second is closed under **future callers**, which is where all four were actually leaking. ⭐ **Precedent is the project's own and now 3-for-3 on safety rule 5:** worker **L73** (a `process.env` denylist was *structurally unwinnable* for a rule-5 completeness invariant — grew **13→81** — replaced by a spawn-env allowlist, denylist retained explicitly as **"belt, not the completeness mechanism"**) · worker **L74** (same call for settings files: presence-degrade subsuming a field enumeration) · 9.36 (a cast census would have been the third instance). **The tell you're on the losing side: the enumeration keeps growing when re-checked, and each re-check is CORRECT** — that's not diligence converging, it's evidence the property doesn't live where you're counting. **Do:** ask "cheapest edit that makes the violation representable again?" — if it's "add a call site," you have a detector; put the invariant where the compiler enforces it (closed literal per L31, branded type, absent param, narrowed field set); **keep the detector but LABEL it belt and report it separately from the mechanism**, else a later reader improves the mechanism away; if the belt is a census, both-anchor + mutation-verify (9.22's was prefix-anchored and accepted a superset). ⚠ **Limits:** costs type complexity; cannot express a RUNTIME property (freshness/ordering/live external state — those want a fail-closed gate with a reason code, L80); and a type claim over trust-boundary data is real only AFTER a runtime parse — `$type<>()` on a DB column is a compile-time claim about runtime-untrusted bytes, which is the defect 9.36 fixes, not an instance of this pattern. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 2.5, where "how would you detect this?" should be answered "you wouldn't — it won't compile." [full](LESSONS.md#103) |
| 104 | 2026-07-29 | [A mechanical check cannot distinguish a USE from a MENTION — so the document explaining a rule is the one most likely to violate it mechanically](LESSONS.md#104) | **Three demonstrations in one round, twice inside a mechanism built to catch the very thing:** L99's stale-pin pattern flagged the correction prose that QUOTES the stale name · a `CLEAR`/`BLOCKED` grep verifying the audits emitted no verdict flagged **all three**, every hit the **disclaimer** · `spec-lint` rejected brief 226's gate-finding note because the note quoted the token it documented, and its first rewrite still did (tripped **twice**). ⭐ **Not a defect in any check — it is what greps ARE:** a check matches a FORM, and use and mention share the form, so any grep-shaped gate trips on prose ABOUT what it forbids. **Corollary 1:** the document explaining a rule is the one most likely to violate it mechanically. ⛔ **Corollary 2 (load-bearing):** a check with no use/mention escape will **systematically SUPPRESS ITS OWN DOCUMENTATION** — the path of least resistance when a gate rejects your finding-record is to delete the discussion, which makes the check green and the gate an enforcer of silence about the thing it catches. **Do:** expect the finding-record to trip the check; write it token-free or give the check an explicit escape; **never** resolve it by removing the explanation; state this limit beside every `pattern:` line (hit count = CANDIDATE count). ⚠ **Don't make the check cleverer** — distinguishing use from mention needs sentence understanding, and an almost-clever check is worse than a blunt one because its false-negative surface stops being obvious. Self-referentially, a check for this rule would flag this row. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — necessarily. [full](LESSONS.md#104) |
| 105 | 2026-07-29 | [The most recent implementation is not the most correct one — it is the one that most recently accepted a compromise](LESSONS.md#105) | 13.8m-**A** made `refusals` **required** on the receipt; 13.8m-**B** made it **optional on the worker seam** to keep 13 containment fakes valid, **recording the bound in-code** (*"a FUTURE adapter could omit it and the sink would silently never fire"*). Briefing the meeting path, *"mirror the newest sibling"* would have carried B's optionality onto a path **with no fakes to migrate** — importing a compromise that existed only to avoid a migration. ⇒ **"Follow the newest sibling" is the default that spreads recorded bounds, and a recorded bound spreads SILENTLY because it was already deemed acceptable once** — its acceptance reads as settled precedent rather than a local trade. **The right template was the OLDER one** (required ⇒ omission unrepresentable, L103). **The check is cheap because the compromise documented itself:** before mirroring an implementation, **read its own caveats** — an in-code note explaining its shape is telling you whether that shape is the design or the concession. **Ask which sibling has the fewest ACCEPTED COMPROMISES, not which is newest**; recency correlates with "most recently forced to trade something." ⚠ **Where recency IS right:** a **supersession** (the newer exists *because* the older was wrong — 9.30 over 9.23, L76). **Distinguish supersession from concession**: the first's note says "the older approach was wrong," the second's says "this is narrower than we wanted, because X." Mirror the first; read past the second. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: brief-writing, when naming the template to mirror. [full](LESSONS.md#105) |
| 106 | 2026-07-29 | [**A CAPABILITY IS NOT A GUARANTEE** — a correctly-typed, fail-closed signal that reaches no consumer is indistinguishable from the failure it was built to distinguish](LESSONS.md#106) | **THREE instances, three areas, one safety posture, one PRE-EXISTING and still open:** **#45** the §5 veto's `AuditSignal`→`AuditRecord` has **no persistence consumer — produced and DROPPED** (`IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md:552`, handoff `018:52`, open since before this round) · **13.8m** refusals reach nobody on the meeting path · **9.36→9.38** a distinguishable stored-row-corruption code that `boot.ts:578-592` folds into one generic `failClosedEgress` value. ⇒ **In all three the PRODUCER IS CORRECT** — right type, right fail-closed direction, green tests — **and the signal never arrives**, so a corrupt row is byte-identical to an outage, a poisoned run to a benign empty one, a vetoed egress to one that never happened: **each indistinguishable from exactly the thing it was created to distinguish.** **Why review misses it:** a producer slice's Done-when is about the producer and is genuinely met; the consumer is "someone else's slice," so its absence isn't a defect in the work under review. ⚠ **Two coincidences are a pattern; three with one already tracked and unfixed is systemic.** **The tell:** *"now distinguishable/surfaced/audited"* in a record whose slice built only the producer — nothing in a green suite contradicts it. **Do:** name the consumer or record its absence as a tracked task **in the same round**; write the scoped claim (*"distinguishable at the repository boundary; nothing surfaces it yet — task N"*), never the unqualified one (L56); ⭐ **mechanical hook — Step 7.5 already asks and accepts the wrong answer:** *"none — wiring lands in `<slice>`"* is legitimate but nothing checks `<slice>` EXISTS as a task, so require it to be a real task by round end (L51). ⚠ **Don't over-correct into blocking producer-first work** — the split is right (L11); the defect is the unqualified claim plus the untracked consumer. Kin L80. `accepted:` for the framing; the Step-7.5 hook is the checkable part. [full](LESSONS.md#106) |
| 107 | 2026-07-29 | [Red-first proves the test predates the CODE; an independently-authored cross-area enumeration proves it predates the AUTHOR](LESSONS.md#107) | 9.36 disclosed a TDD deviation (helper + both adapters before the dual-dialect tests), mitigated by mutation verification — which proves **discrimination** and **cannot touch spec-fidelity**, because a test derived from the implementation ENCODES it and no mutation OF that implementation reveals it. ⭐ **The reframe: red-first is a PROXY, not the property.** What we want is *"this test was not derived from the code under test"*; temporal ordering is one way to get it, **independent authorship is the property itself**. ⇒ a cross-area spec enumeration is a **STRONGER** guarantee of the same thing, so it can **DISCHARGE** a red-first residual rather than apologise for it. Discharged here by cross-mapping against `packages/contracts/test/models/workspace.test.ts` — which enumerates every `WorkspaceSchema` constraint class (`.strict()`, each branded/enum field, both `min(1)` strings, missing required field, **both nested aggregates' own invariants**, the top-level refine **both directions**) and is **contracts territory, authored by another area for another purpose**, so it structurally cannot derive from the worker code under test. ⚠ **Not a licence to skip red-first** — it discharges a residual after the fact when such an enumeration happens to exist; forbidden-pattern #1 still applies with the deviation recorded, residual named, remedy attached. ⭐ **"One fixture per class" was the wrong ask:** several classes are unreachable in a given context (a NOT NULL column can't present as a *missing field* in a stored row), so the artifact is a **per-class disposition** — covered (name it) / unreachable-BECAUSE / gap — and **the "because" IS the artifact, not the fixture count**. **Generalises:** whenever you need "X was not derived from Y," ask whether an independent author already produced X for their own reasons. [full](LESSONS.md#107) |
| 108 | 2026-07-29 | [What a skipped review costs is not a wrong answer — it is an UNASKED ADJACENT QUESTION](LESSONS.md#108) | 13.8m-C skipped the Step-2.5 pause (tests → RED → GREEN → mutation-verified, *then* the write-up). On review **everything checked out**: their central finding was **better than the brief's premise**, their scope reasoning sound (no speculative guards on provably-unreachable-to-fail sites, L75), and the thing the gate most needed to catch — a sibling's recorded compromise being inherited (L105) — verified in source as **not** inherited. ⇒ **So the cost was the question NEXT TO the answer:** their type-forced narrowing to two union members opens an obvious adjacent question — do the other five reach anybody, one being a workspace-isolation signal? At Step 2.5 that costs a sentence; after GREEN it's a new slice or nothing. ⛔ **Hence "already green AND correct" is no defence: correctness of the answer GIVEN is not coverage of the answers NOT SOUGHT.** **Structural reason the gate sits pre-implementation:** a design reviewed after green can only be ratified or rejected **wholesale**, so the reviewer's threshold silently rises and marginal-but-real improvements go unmade — the review still happens, it just becomes a weaker instrument. ⚠ **Distinguish REORDER-inside-the-gate (9.36: Step 2.5 still happened; lost spec-fidelity, addressable per L107) from SKIP-the-gate (lost the adjacent questions; not recoverable, only substitutable)** — do not let one's precedent cover the other. ⚠ **Trend rule:** disclosure makes a deviation **assessable**, not **acceptable**; two in one round is a trend and a third is a finding. `accepted:` — enforcement point: the reviewer, when a Step-2.5 write-up describes completed work. [full](LESSONS.md#108) |
| 109 | 2026-07-29 | [A check chained into the action it guards is not a gate — it prints, it cannot stop](LESSONS.md#109) | Committing an `ARCHITECTURE.md` note, all three shared-tree steps ran — and **14 of another implementer's already-staged in-flight files landed inside a `docs(arch)` commit.** ⭐ **The pre-check WORKED**: it printed the dirty index correctly. It could not **act**, because it sat in the same shell invocation as the commit — `&&` chains **add→commit**, nothing chains **check→add** — and step 3 (`git show --stat` after) reports the damage **as history**, the form in which it is most expensive to fix. ⇒ **A check whose output you read only AFTER the action is a receipt, not a gate.** ⚠ Same defect as the earlier "an assertion that aborts a step but not the pipeline is a warning, not a gate," which survived being banked because it was read as being about failed *edits* rather than about *checkpoint placement* — **recording a hazard does not immunize you against its other shape.** ⛔ **FIX IS STRUCTURAL, L103 applied to the commit itself: `git commit -F <msg> -- <paths>`** commits only those paths **regardless of what else is staged**, making the foreign-file sweep **unrepresentable** rather than detected — no ordering or output-reading required. **In a shared checkout, pathspec-limit every commit** (already recorded once as a mitigation and never adopted as the default; adopting it is the remedy). Fallbacks: pre-check in a **separate invocation**, or fail-closed inline (`[ -z "$(git diff --cached --name-only)" ] && git add … && git commit …`) so a dirty index **aborts** rather than **narrates**. **Repair (non-destructive):** `git reset --soft HEAD~1` (working tree untouched) → `git restore --staged <foreign paths>` (index only) → re-commit pathspec-limited; then **have the files' owner verify their own diff**, since a silent partial is what this class produces. ⚠ **Zsh footgun:** `git restore --staged $PATHS` failed because **zsh doesn't word-split unquoted expansions** — list paths explicitly. [full](LESSONS.md#109) |
| 110 | 2026-07-29 | [A MITIGATION RECORDED AS AN OPTION IS NOT A MITIGATION — make it the default in the same edit that records it](LESSONS.md#110) | `git commit -- <paths>` (pathspec-limiting, which makes a foreign staged file **structurally unable** to enter a commit) **was already in this project's record** — correctly, as one available option. **It had never been adopted as the default.** Then an orchestrator commit swept 14 of another implementer's staged in-flight files into a `docs(arch)` commit (L109), a failure that mitigation would have made impossible. ⇒ **The remedy was in the record, correct, and INERT** — filed as *a thing you could do* rather than *the thing we do*. ⭐ **Three distinct L89 shapes in one round:** recorded-but-**UNBELIEVED** (`lint`-is-typecheck; a whole round still said "lint clean") · recorded-but-**OTHER-SHAPE** ("an assertion that aborts a step but not the pipeline is a warning, not a gate" — filed as being about failed *edits*, so it could not prevent its sibling about *checkpoint placement*) · recorded-but-**NEVER-ADOPTED** (this). **Do:** when routing a mitigation at Step 9, ask *"is this the right default?"* — if yes, **change the default in the same edit** (the convention, the template, the command you type), not only the lesson; if *"it depends,"* say **on what**, or it reads as optional forever. ⚠ **General form, and it is uncomfortable: a record's job is not to be TRUE, it is to change what happens BY DEFAULT.** A correct entry that leaves the default untouched buys only the ability to say it was known — worse than not knowing, since it converts a surprise into a foreseeable omission. ⭐ **Corollary:** structural mitigations (a pathspec, a closed literal, an absent param, a required field) are nearly free to adopt as defaults — L103 applied to **process** — while behavioural ones ("remember to check X") are expensive and unreliable, which is itself an argument for finding a structural form first. `accepted:` — enforcement point: the moment a `pin:`/`pattern:`/`accepted:` line is written. **An enforcement line naming an OPTION rather than a DEFAULT is this defect.** [full](LESSONS.md#110) |
| 111 | 2026-07-29 | [A RETRACTION IS A CLAIM — and an INTERMITTENT failure is the perfect trap for a premature one](LESSONS.md#111) | ⚠ **This row replaced an earlier one that stated the opposite conclusion; the original instance was false and is corrected in place.** An implementer reported root `pnpm lint` failing; I relayed it; the lead ran it, got 11/11, and retracted; **I "verified" the retraction and the retraction was WRONG.** Established (unpiped, two operators): `package.json:12` **is** `"lint": "turbo run lint"`, **but `pnpm lint` fails INTERMITTENTLY before turbo starts** (`ERR_PNPM_RECURSIVE_EXEC_FIRST_FAIL` / `Command "eslint" not found`) — reproduced at **exit 1**, and **my own first run was byte-identical**; while `pnpm --filter . run lint` and `npx turbo run lint` succeed 11/11 in the same shell. ⛔ **How I got it wrong: I dismissed real evidence because it disagreed with a conclusion I'd just formed** — run 1 failed, run 2 passed, and I called run 1 a tooling artifact. ⭐ **An intermittent failure is the ideal trap, because a second run genuinely does "disprove" the first and the disproof is worthless: two runs disagreeing is evidence of NON-DETERMINISM, never that the failing run was fictitious. "Failed, then passed" reads FLAKY, never PASSING.** ⭐ **Separate RAISING (correct — that is what escalation is FOR; sitting on an unsubstantiated doubt is strictly worse) from CORROBORATING (the defect — it converts "please check" into "established" and spends the verifier's budget on a false premise).** ⛔ **The pipe trap caught BOTH investigators in fifteen minutes** — I printed `tail`'s status as `pnpm`'s; the lead's `PIPESTATUS[0]` printed **empty** and was noted as imprecision. They got a defensible answer only via a second independent signal ⇒ **redundancy separated the outcomes, not care.** An empty exit code is a **failed measurement**, not a minor imprecision. ⭐ **Durable fix is EVIDENCE CLASS (L103 applied to evidence):** the finding that mattered — lint means typecheck, nothing more — rests on **script inspection** (11 scripts all `tsc --noEmit`, zero ESLint, no `format:check`). **An inspectable fact cannot be re-opened by a flaky execution; an exit code can.** Prefer inspectable over executed evidence for anything durable. ⚠ **Meta:** a round spent on over-claims trains a suspicion reflex that is an over-claim generator pointed the other way — it fired twice here, once inward as over-harsh self-criticism the lead rejected, once as this retraction. [full](LESSONS.md#111) | `package.json:12` is `"lint": "turbo run lint"` (so `pnpm lint` **IS** `turbo run lint`, it delegates rather than shadows); `pnpm lint` → 11/11, **EXIT=0**; identical via `npx turbo run lint`; **all 11 packages' `lint` is literally `tsc --noEmit`** and **`eslint` is in ZERO workspace manifests**. The genuine finding (no ESLint, no `format:check` ⇒ lint means typecheck) was never in question — **the DOUBT was the error.** ⛔ **And I "corroborated" it wrongly via two measurement failures in one command:** I piped through `tail` and printed `$?`, so I reported **`tail`'s** exit status, never measuring the thing I was reporting on; and the alarming `Command "eslint" not found` text is **unreproducible** and cannot have come from a workspace script — an artifact of my own tooling that I believed **because it agreed with a doubt I already held.** ⭐ **Why doubt is the worse direction: a false CLAIM is specific and therefore checkable, so someone kills it; a false DOUBT needs nothing specific, is unfalsifiable as stated, and lingers. A false green gets fixed — a false doubt makes people stop trusting a gate that WORKS**, and a distrusted-but-working gate is worse than a known-broken one because it gets bypassed *and* never replaced. **Do:** hold a doubt to a claim's evidentiary standard (command + output + real exit code, or don't raise it); **never report an exit code obtained through a pipe** (use redirection or `PIPESTATUS`); **beware agreement** — confirmation is the weakest evidence and feels like the strongest; and retracting needs the same verification as asserting, **including retracting someone else's retraction**, which is how this was caught. ⚠ **Meta:** a round spent on over-claims trains a reflex toward suspicion, and **that reflex is an over-claim generator pointed the other way** — skepticism has a false-positive rate, and its false positives destroy working machinery. [full](LESSONS.md#111) |
| 112 | 2026-07-29 | [An IN-FLIGHT message is indistinguishable from an ABSENT one at the observation point — a stall check detects a WAITING STATE, not its CAUSE](LESSONS.md#112) | A three-way wait was diagnosed from three signals read together (task `in_progress` + owner idle ~4min + uncommitted files ~6min stale) and the conclusion *"the relay never came"* was **wrong** — the relay was **in flight while the measurement was taken.** ⇒ **The signals cannot separate "blocked" from "resolution in transit," because the distinguishing fact lives in an unobservable channel** ⇒ **an IRREDUCIBLE false-positive rate proportional to message latency, which NO additional signal fixes** (a fourth observable doesn't help when the missing fact is unobservable in principle from that vantage point). **Honest form:** it reliably detects a **waiting state** (owner + idle + mtime together beats reading the quiet, and beats each alone); it **cannot** diagnose the **cause**; ⇒ **it warrants a QUESTION, never an ACCUSATION** — *"is anyone blocked on me?"* costs one message and is right either way. ⚠ **The more instructive half is the ledger error:** this was logged as *"the first time the check fired on a REAL stall"* after two prior false positives — **it was the third.** ⇒ **A monitoring instrument's own success ledger needs the same verification as any other claim (L111), and is especially prone to inflation because the operator both raises the alarm and grades it. A check that grades its own hit rate will report a better one than it has.** **Generalises:** any observation-point check on a distributed system — a queued job, an unflushed buffer, a request in the network. **"I cannot see it" and "it does not exist" are the same observation and different facts.** Kin L100, L91. `accepted:` — enforcement point: the wording of the alarm, and verifying the outcome before crediting the check. [full](LESSONS.md#112) |
| 113 | 2026-07-29 | [A lead's RULINGS need the same adversarial channel its CLAIMS have — the person who cannot audit a decision is the person who made it](LESSONS.md#113) | The round produced **ten overturns** of a further reader by a closer one. **Nine covered CLAIMS; exactly ONE covered JUDGEMENT — and only because the decider asked.** **Why:** claim-overturns were cheap because every brief cited a falsifiable `file:line`, so anyone with the file open could contradict it in one command. ⛔ **A decision has no analogue** — a ruling (*hold X · no 4th pass · Option A · not this round*) cites **reasoning**, not a checkable premise, so there is nothing to grep, and the only party positioned to price it is **whoever bore its cost** — structurally not the one who chose. ⭐ **THE PERSON WHO CANNOT AUDIT A DECISION IS THE PERSON WHO MADE IT, AND THE ONLY CORRECTION AVAILABLE COMES FROM WHOEVER BORE ITS COST.** A hold's cost is invisible to whoever imposed it; so is a deferral's, a scope cut's, an ordering's. **Instance:** contract held idle >1h; asked *specifically* whether the hold was WRONG, they **read `be62e348`'s diff** instead of agreeing and found **zero `packages/contracts/**` files** — the real contention surface was `packages/db/src/repositories/interfaces.ts`, one layer down. **The hold was right for a MORE PRECISE reason than the one given** — sharpened, not ratified. ⚠ **And it only happened because the decider asked: a low correction rate on judgement is not evidence judgement is better, it is evidence nobody is checking.** **Do:** ask the party who bore a ruling's cost to price it **at close-out**; ⛔ ask the **SPECIFIC** question — *"anything unfiled?"* returned *"nothing unfiled"*, while *"was this hold wrong, was the collision real, did you form a view you didn't state?"* returned a diff read (**the generic prompt is a different question, and it reliably returns nothing**); record the answer as a **priced** decision, including *"right, for this narrower reason,"* which is the common outcome. ⚠ **Limit:** not "distrust every ruling" — a decider who re-litigates every call stops deciding. **One bounded question, once, at a boundary, to the party who paid.** Kin: the reader-with-the-file-open asymmetry pointed at **decisions** not **claims**; L100's fourth mechanism in a process decision; L112's self-grading instrument. `accepted:` — enforcement point: `/orchestrate-end` + the escalation taxonomy; **a hold or deferral should carry a price-it-back step the way a Step-9 flag carries a routing destination.** [full](LESSONS.md#113) |
| 114 | 2026-07-30 | [A SAFE DEFAULT returned with a SUCCESS FLAG destroys the caller's ability to distinguish causes — the info is gone AT THAT FRAME, not unlogged downstream](LESSONS.md#114) | `egressStatus` (`apps/worker/src/boot.ts`) did `if (!isOk(got)) return {ok:true, value: failClosedEgress(wsId)}` with **no inspection of `got.error.code`**, and folded a thrown outage to the same value — so 9.36's freshly-minted, deliberately-distinguishable `stored_row_schema_violation` was **byte-identical to `not_found` and to an outage**, and because the frame reported **success** the caller could not tell either. ⛔ **NOT [L106](LESSONS.md#106), and the difference dictates the remedy:** L106 is *produced-and-dropped* (signal exists, no consumer ⇒ **add a consumer**); this is *never-produced* (the distinction dies at the frame ⇒ **no consumer can be added later**). ⚠ **And you cannot "widen the error path" — there is no error path**; you must ADD a channel. ⭐ **The trap: the safe default is CORRECT, so the code reads as careful — and is, about the POSTURE. The carelessness is about OBSERVABILITY, and the safe default is exactly what makes the loss invisible** (no failing test, no error log, no unhappy path). **Correct-and-safe ≠ observable.** ⛔ **Wrong fix:** buying visibility by weakening the default. The fix is **orthogonal** — keep the return byte-identical on every branch and mint the distinction BESIDE it (9.38 pins byte-identical across absent/corrupt/outage so the trade is unrepresentable, not merely discouraged). ⚠ **Residual honesty:** adding the channel for one cause does not restore the others — an outage still mints nothing, so absence-of-signal still conflates *healthy* with *outage*; declined for a real reason (no reliable outage signal without risking a FALSE corruption report), not a scheduling one. [full](LESSONS.md#114) | `pattern:` warn-grep `catch`/`!isOk` branches returning `ok: true` in anything serving a posture/status/projection; a hit is not automatically wrong but **must carry an in-code note naming which causes it collapses and why that is acceptable.** |

| 115 | 2026-07-31 | [A TOTAL function over `unknown` costs nothing at the definition site and is paid back at a call site its author could not have known would exist](LESSONS.md#115) | Extends **L103**. 13.8g-A shipped `normalizeAttendees(raw: unknown)` TOTAL/never-throws for an imagined raw-string-array caller. That caller **never existed**; a round later the real one was `ValidatedExtraction.fields["attendees"]?.value` — string \| array \| `TBD` sentinel \| absent, **four shapes, none the intended one** — and the wiring needed **zero new guards**. Had it been typed `readonly string[]`, the corrected path would have needed a shape probe + sentinel check + coercion + a per-failure decision: **four branches, each a chance to get the fail-safe direction backwards**, written by someone without the function's invariants in their head. ⇒ **ECONOMICS, not taste:** definition-site cost is one `Array.isArray` + an empty constant; call-site saving is a branch per shape, paid by someone who doesn't hold the invariants. ⚠ **Limit — do NOT type everything `unknown`:** applies only at a **trust boundary** (parser/normalizer/gate/adapter over content the process didn't author). Inside a boundary it erases a real guarantee (L80). **Test: would a wrong shape be the CALLER'S BUG or a FACT ABOUT THE WORLD?** Bug ⇒ type it precisely; world ⇒ `unknown` + total. ⭐ Banked as **evidence not principle** — the payoff was collected by a different person, a round later, on an unimagined call site, which is exactly the payoff shape that never appears in the slice paying the cost and therefore gets argued away. [full](LESSONS.md#115) | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 2.5, on any new function taking external/untrusted/not-yet-shaped input. |

| 116 | 2026-07-31 | [In a hub-and-spoke team, authority attaches to the SPEAKER not the claim — a relay arrives wearing the relayer's confidence, not its source's](LESSONS.md#116) | Complements **L113** (which says a ruling needs a challenge channel; this says you must first tell a ruling FROM a relay). Two same-day instances, both lead-self-reported: a stale tracker line restated as fact (nearly briefed as an unchallengeable ruling), and an orchestrator's own UNVERIFIED aside restated as an instruction (*"no built precedent"* — two thirds of it was already built; carrying it would have had an implementer rebuild working machinery). **Structural, not diligence:** every fact reaches the spokes through one node, and confidence is attached by the LAST speaker rather than carried from the origin — a 60%-confidence doc line and a 30%-confidence aside both arrive at 100%, indistinguishable on the wire. ⭐ **STANDING RULE:** a lead restating a document or a teammate is a **POINTER, not a ruling** — weight it as its ORIGINAL source deserved. **A real ruling says so and carries a REASON; the missing reason is the tell, because a relay has nothing to give one from.** ⚠ **Asymmetric:** a relayed false CLAIM gets checked and dies cheap; a relayed false DOUBT makes someone rebuild working machinery and is unfalsifiable as stated (**L111** at the coordination layer). ⇒ relayer marks a relay as a relay (one clause); recipient verifies a relayed premise like a brief premise (**L81**) and **reports back**, since the relayer holds the same wrong picture. ⭐⭐ **AMENDED 2026-08-12 — THE OPERATIONAL HALF, previously missing: L116 said authority attaches to the speaker but never how a RECIPIENT DISCRIMINATES, and the "a real ruling carries a REASON" tell FAILS when the plan prose already contains the reason.** `24.44`'s no-red-window Done-when was dispatched as *"the lead's"* while existing **verbatim in the tracker** ⇒ a fresh ruling and plan-text-wearing-lead-authority were indistinguishable, **and they are different facts** (one re-assigns the implementer and supersedes three standing instructions; the other means nobody moved them). ⭐ **DISCRIMINATOR: CITE THE PART OF THE RULING THE PROSE CANNOT CONTAIN — here the ASSIGNMENT** (the lead named the pair, the file, and the line). **A doc can state a requirement, a rationale, a Done-when, even a sequencing constraint; it cannot assign a teammate to a half at a moment. Prose can be quoted — a dispatch cannot be forged from it.** ⇒ **relayer cites the NON-overlapping part as evidence** (⚠ if every part also exists in the doc, that IS the finding — you are relaying); **recipient asks *"is there anything here the tracker could not have said on its own?"*** ⭐ Found by an IMPLEMENTER refusing to start — the furthest spoke, where the diagnostic form was least usable. [full](LESSONS.md#116) | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: any message that becomes a brief premise or a dispatch decision. |

| 117 | 2026-07-31 | [After a session death run TWO checks — a diff is structurally blind to untracked files, and that file is the only one with a real loss vector](LESSONS.md#117) | A 529 killed a turn mid-safety-slice. Recovery reported *"369 insertions across 4 files, all present"* — arithmetic exact, inventory structurally incomplete: a **fifth, UNTRACKED** 152-line file held the slice's highest-value test. ⇒ **Always run BOTH `git diff --stat` (tracked mods) AND `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` (what the diff cannot see), then STAGE the untracked work** — a tracked change survives almost anything; an untracked file is one `clean`/`checkout` from gone, so it is the *only* real loss vector. ⭐ **Reporting defect, self-diagnosed:** *"I ran two checks and reported one"* — the summary merged a 4-file diff with a 5-file existence check into one number, **and the number is what travels**; a summary silently spanning two scopes carries the authority of both while covering neither (**L100** relocated from a search into a status report). ⭐⭐ **Deepest form, reusable far past git:** *"I checked PRESENCE and reported SAFETY — not the same property."* A service that responds is not healthy; a row that exists is not valid; a test that runs does not discriminate. **When you report reassurance, name the property you actually measured.** Companion to **L83**. | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: any crash/teardown recovery, and any message asserting someone else's in-flight work survived. |

| 118 | 2026-07-31 | [⭐ THE FAMILY: a PROXY standing in for the PROPERTY — the round's unifying defect, banked as a family](LESSONS.md#118) | ⛔ An INDEX, not a new rule. **Shape:** you need property **P** (safe? done? necessary? covered?); **P** is expensive; proxy **Q** is cheap (exists? how many lines? where is it usually done? exit 0?). **Q correlates with P almost always — which is why substituting it is invisible** — and it fails exactly where the correlation breaks, which is disproportionately the interesting case. **Members:** presence→safety (**L117**) · lines-changed→progress (a wake message inferred "Step 3/4" from `git diff --stat`; it was at Step 8) · conventional-location→necessary-location (reviewer dispatch treated as bound to the implementer's session; it needs only the diff + brief — *this one cost an OPTION, not a wrong answer*) · a-gate-that-runs→a-gate-that-checks (**L89**) · a-test-that-runs→a-test-that-discriminates (**L75/L84/L90**) · the-tracker-says-X→X-is-true ((a0)(viii)) · a-summary→what-it-summarises (the Phase-25 track column). ⇒ **DETECTION, the only transferable part: NAME THE PROPERTY YOU MEASURED, THEN ASK IF IT IS THE ONE YOU CARE ABOUT.** Every check above was *correct*; the proxy answers a different question than the one reported. **Tell:** the verb of measurement and the noun of the claim disagree — *"I stat'd the files, so the work is safe."* ⚠ **NOT "distrust proxies"** — measuring P directly is often impossible. **The rule is REPORT THE PROXY, NOT THE PROPERTY:** a stated proxy invites the reader to notice the gap; a proxy reported as the property closes the question. ⭐ A family because the remedies share nothing (a git command · a rename · mutation · a bounded sweep) — **only the noticing is shared.** | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — necessarily; a check for this would itself be a proxy. Enforcement point: any sentence reporting reassurance, status, or coverage. |
| 119 | 2026-07-31 | [A schema-declared per-field property needs a correspondence pin in the CONSUMER's own suite — not a locally re-derived belief that happens to agree](LESSONS.md#119) | 13.8g-C leg C: leg A declared `attendees`/`decisions` list-valued in `packages/contracts`; `packages/knowledge`'s `normalizeAttendees` had independently assumed array input, correctly — but nothing tied the two together. ⭐ **The ruling's own words: "list-ness has exactly one source... no consumer may hand-assume... that divergence IS the bug."** Fix: import the SAME declared constant into the CONSUMER's suite and assert membership there — the declaring package's own test pins the declaration's shape, not any consumer's agreement with it. Distinct from L39/L61 (parsers) and L5/L37/L88 (literals that must vs. must-not agree): this is a declared per-field TRAIT consumed by N independent modules, each needing its own tripwire against the one shared declaration. `pin: packages/knowledge/test/attendee-refs.test.ts::attendees_is_declared_list_valued_in_the_shared_schema` (mutation-verified via a swapped checked literal, never the real array — a different track's territory) · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable beyond instances pinned` (enforcement point: any slice reading a shared declaration asks whether its OWN suite pins the correspondence). [full](LESSONS.md#119) |
| 120 | 2026-07-31 | [A fact about our STAFFING is not a fact about what is PERMITTED — and a weak reason bundled with a strong one gets LAUNDERED](LESSONS.md#120) | Authorizing a cross-area fixture fix, the orchestrator gave a sound reason (**L121**'s compile-break argument) **and** *"no eval-security-implementer is live this session to route it to regardless."* **The fact is true; the inference is invalid — availability is not authority.** Nobody to route to does not grant permission; it removes the reviewer, the **opposite**. ⭐ **Family: a staffing fact offered as a permission fact** — second member is *"an area is idle"* offered as a reason to delete a specified affordance (`9.40`, already ruled). ⚠ The author had read that ruling **hours earlier** and reproduced it anyway (**L91/L94**: knowing a failure mode does not make you notice it in your own reasoning). ⭐ **THE NEW HALF: a weak reason bundled with a strong one gets LAUNDERED** — the reader who accepts the strong reason **inherits the weak one silently**, pre-endorsed and never argued on its own; **the strong reason is what makes the weak one dangerous**, since alone it would have been challenged. ⚠ **Tell, available before anyone else reads it: the weak reason was doing no work** — the sound argument already sufficed, and **a surplus reason is there because the author wanted more support than they had**, which is exactly when it is the unsound one. ⇒ **Writing: ship ONE reason** — if an addition would not survive alone, deleting it makes the decision stronger. **Reading: evaluate each reason separately; accepting a conclusion is not accepting the bundle** — say which reason you accepted so the other does not travel on your endorsement. | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: any authorization/ruling/justification carrying more than one reason; check each alone. |
| 121 | 2026-07-31 | [A shared-type widening's own compile-break in another area is part of the CHANGE, not a CROSSING — and the test is whether you can state it that way](LESSONS.md#121) | **LEAD DECISION**, ruled after challenge. **Narrow form, verbatim:** *"a shared-type widening's own compile-break in another area's fixture is part of the change, not a crossing — one line, assertion-preserving, its own commit."* ⛔ **Boundary of the boundary (the load-bearing half — the narrow form alone reads as a licence):** covers a compile-break **caused by your own change**; NOT editing another area's tests to agree with you, NOT changing an assertion, NOT anything that would still be wanted if the compile were fine. ⇒ **TEST: if you cannot state it as "my change broke this and it cannot land red," it IS a crossing and it comes to the lead.** ⭐ **The boundary was avoidable by making the new field `optional` — REJECTED:** an optional *produced* field makes "an adapter that omitted it" indistinguishable from "one that correctly had nothing" (**L106**), i.e. precisely the `receipt.plans` silent drop 13.8f-C exists to prevent ⇒ **optional-to-dodge-a-boundary trades a real invariant for process convenience** ((a0)(iii)'s ruling + the container pre-ruling in a third costume). Evidence the implementer reasoned rather than defaulted: the sibling fault field (`meetingVaultRewriteFault`, renamed from `livingVaultRewriteFault` at the same slice's Step 9) was made optional in the same edit, correctly. **Mechanics (all three or it is a silent touch):** own commit, pathspec-limited · message naming the upstream slice + *"mechanical, no `<area>` logic touched"* · scope held to the compile fix. ⛔ **Provenance recorded because the process failure outranks the ruling: the orchestrator authorized unilaterally and was challenged; a first-of-its-kind interpretation of a standing rule must arrive as a REQUEST.** ⚠ The lead's suspected mechanism was wrong (an inherited slice-scoped owner exception — never held); the real defect came from the orchestrator self-reporting (**L120**). | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 8's full-graph typecheck (**L81**) is where these surface. ⚠ Discoverability gap stated in-lesson: an implementer hits this mid-slice reading its OWN area's CLAUDE.md, not this file; a root `CLAUDE.md` line may be owed, deliberately not written unilaterally. ⛔⛔ **AMENDED 2026-08-18, THEN CORRECTED THE SAME DAY (LEAD) — ~~the compile fix must land AFTER the change that requires it~~ WAS THE WRONG REMEDY: ⭐ A FORCED CONSEQUENCE RIDES WITH ITS CAUSE — SAME-COMMIT IS MANDATORY FOR A COMPILE DEPENDENCY, because separation is what CREATES the ordering bug. Attribution is a MESSAGE problem and is always solvable there.** A fixture supplying a now-required field, committed FIRST, is an excess-property error against the un-widened type ⇒ **it INVERTS the compile break instead of closing it and leaves the target package RED AT HEAD.** ⚠ Happened live: the crossing landed at `ca8dc207` while the shape change sat uncommitted, so the author's `tsc --noEmit` 0-errors was TRUE OF THEIR TREE and FALSE OF HEAD (`L193`/`L98`). ⭐ **The orchestrator's mechanics bought ATTRIBUTION and sold ATOMICITY, and only attribution was ever at risk.** ⭐⭐ **The same lead had ruled TWO HOURS EARLIER (`### 24.93`) that a tracker edit must land in the SAME COMMIT as the code that expires it — so the opposite mechanics on a compile dependency produced exactly the failure the first ruling prevented. Two careful people, opposite mechanics, two hours apart ⇒ the rule was UNDER-SPECIFIED, not a person careless.** ⚠ **Scale: red at HEAD across THREE commits, not a moment.** |
| 122 | 2026-07-31 | [AN UNRESOLVED PREMISE IS NOT WRITTEN AS A PREMISE — a doubt filed as a question does not weaken the assertion it doubts](LESSONS.md#122) | **TWO INSTANCES, one shape: the information that would have prevented the error was in hand or in the ledger, and the brief was written past it.** **(1)** Brief 241 asserted *"the sink instance ALREADY EXISTS at boot (`boot.ts:1756`)"* **and in the same document asked** *"is `boot.ts:1756` inside a CONDITIONAL scope? Its indentation suggests a nested block."* ⛔ **The Step-2.5 question IS the proof the author knew the premise was unestablished.** Reality: that line is inside a **lazy** `agentSynthesisFactory` body, defined only when `copilotRealModel && copilotAgentMode`, run per-ask — **no boot-time instance, and a different feature.** ⭐ **Why filing the doubt did not save it: THE CHANNELS ARE NOT EQUAL** — an implementer **builds from the premises** and reads Step-2.5 questions as **review scaffolding**, so a brief asserting X while asking *"is X true?"* **ships X.** ⇒ **resolve it before the brief, or write the premise AS the open question** (*"UNVERIFIED — establish before Step 1"*); **a premise and a question about that premise cannot coexist in one brief.** ⚠ **NOT L118** (that is measuring the wrong property; here the measurement was correct and *known incomplete*) — nearest relative is **L94**, but worse: a correction at least knows it is fixing something. **(2)** ⛔ **L59 was literally the answer and the brief was written past it** — *"when the consumer is workflow-SANDBOX code the arming gate belongs in the ACTIVITY; sandbox code cannot read boot config"* — **banked from 13.8d, the task 13.8i-B names as its dependency.** ⇒ **a brief that names a dependency INHERITS that dependency's lessons.** ⭐ Caught by the brief's own hard-line guard, which was written for a PERMISSION hazard and caught a PREMISE defect — *pre-rule the principle, not the instance*, second instance this round — and cheap because it fired **before Step 1**. | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` in full; **two cheap dispatch-time halves:** (1) a brief must not ask a Step-2.5 question about premise X while asserting X — the author's own question is the detector; (2) read the banked lessons of every task in `Depends:` before dispatch. **Enforcement point: brief authoring, before the spec-lint stamp — not review.** |
| 123 | 2026-07-31 | ["MUST" IS PROSE, NOT A GATE — an optional guard is type-indistinguishable from a bound one at the call site](LESSONS.md#123) | **Banked at the lead's instruction with the phrase verbatim — *"the whole audit in five words."*** 24.6 finding F1 → task 24.9. `local-embed.ts` enforces its rule-5 floor from a caller-**self-declared** `backend.egressClass`, while the mechanism that would PROVE it (`egressGate?:`, the real endpoint-proof `egressVeto`) **is optional** — and the module's own header says a remote backend **"MUST bind `egressGate`."** ⛔ **That sentence is the entire enforcement. It is a comment.** ⭐ **Why worse than an ABSENT guard: `f(x,{backend})` and `f(x,{backend,egressGate})` BOTH compile, both pass review, both look complete** — an absent guard is a visible gap someone must close; an optional one **satisfies the compiler while omitting the proof** (L106's capability-not-guarantee aimed at a guard, not a signal; same instinct as L2/L28's strict `=== true` one level down). ⚠ **Severity stated honestly or it gets dismissed:** zero production callers, gate bound in tests only, sole backend is the eval harness's recorded zero-egress one ⇒ **NOT a live breach — a live arming PRECONDITION with no enforcement.** ⛔ **And that is the wrong reason to close it** — the audit assumes the gates are open, and the slice that makes retrieval real is the one that will satisfy the type and skip the proof. ⛔ **NEVER close such a finding by citing the comment as coverage** — that is the asserted-constant mechanism living in a comment. ⭐ **Generalisation: a comment stating an obligation is evidence someone KNEW, never evidence anything ENFORCES.** Ask of every *"MUST"* / *"callers must"*: **what fails if they don't?** *"Nothing, until an incident"* ⇒ undefended. ⇒ make the guard non-optional for anything not proven safe, or the unproven path unrepresentable (L87/L103); if it must stay optional (L11 fakes), the obligation needs a **named mechanical backstop** and the comment must say which. | `pattern: grep -n 'egressGate' packages/knowledge/src/gbrain/local-embed.ts` — an **optional guard on a rule-touching seam is a finding until something enforces it.** Enforcement point: any review of a seam whose safety depends on a caller doing what the type does not require. |
| 124 | 2026-08-11 | [A DEBT'S RECORDED SCOPE IS NOT A SPECIFICATION — paying it as written can be the defect](LESSONS.md#124) | **Carry-forward item 11**: an acknowledged-but-unwritten orchestrator hot-write, absence confirmed by measurement in **both** files, working tree **and** history — **sound evidence, wrong prescription.** It asked for an `ARCHITECTURE.md` Appendix-A row **and** a `packages/contracts/CLAUDE.md` cross-doc row for `UiSafeAuditDrillSummary`. ⛔ **That table is the 29 FROZEN SEAMS** (Zod-source → generated schema → frozen `.snap` → ajv registry); **a UI-safe projection has none of those**, and the cited precedent proves it — **`UiSafeCopilotAnswer` has an Appendix-A row and is ABSENT from that table, and its absence IS the pattern.** ⇒ writing the row as recorded would have **asserted a freeze that does not exist** and put the *"29 frozen"* count in doubt — a false safety claim in the table that exists to prevent silent disagreement, i.e. **24.6's own defect class, minted while paying down 24.6-adjacent debt.** ⭐ **Why it was invited: the item cited a precedent BY NAME** (*"the `UiSafeCopilotAnswer` pattern"*) **instead of by observable content** — a named pattern compresses to whatever the reader already believes; the writer meant *projection-not-frozen-seam* and it reads as *do for X what was done for Y*, **and what was done for Y was one row, not two.** ⇒ **cite a precedent by what it DID and the file it did it in, never by a name for what it did.** ⭐ **Honest disposition: the residual gap was real but DIFFERENT** — that file indexed **no** UI-safe projection at all, so the fix is a scoped companion note (covering `UiSafeCopilotAnswer` too) keeping the orchestrator-write obligation intact. **A wrong prescription over a real symptom is still a real symptom.** ⚠ This is **(a0)(viii) face 2** — *the tracker wrong about WHAT* — in a **Carry-forward item** rather than a task. | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — no gate distinguishes a debt paid correctly from one paid as literally written. **Enforcement point: re-derive a debt's target from the DESTINATION artifact's own scoping note before paying it** — the debt proves something is missing, never what belongs there. |
| 125 | 2026-08-11 | [A REMEDIATION LEDGER IS A CLAIM LIKE ANY OTHER — "amended" counted as "corrected"](LESSONS.md#125) | **Banked at the lead's instruction** — 24.6 round 2 / DOC-1 → task 24.10. `material-direction.md:92`'s normative paragraph **opened** with *"the egress pill … was removed"* and **in the same sentence** enumerated it among signals that *"stay first-class and visible."* Lines 58/84 struck it correctly ⇒ **the file contradicted itself three ways and the stale copy sat in the NORMATIVE paragraph.** ⛔ **The lesson is that the fix ledger recorded it DONE:** `Residuals (9)` claimed *"material-direction.md ×3,"* counting *"its guardrail amended"* — **it WAS amended and was NOT corrected.** ⭐ **Why this survives a re-read, and why it is the DANGEROUS form: the surrounding prose CHANGED, so fresh edit-marks read as a completed fix.** An untouched paragraph looks suspicious; a half-edited one does not ⇒ **partial remediation is harder to detect than none.** ⛔ **Compounding factor is the LOCATION — a completion record standing over an incomplete fix, inside the remediation ledger of the very defect it remediates** (L106 inverted: *a remediation is not automatically a fix*). ⚠ **It also survived a GREP**, because the paragraph's other sentences carry the correction — **the check that found it was READING THE PARAGRAPH** (L64's shape). ⇒ **a sweep's `<file> ×N` counts sites TOUCHED, never sites CORRECTED — name the sites, not the number** (L100: state the unit); **verify a doc fix by re-reading the enclosing paragraph, never by re-running the grep that found it** — its hits now include your own correction. **A dated amendment marker is evidence someone LOOKED, never that they FINISHED.** | `pattern: grep -n 'egress pill' docs/design/ui-ux/material-direction.md` — every surviving hit must sit inside a strike-through or an explicit do-not-re-add line. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any sweep close-out reporting a per-file count.** |
| 126 | 2026-08-11 | [A BANKED LESSON RECORDS THAT A DEFECT *WAS* FIXED, NEVER THAT IT *IS* FIXED — re-derive the negative from current source](LESSONS.md#126) | **Banked at the lead's instruction** — 24.6 round 2 / `AC-2b`; called *"the single best methodological call of the round,"* because ⭐ **a negative at risk of being ASSUMED is worth more than another finding.** `knowledge/src/synthesis/*` carried five lessons recording heavy prior fixing of one class (L32/L37/L38/L60/L65) and the tracker warned *"do NOT treat past fixes as present coverage"* — **AC-2b re-derived it from current code and reported the live mechanisms** (the prototype-pollution `Map`; `admitGroundedPath` as sole chokepoint with all four routes closed **in the code, not the comments**). ⛔ **MECHANISM: a CITATION is evidence about the PAST; COVERAGE is a claim about the PRESENT.** Between fix and now sit refactors, container swaps and new call sites — this repo watched one `.catchall()` swap silently drop **three** guards a green suite had covered for months ⇒ **the lesson proves someone once closed it; nothing in the citation observes it is still closed** (L118's PROXY-for-PROPERTY, aimed at our own remediation history). ⭐ **WHY DANGEROUS, not merely loose: an assumed negative and a verified one produce BYTE-IDENTICAL REPORTS** — *"Sound — see L37"* and *"Sound — sole chokepoint, four routes closed, read at HEAD"* carry equal weight and **only one would notice a regression** ⇒ the audit's own explicit-negatives rule one level up. ⚠ **NOT "distrust the ledger"** — the lessons were right when written; the challenged claim is not *was this fixed* but *is it still*, and only the second is what an audit / phase-exit / arming gate needs. ⇒ **when a lesson would be your evidence, open the file and cite `file:line` at HEAD; cite the lesson beside it as PROVENANCE, never proof. A lesson is a SEARCH KEY, not a result.** | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — no gate distinguishes a cited negative from a derived one. **Enforcement point: any audit / `/phase-exit` / arming gate whose "sound" verdict rests on a lesson citation rather than a line of current source.** |
| 127 | 2026-08-11 | [A SCOPE CONSTRAINT AND A SCOPE EXPANSION IN ONE MESSAGE ARE INVISIBLE TO THEIR AUTHOR — each half is individually correct](LESSONS.md#127) | **Self-reported by the lead** after the orchestrator declined to act on it; **sibling of L125** — L125 is a partial FIX reading as complete, this is a partial CONSTRAINT reading as whole. One message carried *"THE AUTHORIZATION IS THIS ONE EDIT… any further `docs/design/**` edit needs a fresh gate"* **and** *"take 9.42's sibling obligation on the same file in the same pass"* — ⭐ **the second authorises a second edit to the same locked file the first forbids.** ⛔ **MECHANISM: authoring reviews each SENTENCE; execution reviews the PAIR.** Both clauses pass *"is this true?"*; **nothing in writing forces *"do these describe the same scope?"*** — that question only arises for someone satisfying both at once ⇒ **no detection point on the authoring side by construction.** ⛔ **COMPOUNDING: the message RELAYED someone else's authorization** (the owner granted ONE edit) ⇒ the broad half would spend an authorization **the relayer did not hold and did not intend to grant** — ⭐ *authorization widens as it is relayed, and the relayer is LEAST able to notice because they know what they meant* (L116's neighbour: authority attaches to the speaker — **so does scope**). ⭐ **What saved it: the executor treated it as a FINDING, not an ambiguity to resolve** — ⛔ **and the pull is always toward the BROADER reading, because that is the one that lets you proceed**; resolved silently, an owner authorization is exceeded through an intermediary and every party believes they acted correctly. ⚠ **Separately, the narrow reading was ALSO right on the MERITS** (a retired goal makes a doc false; a LIVE goal re-tracked as a task does not) — **a correctness argument and a permission argument reaching the same answer is not one argument.** ⇒ **do not pick — send it back naming both halves.** Author's test: after writing a constraint, re-read asking only *"does anything later in this message spend what I just withheld?"* | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — no gate reads intent across two clauses. **Enforcement point: the RECIPIENT, and only if licensed to flag rather than expected to interpret.** |
| 128 | 2026-08-11 | [ACCUMULATE IN A `Map`, PUBLISH AS A PLAIN OBJECT — the prototype-safe shape and the JSON-friendly shape differ, and the CONVERSION is the seam](LESSONS.md#128) | 13.23 leg A; `security-reviewer` **low**, **fixed in-slice not deferred.** A public field keyed by a closed string-literal union must stay a plain object (serialized across a boundary), but `acc[key]++` indexes a live prototype chain ⇒ `__proto__`/`constructor`/`toString` collide with `Object.prototype` instead of creating own-properties. ⚠ **No CURRENT member collides — the point is the FUTURE one:** this union is **composed** from another module's union precisely so new members propagate automatically, ⭐ **so the property that makes the type good (extends without edits here) is what makes the accumulator unsafe without edits here.** L65 guards the identical shape two lines away, which is how the reviewer recognised it. ⇒ **accumulate in a `Map`, convert ONCE at the publication boundary via `Object.fromEntries`** — it uses `[[DefineOwnProperty]]`, **not assignment**, so even a hypothetical `"__proto__"` key becomes a real own-property. **Public type and assertions unchanged**, which is what makes it cheap enough to be the default. ⛔ **DISTINCT FROM L65** (*ReadonlyMap all the way through*), and that is why it is banked separately: **here the value MUST leave as a plain object**, so *"use a Map"* under-specifies — **the conversion is the load-bearing half, and `Object.assign` / spread / `for` with `obj[k] =` all silently re-open the hole at the last step.** ⇒ **when internal and external shapes differ on safety, name BOTH shapes and the converter.** | `pattern: grep -nE '\[[A-Za-z_.]+\] *(\+\+\|\+=\|=)' --include='*.ts' packages apps \| grep -v test` — an accumulator indexed by a **variable** key is the CANDIDATE shape; a defect only when the key domain is not provably prototype-free. `accepted: partially enforceable` — the grep over-approximates. |
| 129 | 2026-08-11 | [TYPE NARROWING IS A PROPERTY OF POSITION, NOT OF THE EXPRESSION — and a green vitest run is not a typecheck](LESSONS.md#129) | 13.8i-B, worker — **self-caught during a code-quality fix**, which is why it is worth banking: introduced by a CORRECT refactor, found by the RIGHT gate. A reviewer flagged byte-identical duplication across two drivers; extracting a shared helper was right, **but the inline version relied on cross-statement narrowing supplied by the original call site's enclosing `if`/`else`**, and once lifted the body no longer compiled (`Property 'error' does not exist on type Ok<…>`). ⭐ **MECHANISM: narrowing belongs to the POSITION an expression occupies, not to the expression** ⇒ **a "pure move" is NOT type-preserving in general** — the text is identical, the type context is not — and the hazard attaches to **extract-function / de-duplicate refactors, i.e. exactly the ones code-quality review asks for** (L118's family at the type level: you preserved the TEXT, you needed the INFERRED TYPE). ⛔ **What generalises furthest: VITEST DID NOT CATCH IT, BECAUSE VITEST DOES NOT TYPECHECK** — the suite stayed green over a build-broken tree. ⭐ **And the gap is wider here than usual: `lint` IS `tsc --noEmit` (L111), so *"tests green"* and *"lint clean"* are NOT two independent confirmations unless `tsc` actually ran** — the test runner will never supply it. ⇒ **after any extract/de-duplicate refactor, run the repo-wide typecheck, not the suite; and when a lifted body stops compiling, restore the narrowing INSIDE the new function rather than reaching for a cast — the error reports a real loss of information, and silencing it keeps the loss while hiding the report.** | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` beyond the existing gate. **Enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 8's repo-wide `tsc` — the lesson is that a GREEN VITEST RUN MUST NOT BE READ AS COVERING IT.** |
| 130 | 2026-08-11 | [A VALUE PIN AND A WIRING PIN ARE DIFFERENT TESTS — one must use the literal, the other the symbol](LESSONS.md#130) | 24.16, providers-integrations — surfaced by a pure rename, **banked as a CORRECTION to the orchestrator's own first framing**, which was too flattering to one shape. Renaming a constant (value unchanged) left one test untouched (it compares the emitted class **to the imported constant**) and forced an update on another (it asserts the constant **equals the literal**). ⛔ **The tempting, wrong conclusion — which I first wrote — is that the surviving shape is better. It is not: they pin DIFFERENT PROPERTIES and both were right.** **Symbol comparison pins WIRING** (*does production actually use this constant?* — must use the symbol); **literal comparison pins VALUE** (*is it still `"write_through_failed"`?* — must use the literal). ⭐ **Swap them and each fails characteristically: a value pin written symbol-to-symbol is VACUOUS (`expect(CONST).toBe(CONST)` = `X === X`, passes under every value including a wrong one — L80/L62's vacuity family arriving via a rename); a wiring pin written against a literal is BRITTLE — red on a pure rename, a FALSE POSITIVE whose real cost is teaching the next engineer that renames are dangerous, when what happened is a test asserted something it did not mean.** ⇒ **the question is not *"which is more robust?"* but *"WHAT DO I WANT TO BE TOLD ABOUT?"* — a changed VALUE or a broken CONNECTION. Robustness is the wrong axis: a value pin SHOULD be fragile to value changes; that is its job.** ⚠ **Corollary: a rename is a FREE AUDIT of this distinction — every test that broke claimed to be a value pin, every one that survived claimed to be a wiring pin. If a test broke and you cannot say which it was pinning, THAT is the finding, not the rename.** | `pattern: grep -rnE 'expect\([A-Z_]+\)\.toBe\([A-Z_]+\)'` — constant-compared-to-constant is the **vacuous value-pin** candidate. `accepted: partially enforceable` — catches the vacuous direction only; the brittle direction is not mechanically distinguishable from a correct value pin. |
| 131 | 2026-08-11 | [A RELEASE CONDITION PHRASED AS AN ACTION IS DISCHARGED BY ACTIVITY — write gates as OUTCOMES](LESSONS.md#131) | **Lead, self-reported — and the PROVENANCE IS THE LESSON, kept at his request: he DIAGNOSED this failure mode and COMMITTED IT TWO SENTENCES LATER, in the same message.** A block-release condition read *"`gcl/*` has actually been audited"*; the audit ran and **returned a HIGH finding on the exact claim the condition protected** ⇒ **satisfied as an ACTION, worthless as a guarantee.** ⛔ **The same message was fixing a DIFFERENT condition that had already released by attrition** (its trigger *"until 24.6 round 2 closes"* had been met) ⇒ ⭐ ***noticing a class does not immunise you against it*** — the transferable claim. ⭐ **The follow-up sweep found a LIVE NEAR-MISS proving it systemic: `§ARM-RESEARCH` precondition (1) is a TASK REFERENCE (*"task 13.8i — route the withheld PROPOSE tier…"*) and 13.8i was ticked DONE while the capability was ABSENT** (it built the mechanism, left the binding to 13.8i-B) ⇒ **an operator would have read a TICK as a CAPABILITY** — ⛔ **and the tracker had already grown a prose warning against exactly that misreading, i.e. the defect patched at the wrong layer.** ⭐ **Cheap-fix contrast in the same ledger: precondition (3) states a STATE (*"no `bootWorker` call site, nothing constructs `IngestRewriteDeps`"*) and therefore CANNOT be discharged by activity** ⇒ **repair is re-phrasing, not re-design.** ⇒ **write every gate/precondition/Done-when as a property of the SYSTEM; test with *"could this be true while the thing it protects is broken?"*** ⚠ **a task reference is action-phrased BY CONSTRUCTION (L106's five-instance ledger); cite the CAPABILITY, with the task beside it as provenance.** ⚠ **A prose warning attached to a gate is a smell — it usually means the gate is mis-phrased and someone compensated with a comment.** | `pattern: grep -nE '(Done-when\|condition\|precondition).*(has been\|have been\|was (run\|audited\|reviewed)\|is tracked\|task [0-9])' IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` — candidates to classify. `accepted: partially enforceable`. ⛔ **Sweep scope stated: arming conditions + `§ARM-*` ledgers classified 2026-08-11; the ~200 per-task Done-when lines NOT.** |
| 132 | 2026-08-11 | [METADATA-IS-AUTHORITATIVE HANDLES A STALE *STATUS*; IT DOES NOTHING FOR A STALE *ARGUMENT*](LESSONS.md#132) | **Six crossings in one round, two with real damage. Banked at the lead's instruction, with his own instance as the headline because it is the more instructive.** The established mitigation (task metadata authoritative over prose) reliably resolves *dispatched/approved/done* — ⛔ **and does nothing when what crossed was REASONING: a status has a canonical home, an argument does not.** ⭐ **COST RULE: a crossing's damage scales with how much reasoning was built ON TOP OF the stale half** — a crossed status costs one read-back; a crossed argument costs everything derived from it. **Lead's instance:** he **reversed a correct decision** and **issued a wrong priority downstream in a spawn prompt**, having held a session on *"none of 24.12's candidate REMEDIES is providers territory"* (correct) and then reversed on a claim about a **leg of the FINDING** — ⛔ **a silently substituted noun inside a sentence that still read true, and the reversal reached a third party before the retraction did.** **Orchestrator's:** answered a HOLD already reversed; cheaper, because nothing was built on it. ⇒ ⛔ **before reversing your own decision on new information, re-read the ARGUMENT, not just the conclusion** — the failure was a substituted noun, not a wrong fact. ⭐ **And when a reversal was already relayed onward, the retraction must reach the SAME CHANNEL that carried it (L94)** — a correction sent only upstream leaves the downstream instruction authoritative. ⚠ **Check your inbox before reporting something outstanding; check whether you already ruled before re-ruling** — both crossings were resolvable by reading. ⚠ **Honest limit: crossings are STRUCTURAL in an async team and cannot be eliminated; what is controllable is BLAST RADIUS** ⇒ the discipline is *notice when a crossed message is an ARGUMENT rather than a STATUS, and re-derive rather than build.* | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — no gate reads whether a message carried reasoning or state. **Enforcement point: the moment you are about to reverse a decision, or act on one that reverses yours.** |
| 133 | 2026-08-11 | [VERIFY A COMMIT BY `git log`, NEVER BY THE COMMIT'S OWN OUTPUT — the `ok` trap reaches the receipt](LESSONS.md#133) | **Lead, self-reported:** `git commit -F … -- <path>` returned **`ok (nothing to commit)`** and **silently did not land**; `git log` caught it, **the receipt would have had him move on.** The project already records that `git status` returns a literal `ok` here and that its porcelain form is worse (a `\| wc -l` reads as *"1 modified file"* — a plausible wrong answer) — ⛔ **but NOT that the trap reaches the COMMIT RECEIPT.** ⭐ **WHY THAT IS STRICTLY WORSE: a misread status costs one wrong belief about the tree; A COMMIT THAT SILENTLY DID NOT LAND IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ONE THAT DID, AND EVERYTHING DOWNSTREAM ASSUMES IT EXISTS** — the hash gets quoted into task metadata, a tracker tick, a handoff, a session doc, **and this project's entire close-out discipline is built on hashes** ⇒ **the failure reaches the durable record before anyone re-checks.** ⚠ **It also composes with L117** (work lost twice to a session dying over an uncommitted diff): **a silent no-op commit produces exactly that state while reporting success.** ⇒ **confirm every commit with `git log --oneline -1` before quoting the hash anywhere.** ⭐ **General rule: in this environment a command's own success output is NOT evidence — confirm state-changing operations by INDEPENDENTLY QUERYING THE STATE** (also `git add` → `git diff --cached`). ⭐ **A receipt is a CLAIM BY THE ACTOR; `git log` is an OBSERVATION OF THE WORLD** — L118's proxy-vs-property distinction arriving in the tooling layer. | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: the moment before a hash enters task metadata, a tracker tick, a session doc, or a handoff.** |
| 134 | 2026-08-11 | [A `default:` BRANCH IN A MAPPER OVER A CLOSED UNION IS A SILENT ABSORBER — guard exhaustiveness, don't terminate it](LESSONS.md#134) | 24.12's reviewer chase, **traced to ROOT from a single failing test.** `mapWriteFailure` switches over the closed `KnowledgeCommitFailureCode`, has no case for 24.12's new `workspace_path_violation`, and falls to `default: return "commit_failed"` ⇒ **a workspace-scope rejection reported as a generic commit failure.** ⭐ **MECHANISM: `default:` over a CLOSED union converts a COMPILE-TIME obligation into a SILENT RUNTIME RE-CLASSIFICATION** — the union's whole value is being enumerable, and `default:` discards it at exactly the moment it would pay: **the day someone adds a member. Nothing fails, nothing warns, the new case quietly means something else.** ⭐⭐ **What makes it a lesson not a style note: THIS REPO CONTAINS BOTH PATTERNS OVER SIBLING UNIONS** — `defaultSeverityForFailureClass` over `FailureClass` is **`assertNever`-guarded** (adding a member = compile error) while `mapWriteFailure` is `default:`-terminated (adding a member = silence) ⇒ **the correct pattern was already used twenty lines away in the same subsystem.** ⚠ **THIRD instance in one round of *the vocabulary grew and the consumer did not*** — 24.16's citation rot · 24.21's `kind` conflation · this — ⭐ **all three were INVISIBLE while the vocabulary was poorer and became defects the moment it grew, WITHOUT ANYONE EDITING THE DEFECTIVE FILE** ⇒ ***a change to a shared vocabulary silently re-grades every consumer that did not move with it.*** ⇒ **never `default:` over a closed union — use `assertNever`; and when you WIDEN one, the change is not done at the declaration: enumerate its consumers and check each is exhaustive rather than absorbing.** ⚠ **`default:` is legitimate over an OPEN domain (a parsed string, an external code) — the defect is `default:` over what the type system could have enumerated.** | `pattern: grep -rnE 'default:' --include='*.ts' packages apps \| grep -v test` — candidates to classify by closed-vs-open subject. `accepted: partially enforceable`. |
