| 135 | 2026-08-11 | [AN EXEMPTION PREDICATE REUSED OUTSIDE ITS ORIGINAL CALLER'S VALIDATED CALL ORDER NEEDS ITS OWN PRECONDITION](LESSONS.md#135) | 24.12, knowledge — **the traversal bug and its fix in one sentence.** `isStructuralSurface` correctly exempts KN-12 surfaces and was **sound in its original home because `admitGroundedPath` runs a TRAVERSAL CHECK FIRST.** ⛔ **Reused without that ordering, `"Logs/../employer-work-secret.md"` satisfies the exemption while `path.resolve()` lands it at the VAULT ROOT, unprefixed — the exact unsound state 24.12 exists to prevent, reachable through 24.12's own remedy.** ⭐ **The predicate did not change and did not become wrong: its GUARANTEE was never its own, it was the CALLER'S.** ⭐ **MECHANISM: a predicate's soundness can live in its CALL ORDER rather than its body, and call order does not travel with the symbol** ⇒ **reuse — correctly preferred over re-derivation (L39) — moves the body and silently drops the context that made it safe; the safest-looking refactor in this playbook is the one that can strip a precondition.** ⚠ **Second half, and why the fix is not "add a traversal check to the exemption": the same shape defeated the plain prefix match too** ⇒ **the precondition belonged to the WHOLE guard; it now gates the entire function before any exemption or match.** ⛔ **An exemption evaluated before a traversal check is a hole BY CONSTRUCTION, whichever branch it sits in.** ⇒ **when lifting a predicate, ask what the original caller did BEFORE calling it — reproduce it, or state the assumption in the predicate's own doc.** ⚠ **A string-prefix comparison is NOT path containment; the gap is where traversal lives.** | `pin: 24.12's traversal-lookalike tests (3 via applyPlan, one per match kind)` · `accepted: partially enforceable`. **Enforcement point: any refactor that EXPORTS a previously-inline predicate.** |
| 136 | 2026-08-11 | [A DOCUMENTED FALSE-POSITIVE SIGNATURE IS A CITATION, NOT A SILENT SKIP](LESSONS.md#136) | `24.25` — two implementers hit the identical `main-bundle-resolution.test.ts` failure minutes apart with identical evidence; one filed it as a task, one recognized it as **L83**'s documented concurrent-WIP signature and silently skipped it. **Both dispositions were defensible; the divergence tracked who recalled L83, not the failure itself** — the round's own propagation-not-detection headline recurring at the level of one lesson's application. | `pin: none — process convention` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: cite the signature in the same message that dismisses the failure — never a silent skip.** |
| 137 | 2026-08-11 | [A REFERENCE-EQUALITY CHECK AGAINST ONE KNOWN-BAD SINGLETON STATES "NOBODY LEFT THE DEFAULT," NOT "WHAT'S THERE IS GOOD"](LESSONS.md#137) | `24.13`'s boot guard (`activeVerifier === defaultVerifyKwSig`) genuinely blocks the one case it names — nobody overrode the placeholder — but a distinct-but-equally-weak verifier passes it silently, since identity is checked against one bad value, not a strength property. **L118/L123's family at the runtime-identity-check granularity**: a check can be real and still narrower than its surrounding prose implies. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: doc text beside an `=== <oneKnownBadValue>` guard states its precise scope, not a broader one.** |
| 138 | 2026-08-11 | [A PROPAGATION GAP EXPRESSED AS DUPLICATED CODE IS WORSE THAN ONE EXPRESSED AS DUPLICATED PROSE](LESSONS.md#138) | `24.32` — `24.19` hardened the raw-content-shape traversal in `gcl-projection.ts`; `proposeWindows.ts` kept an independent, unfixed fork of the identical check, self-described as "LOAD-BEARING" while dormant (zero production callers, established by grep-and-classify). **Every prior propagation-gap instance this round was a correction failing to reach PROSE; this one failed to reach a second IMPLEMENTATION** — worse, because nothing reads a dormant code fork until it's wired live, by which point its own comment has already told a reader the path is covered. Fix is delete-and-reuse (L39), not re-harden the copy. | `pin: none` · `pattern: grep -rn "isRawContentShaped\|carriesRawContent\|payloadCarriesRawContent" packages apps` — enumerate every implementation, not just this fork. `accepted: partially enforceable` — a differently-named fork wouldn't surface without a concept-level review. |
| 139 | 2026-08-11 | [A REVIEWER'S EMPIRICAL MUTATION TEST IS UNSAFE IN A SHARED NON-WORKTREE CHECKOUT](LESSONS.md#139) | `24.30`'s Step-9 review — a reviewer's `git checkout --`, run to mutate-then-restore a fix as this project's own recommended empirical-verification method, transiently reverted the file in the team's single shared checkout. Fully restored, no harm — but for that window, any concurrent teammate's read/test/commit on that file would have silently observed the pre-fix state. The shared-tree commit discipline this project already has (three-step commit, pathspec limits) never named a reviewer's local mutation test as needing the same care. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: keep the window short, confirm restoration explicitly, and say so if concurrent implementers are active on the same file.** ⭐⭐ **AMENDED 2026-08-13 — THE INVERSE DIRECTION, AND IT IS THE ONE A THIRD PARTY CAUSES: someone TIDYING files that belong to a RUNNING agent's working set.** The orchestrator flagged an untracked `packages/policy/test/tmp-sec/poc.test.ts` as probable throwaway scratch to dispose of before Step 10; ⛔ **it was the implementer's CURRENTLY-RUNNING `security-reviewer`'s attack harness, written minutes earlier, agent still working.** The implementer **refused to delete mid-run** — *"that would destroy a running reviewer's working set mid-measurement"* — and was right. ⛔ **MECHANISM: NOTHING IN A WORKING TREE DISTINGUISHES "abandoned scratch" FROM "a live harness belonging to a process still running"** — both untracked, both named like throwaways, **and the second is destroyed by exactly the hygiene the first invites** (`L117`'s *"untracked is the only real loss vector"* pointing the wrong way: true, and it makes a well-meant cleanup a data-loss event). ⚠ **Compounding orchestrator error: ATTRIBUTION BY TERRITORY** — an untracked test file in providers' tree during providers' slice assumed to be providers' authorship; ⭐ **the mtimes were checkable and `stat` settles it in one command** (`L159`, orchestrator as subject). ⇒ **before flagging or disposing of any untracked file you did not create: check whether a subagent is IN FLIGHT in that area, and address the DISPOSITION to the owner rather than proposing the ACTION.** ⭐ ***"Decide its fate" is safe; "this looks like scratch, clear it" is not*** — and in a team that dispatches reviewer subagents, **the window in which a harness is live is exactly the window in which it looks most disposable.** |

It wasn't — the real caller lived in `packages/`. Owner caught it; self-re-verification then found a second error (a cited function that doesn't exist). **L131 proven a second time, sharper: the SAME person who diagnosed the class in writing reproduced it within the hour, on the same subsystem.** A lesson banked does not immunize its author. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: any reachability grep written ad hoc — scan `packages/` AND `apps/` by default, or run an existing census test instead of a fresh one.** |
| 141 | 2026-08-12 | [THREE WRONG REACHABILITY CALLS ON ONE MODULE IN ONE SESSION — HAND-VERIFICATION OF REACHABILITY DOES NOT WORK HERE](LESSONS.md#141) | `24.33` — orchestrator's `apps/`-only grep (L140), then its "LIVE via the chain" claim, then the lead's inner-hop-verified claim: **three wrong reachability calls on `crossWorkspaceRead.ts`, each by someone who had just watched the previous one fail.** Ground truth: `resolveApprovedCrossWorkspaceSlice` has ZERO production callers (every call in its own test file; the three cited sites are comments). ⛔ **Error 3 was relayed to the owner as the basis for EXTENDING A RELEASE CONDITION.** Each check was locally correct and answered a different question — *in this directory?* · *invokes the real impl?* · *does this hop resolve?* — **none of them "does a production entry point reach this?"** (L118, three proxies, one module, one session). ⛔ **Failures are BIASED toward "reachable":** a found call site is pointable evidence; finding nothing feels like an incomplete search. ⇒ **the conclusion is not "try harder" — point `24.29`'s mechanical census at every arming-relevant reachability claim.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: any reachability claim becoming a release-condition premise, phase-exit input, or brief premise — cite the census run, not a grep.** |
| 145 | 2026-08-12 | [A CODE COMMENT ASSERTING ITS OWN CODE IS LIVE OR LOAD-BEARING IS A REACHABILITY CLAIM NOTHING RE-MEASURES — and it sits where it is most believed](LESSONS.md#145) | **Four instances, one round, three areas, three finders:** `24.33`'s task text (*"ONE HALF IS LIVE"* — **extended a RELEASE CONDITION before it was falsified**) · `gcl-projection.test.ts` (*"the path that actually runs today"*) · `proposeWindows.ts` (*"the LOAD-BEARING Flow-3 leakage check"* on an activity with **zero production callers** — **two occurrences in one file**; the finding cited one, the implementer fixed both) · `calendar-conflict.test.ts:15`, **still asserting it after the third was corrected**. ⭐⭐ **MECHANISM: a reachability assertion is a claim about the WHOLE PROGRAM and a comment sits INSIDE ONE FILE** — the author knows their file, not who calls it, and nothing about writing a comment prompts checking. ⛔ **Then POSITION does the damage: a reader trusts a comment as authoritative about the code it sits on, because for every OTHER claim it is** ⇒ **the one claim a comment is least equipped to make is the one it is most trusted for.** ⛔ **Decays in BOTH directions** — *live* about a dormant path, or `L138`'s *LOAD-BEARING* about an unfixed dormant fork that told readers the path was covered. **`L143` for comments, and worse: a finding lives in a ledger someone triages; a comment lives where it is quoted and never audited.** ⭐ **TELL: `LIVE` / `LOAD-BEARING` / "actually runs" / "the path that" IS a reachability claim — cite its evidence or don't make it.** ⇒ **state what is STRUCTURALLY true and let reachability be looked up; when correcting, KEEP THE TRUE HALF** (*"`crossWorkspaceRead.ts` calls `serveProjection`"* was accurate; *"the path that actually runs today"* was not) **and fix every occurrence in the file AND its siblings** (`L94`). ⚠ **Not a ban on the word — a comment MAY say a path is live if it says how that was established.** | `pattern: grep -rnE "LOAD-BEARING\|actually runs\|the LIVE " --include="*.ts" packages apps` — ⚠ candidates to classify, not defects (`L104`; this row would trip it). `accepted: partially enforceable`. |
| 146 | 2026-08-12 | [WHEN YOU CORRECT A WRONG VALUE, ASK WHAT ITS WRONGNESS WAS INCIDENTALLY PREVENTING — a defect can be load-bearing without anyone knowing it](LESSONS.md#146) | `24.15`: `rebuildAction`'s hardcoded `"queued"` literal was a real defect (an auto-eligible action came back needing approval) — ⛔ **and it was ALSO, incidentally, the only reason `requiresApproval`'s `autoAllowed` branch was STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE on every redrive.** Fixing it un-masked a latent posture-binding gap where a cross-workspace drain could evaluate an **employer-work** entry under a **personal** workspace's posture (rule 4). ⭐ **MECHANISM: a defect can be doing safety work NOTHING RECORDS, because nobody knew the guarantee existed to write down** — the branch was unreachable **by accident**, so no test pinned it, no comment claimed it, no ledger listed it. **A free, untested, but REAL guarantee.** ⛔ **Nothing reports the loss:** no test reds (the branch had none), no type change, and the fix's own direction is fail-SAFE — **every signal says the change was an improvement.** ⭐ **The INVERSE of the round's dominant shape and rarer:** many remedies rebuild their parent defect (`L79`, `L134`'s family) — **this is a correct remedy DELETING an unrecorded protection, and an unrecorded protection has no name to search for.** ⇒ **ASK AT STEP 2.5 OF ANY VALUE-CORRECTING SLICE: *what was the wrong value incidentally preventing?* If it made a branch/state/path unreachable, NAME that path and ask what guards it once the value is right.** ⚠ **Tell: a fix that is fail-safe in direction AND one-literal in blast radius** — that combination suppresses scrutiny exactly when a reachability change hides under it. ⭐ **DISPOSITION: file the un-masked gap as a WIRING PRECONDITION, not a follow-up** (`### 24.50`) — as a follow-up it is safe only by dormancy (`L106`); as a precondition it is safe BY CONSTRUCTION, **which is what lets the correct fix ship instead of being held.** ⚠ **Do not let dormancy settle severity — re-derive it mechanically** (`L141`: three wrong reachability calls in one day, biased toward "reachable"). [full](LESSONS.md#146) | `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 2.5 of any slice correcting a literal/default/constant/hardcoded token, and any Step-9 review of one. |
| 144 | 2026-08-12 | ["DONE" PLUS A GREEN SUITE DOES NOT PROVE A MIGRATION EXISTS — the deployment artifact and the test substrate are different things](LESSONS.md#144) | `24.39`: task `13.15` shipped the `Task` model, schema, repository and tests, was ticked `[x]` DONE at `54b052a7` and certified — ⛔ **with NO migration generated in either dialect, for eighteen days, green the whole time.** ⭐ **MECHANISM: `packages/db`'s repo-contract suite builds its test DBs DIRECTLY FROM THE DRIZZLE SCHEMA and never calls `applyMigrations()`** ⇒ every `TaskRepository` test passed against a table the deployment path could not produce. **The suite and the deployment disagreed, and the suite is the one everybody reads.** ⛔ **Invisible to typecheck (schema is real), to the suite (bypasses migrations by construction), to review (the diff WAS complete but for the artifact no tooling touched), and to `/wired` (the symbol is reachable; the TABLE isn't)** — it surfaced only because `drizzle-kit generate`, run for an unrelated one-column change, tried to bundle the missing `CREATE TABLE` into that slice. ⭐ **GENERAL FORM: whenever the artifact that VERIFIES a capability is built by a different mechanism than the artifact that SHIPS it, "tests pass" says nothing about "it ships."** Family with `L89`/`L84`/`L85`, and the sharpest member — **the other three are about a weak test; here the test is STRONG and points at a different object.** ⇒ **exercise the real artifact (migration/bundle/generated schema/packaged binary/registered workflow), and derive the expected set from the source of truth, never a hand-maintained list** (the sibling `DOMAIN_TABLES` array had already drifted by five tables — `24.43`). ⚠ **Corollary that reprices history: "certified DONE" for a schema-touching task carries less than it appears to** — `13.15`'s Done-when never mentioned a migration, which is exactly how it passed. | `pin: packages/db/test/migrate/schema-migration-coverage.test.ts` · `pattern: none reliable — the defect is an ABSENT artifact and no grep finds a file never written` · `accepted: partially enforceable`. **Enforcement point: any slice adding/altering a persisted schema, and any Done-when for one.** |
| 143 | 2026-08-12 | [A RECORDED FINDING DECAYS — only its TEXT is re-read, its CLAIM is never re-measured; triage must RE-MEASURE](LESSONS.md#143) | `24.41`'s decomposition of Carry-forward item 6: of the **four** sub-findings that could be checked mechanically, **THREE contradicted their own recorded status** — two were **already fixed and nobody noticed** (`(a0)(ix)(2)`'s claimed `0` measured `1` for all five areas; `(a0)(vi)`'s proposed staleness fix was implemented), one was **false as written** (`(a0)(v)`'s *"`plan-lint` has no trigger ANYWHERE"* — `orchestrate-end.md:100` runs it; a real, narrower residual survived under a wrong headline). ⭐ **MECHANISM: a finding is written once with evidence, and thereafter only its TEXT is re-read — never its CLAIM re-measured**, so an item sits `OPEN` for rounds while the defect is gone. ⛔ **The INVERSE of the round's propagation headline, and the expensive direction:** a fix that failed to propagate makes someone rebuild a guard (wasteful, safe); **a fix that propagated while the record didn't catch up inflates the open set and lends stale items the credibility of their neighbours.** ⭐ **`L126` for FINDINGS rather than lessons — sharper, because a finding is an assertion about a specific file at a specific commit and rots immediately.** ⇒ **treat any finding older than a round as a HYPOTHESIS and re-run the check it specifies** — all three were disproved by running the finding's own grep. ⚠ **A finding stating no re-runnable check is the one to worry about** — write findings with their falsification command attached. ⚠ **Not "distrust the ledger"** — the record is a snapshot; triage is where it refreshes. **A triage pass that only re-reads is transcription, not triage.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: `/orchestrate-end` Carry-forward triage, and any audit/phase-exit verdict resting on a recorded finding rather than a fresh measurement.** |
| 142 | 2026-08-12 | [A RELEASE-CONDITION CHANGE IS COMPLETE WHEN THE LEAD RE-READS THE DOC AND CONFIRMS — not when acknowledged, not when committed](LESSONS.md#142) | The condition-(d) surface **drifted TWICE in one day, identical mechanism**: an owner ruling reaches the orchestrator's inbox → is **acknowledged in a message and acted on** → the doc write crosses with other work or slips ⇒ **everyone's messages reflect the new state while the artifact everyone reads stays stale.** ⛔ **Acknowledgement is the ACTIVE INGREDIENT, not an aggravating detail: the ruling LOOKS RECEIVED** — instance 2's orchestrator correctly held `24.33` and dispatched `24.36` instead, and that visible behavioural change is exactly why nobody checked the file. **A ruling ignored gets caught immediately; one acted-on-but-unwritten is invisible BECAUSE it was acted on** (L91 inverted). ⛔ **Stakes are maximal — the artifact is the release criteria.** ⭐ **Both times, the only thing that caught it was the lead re-reading the file** — not `plan-lint` (well-formed prose), not the commit (instance 2 had none), not close-out, not the task list. ⇒ **writer quotes the amended text back; lead re-reads the file regardless** (L133's receipt-vs-`git log`, in the coordination layer). ⚠ Scoped to release-condition/arming-ledger text deliberately, or it becomes skippable ceremony. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable`. **Enforcement point: any owner ruling changing a release condition or arming ledger. Neither step optional; the second is the one that has actually caught it.** |
| 147 | 2026-08-13 | [TRUSTED PROVENANCE IS NOT VALIDATED SHAPE — "it came from somewhere safe" is a claim about the SOURCE, never about the BYTES](LESSONS.md#147) | **Banked at the lead's instruction after the SAME insight was independently re-derived at FOUR sites in one day** — *"four independent re-derivations is the signal that it has no home."* Sites: `### 24.55` · `packages/policy/src/visibility.ts:152-155` (`24.45`'s own residual comment, stating it verbatim) · `### 24.62` (`workspaceId` is **registry-validated**, which proves *the id EXISTS in `workspace_config`* and says **nothing** about whether it is well-shaped) · `### 24.65` (`from`/`to`, same trust class). ⛔ **MECHANISM: a provenance check answers *where did this come from?*, a shape check answers *what is in it?* — independent questions, and the provenance check is cheaper to write, easier to review, and READS as more rigorous** (*"resolved against the registry"* sounds stronger than *"matches a pattern"*), so it **displaces** the shape check instead of complementing it. ⭐ **What makes it durable rather than an ordinary omission: the provenance check is usually CORRECT AND LOAD-BEARING** — `24.62`'s registry resolution genuinely closes caller-injection. **A real guarantee, correctly implemented, about a different property than the consumer needs. Nobody is wrong; the argument stops one step early.** ⛔ **The trust is CIRCULAR wherever the caller can influence the source** — *"trusted because config-sourced"* is only as strong as the WRITE PATH into that config, which is almost never examined in the same breath (filed as its own task here, deliberately). ⚠ **Applies to closed-by-TYPE values too — the least intuitive corner:** a `denialCode` is unscanned because it is closed **by type**, but that is a **compile-time claim about runtime-untrusted data** across a deserialization boundary ⇒ **closed-by-type is a provenance argument wearing a type-system costume.** ⇒ **state the provenance claim explicitly, then ask out loud *what SHAPE could this value have, given that source?*** — if the answer is *"anything a writer to that source can put there,"* say so in-code (`L82`) rather than letting one guarantee stand for both. | `pin: none — the subject is which of two questions an argument answered.` `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any review admitting a value because of WHERE IT CAME FROM; and any `Done-when` whose closure argument contains "trusted", "registry-validated", "config-sourced", or "closed union".** |
| 148 | 2026-08-13 | [WHEN YOU FIX SOMETHING, ASK WHAT IT INCIDENTALLY FIXED — a residual-documenting comment outlives the residual and nothing reports it](LESSONS.md#148) | ⭐⭐ **The DIRECTIONAL INVERSE of [L146](LESSONS.md#146), banked together at the lead's instruction — *bank them together or neither will be found.*** `### 24.65`'s second defect (*"a null `sourceWorkspace` THROWS … a pre-existing §16 never-throw violation"*) **DOES NOT EXIST**, falsified by probe at Step 2.5: `24.45`'s own `?.` at `visibility.ts:156` makes `srcId` `undefined`, so the mismatch branch at `:191` **returns before `:195` is ever reached.** ⭐⭐ **MECHANISM: `24.45`'s single-read hardening was made for a TOCTOU/getter reason and INCIDENTALLY closed a never-throw hole nobody was looking at — then the comment documenting the residual OUTLIVED THE RESIDUAL BY ONE COMMIT.** ⛔ **Why nothing reports it: a residual-documenting comment is BORN TRUE and is falsified by a fix aimed at something else entirely** ⇒ no test reds (the residual had no test — that is what made it a residual), no type change, and no reviewer of the TOCTOU fix had reason to re-read a comment about never-throw. ***Its lifetime is tied to something nobody tracks.*** ⛔ **Blast radius: FOUR artifacts all asserting an already-fixed violation** — the tracker task, the in-file comment (`visibility.ts:149-151`), **session doc `161:72` (committed)**, and the orchestrator's brief; each correct when written, each copied forward by someone with no reason to re-measure (`L143`'s decay with a specific cause rather than mere age). ⭐ **The pairing is the reusable part and neither half is complete alone: `L146` — a fix can DELETE an unrecorded guarantee; `L148` — a fix can SATISFY a recorded finding. Same blind spot, opposite directions: everybody checks whether a change BROKE something; nobody checks what it silently REPAIRED** — and the second inflates the open set with items that no longer exist, lending stale findings the credibility of their neighbours. ⚠ **Correct a propagated residual in a session doc by DATED ERRATUM IN PLACE, never by rewrite** — silently correcting it destroys the evidence the false claim travelled, which is itself the data (`### 24.66`). ⚠ **NOT "re-audit every finding after every fix"** — the trigger is narrow and checkable: *you hardened a predicate that other findings are recorded against*, which is a grep of the file's name in the tracker, not a review. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 9 of any slice hardening a shared predicate or guard; and `/orchestrate-end` Carry-forward triage, where a finding closed by someone else's fix is otherwise re-READ rather than re-MEASURED.** |
| 149 | 2026-08-13 | [THE AUTHOR OF A PIECE OF REASONING IS THE WORST REVIEWER OF TEXT THAT RESTATES IT — they check whether it matches their INTENT, not whether it is TRUE](LESSONS.md#149) | **Banked at the lead's instruction, who then applied it to themselves.** `#48`'s item 4 was ~19 comment lines restating a lead ruling and the orchestrator's reasoning; the implementer **disclosed that no reviewer had seen it** (written after review dispatch) and asked whether to accept or re-dispatch. ⛔ **Answer was re-dispatch, and the reason is the lesson: *"I am the worst available reviewer for it — I authored the reasoning it restates."* The lead added the other half — the ruling is theirs, so they are second-worst.** ⇒ **the two people most motivated to check it were the two least able to.** ⛔ **MECHANISM, and why it is not ordinary bias: reviewing text that restates your own reasoning is a DIFFERENT COGNITIVE OPERATION — you compare the text to the version in your head and pass it when they match.** ⭐ **That is INTENT-MATCHING, and from the inside it feels exactly like truth-checking; the fluency of recognition is indistinguishable from the confidence of verification.** ⇒ **if the reasoning was wrong, the restatement is faithful and the review CONFIRMS it.** A reviewer who does not hold the intent has only the text and the code — the pair that can disagree. ⭐ **Bites hardest on the safest-looking artifact: a COMMENT** — pure prose, zero logic, mechanically verifiable as behaviour-preserving, **so it invites exactly the review that cannot catch a false claim** — and here the artifact was a RESIDUAL NOTE, whose whole purpose is *to be believed by someone who will not re-derive it.* ⭐ **Family with *a rule does not protect its own author* (`### 24.66`), extended from application to REVIEW — and worse, because it produces a review artifact that READS as independent.** ⚠ **NOT "never review your own area"** — narrow claim: when the text RESTATES reasoning you produced, your review does not count as coverage; say so and route it. ⚠ **Route to someone holding NEITHER the intent NOR the authority** — a reviewer who knows the ruling came from the lead is reviewing an authority, not a claim. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: choosing a reviewer for any slice whose diff includes prose restating a ruling, a lesson, or another agent's reasoning; and any Step-9 disclosure that part of a diff post-dates its review dispatch.** |
| 150 | 2026-08-13 | [TWO DEFECTS THAT REINFORCE EACH OTHER ARE WORSE THAN TWO INDEPENDENT ONES — each makes the other look CONFIRMED](LESSONS.md#150) | **Banked at the lead's instruction; both defects were the orchestrator's.** A tracker entry and commit message about `denyDirectCrossWorkspaceRaw` carried **(1)** a wrong PROPERTY — *"has no production caller"* where the measurement was *"not production-REACHABLE"* (it HAS two; the CHAIN terminates at nothing — `L118`) — and **(2)** a wrong PACKAGE, citing both hops as `packages/policy/src/…` when **neither file exists** (they are `packages/knowledge/src/gcl/`). ⛔ **MECHANISM: a reader who follows citation (2) lands in `packages/policy`, finds nothing, and CONFIRMS claim (1).** **The mis-attribution MANUFACTURES CORROBORATION for the false claim — and it feels like diligence, because the reader did go and look.** ⭐ **Worse, it HID THE REASON the other defect mattered:** the hops living in `packages/knowledge` behind a public `export *` is precisely what makes *"no caller"* dangerous — **one import arms a rule-4 gate with fully caller-supplied values** — so **the wrong package removed the very fact that would have exposed the wrong property.** ⇒ **Independently each is a routine citation error; together they form a CLOSED LOOP THAT SURVIVES BEING CHECKED.** ⭐ **GENERAL FORM: defects COMPOSE, and error-correction assumes they do not** — every check (re-read the citation, follow the reference, re-measure) tests one claim while taking the rest of the context as sound, and **two defects in one sentence break that assumption because the check for one runs THROUGH the other.** ⚠ *"I verified it"* is weaker than it sounds when the verification path shares state with the claim (kin `L84`, `L112`). ⇒ **when a claim fails verification, DO NOT STOP AT THE FIRST DEFECT** — ask what else in that sentence you took as background; **resolve a citation BEFORE using it to evaluate the claim**, and if it does not resolve, treat the claim as **unverified rather than refuted** (`L93`). ⚠ **Report that they COMPOUND** — a reader told only "two errors" fixes them one at a time and never sees the loop. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any correction of a durable claim that turns up more than one defect; and any review that resolves a citation in order to test the sentence containing it.** |
| 151 | 2026-08-13 | [CITE WHAT A GATE TESTS, NEVER THAT IT PASSED — a passing heuristic is not a validated shape](LESSONS.md#151) | ⭐ **The ACTIONABLE form of [L147](LESSONS.md#147); adopted as a convention at the lead's instruction, in the implementer's words.** `24.62`: a draft justified keeping `event` on a log line with ***"the gate PASSED, so all six scanned fields are clean."*** ⛔ **False, and already retracted in-repo** — `packages/policy`'s own `isRedactionSafe` doc (from `24.45`) says it is a **credential-shape HEURISTIC, not a shape allowlist**; *"an employer project codename, a person's name, or an internal identifier"* all pass. ⇒ **gate-passed means only *"did not look like a leaked secret."*** ⛔ **MECHANISM: a gate's NAME describes its ambition, its DOC describes its test, and a citation carries the NAME.** *"`isRedactionSafe` passed"* reads as *"this is redaction-safe"* — **not a lie, not the guarantee**; the reader supplies the missing quantifier and supplies the generous one. ⭐ **`L147`'s displacement one layer in: there the provenance check displaced the shape check; here the gate's NAME displaces the gate's TEST.** ⭐ **The fix is checkable in a diff, which is why it is a convention and not an exhortation** — state the real reason (*`event` is a string literal at all three reachable producers*) **and flag it as a CALL-PATH property, not a type one**, so the next reader knows what would invalidate it. ⇒ **a justification you can CHECK beats a citation you can only TRUST.** ⚠ **PROVENANCE — the round's most reproduced result: written ELEVEN LINES BELOW the author's own sentence warning that the next producer inherits the reasoning rather than the audit. Third instance in one day of someone breaking a rule they had just written, in the same file** (an implementer one slice apart · the orchestrator within the hour of authoring the uncritical-relay taxonomy · this). ⇒ ***a rule does not protect its own author*** — **which is why these are written as diff-visible checks, not as things to bear in mind.** ⚠ **Hardest on gates whose names are ADJECTIVES** (`isRedactionSafe`, `isValid`, `isTrusted`, `assertSafe`): **the name is a claim about the WORLD, the implementation is a claim about ONE PREDICATE.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any comment, commit message, or Done-when whose safety argument is *"X passed"* / *"X validated it"* / *"it went through X."* Replace with what X tests, or say the guarantee is unestablished.** |
| 152 | 2026-08-13 | [AN UNRESOLVABLE CITATION MAKES A CLAIM UNVERIFIED, NOT REFUTED — "I looked and found nothing" feels like refutation AND feels like diligence](LESSONS.md#152) | **Split out of [L150](LESSONS.md#150) at the lead's instruction, because ONE discrimination covers THREE of the round's separate defects.** **(1)** a wrong-PACKAGE citation (`packages/policy/…` where **neither file exists**) — a reader follows it, finds nothing, and **confirms** a false claim in the same sentence; **(2)** a cross-NAMESPACE collision (`#43` shared-list vs `### 24.43` tracker) where **both resolve, to unrelated tasks**, so landing on the wrong one reads as *"I misremembered"*; **(3)** **eleven rotted line numbers** to `boot.ts:588` whose symbols now sit 53–90 lines away **in a different function** — each still resolves, each to the wrong thing. ⛔ **MECHANISM, and it is a fact about the READER not the citation: AN ABSENCE TERMINATES A SEARCH.** *"I went and looked and it wasn't there"* has the texture of a completed check — **it costs effort, produces a definite result, and feels like exactly the diligence we ask for** ⇒ **the reader STOPS, holding a CONCLUSION derived from a FAILED LOOKUP, which supports no conclusion at all.** ⭐ **The discrimination is one question: DID THE POINTER RESOLVE?** If not — wrong path, wrong namespace, moved line, renamed symbol — **you have learned nothing about the claim.** ⚠ **Direction is what makes it expensive: it manufactures FALSE NEGATIVES, and all three instances pointed that way** — a false positive gets one classification and is corrected; **a false negative closes the question with a ✅ and ships an incomplete fix under a confident all-clear.** ⇒ **when a lookup comes back empty, ask whether the POINTER was good BEFORE concluding anything** — in this repo the seams span packages deliberately, so "another package" is usually the answer. ⭐ **Report *"citation did not resolve"*, NEVER *"the thing is absent"*** — different findings, different next actions, and only the first is what you measured. ⚠ **Corollary for the AUTHOR: this is why an anchor must fail LOUDLY (`### 24.71`)** — an absent symbol gets investigated; a line number that silently resolves to the wrong thing gets believed. **Generalises [L93](LESSONS.md#93) from lesson citations to every pointer.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any negative conclusion drawn from a failed lookup — a review, an audit, a premise check, a sweep. State whether the pointer resolved, SEPARATELY from what you found.** |
| 153 | 2026-08-13 | [CORRECTING A COMMENT HAS TWO FAILURE MODES — true-for-a-different-reason gets STRUCK not rewritten; an instruction whose imperative INVERTS is not a comment fix](LESSONS.md#153) | **Both halves banked together — they are the only two axes a comment-correction slice can go wrong on, and separated neither gets found.** **HALF 1:** a `buildActivities.ts` block said *"do not read the absence of a behavioural test as an oversight"* and was **struck without replacement.** ⛔ **The claim had not become FALSE — it became TRUE FOR A DIFFERENT REASON** (absence is now caught by `TS2741`; what no test can reach is whether the supplied STRING is correct). ⭐ **MECHANISM: a FALSE claim announces itself; a still-true claim whose GROUND MOVED reads as merely stale, so the cheap edit is DELETION — which silently discards a real guarantee nobody re-derives.** ⚠ **The correction and the deletion look IDENTICAL in a diff: both remove the sentence, only one puts something back.** ⇒ **before striking a stale-looking claim ask *is it FALSE, or TRUE ON A GROUND THAT MOVED?*** — if the second, **rewrite with the new ground, never delete.** ⭐ **[L146](LESSONS.md#146)'s shape aimed at PROSE, and sharper: there the fix and the loss were different edits; here they are the SAME edit.** **HALF 2:** a comment can INSTRUCT rather than describe. `worker-host/index.ts` told a reader to **perform the binding a new rule forbids** ⇒ **action became WRONG** ⇒ needed lead authorization. `legacy-workspace.ts`'s *"do NOT delete it"* ⇒ step 3 made the const the sole supplier of a required parameter ⇒ **action became MORE right**; only its time-binding was stale. ⭐⭐ **DISCRIMINATOR: an instruction whose imperative STRENGTHENS is safe to fix in place; one whose imperative INVERTS needs authorization.** ⇒ **the question is not *"is this comment stale?"* but *"did the action it prescribes change DIRECTION?"*** ⛔ **PLACEMENT FINDING that re-prices the exercise: NONE of the three DISPATCHED sites contained an instruction — the only one was in a FOURTH site the implementer found and added.** ⇒ ***the risk lived entirely in the part nobody had scoped*** — at once the strongest argument for stating the axes in a no-brief dispatch AND for the implementer widening scope. **A guard aimed at a filed scope cannot cover what the filing missed.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any slice correcting comments. Half 1 at the moment of striking a sentence; half 2 before treating an instruction-shaped comment as an ordinary comment fix.** |
| 154 | 2026-08-13 | [A SOUND MEASUREMENT DOES NOT LICENSE A SYSTEM-LEVEL CLAIM — the claim needs the CALL PATH](LESSONS.md#154) | **Banked at the lead's instruction as the GENERAL FORM: three parties, three roles, one shape, one round.** A probe measured — **correctly** — that `applyPlan` throws on three cast-free shapes with the vault already written. ⛔ **The reported conclusion, *"nothing catches the fault,"* was FALSE:** `createCommitActivity` wraps it in try/catch and folds any throw to `commit_failed`, test-pinned, all three production compositions funnelling through it. ⭐⭐ **The author's own diagnosis IS the lesson: *"my measurement was correct; my conclusion about what it meant was not"* — they measured `applyPlan` IN ISOLATION and reported a SYSTEM-LEVEL consequence, while quoting `L145` at other people.** ⛔ **THE PROPAGATION IS THE POINT — implementer measured a UNIT and claimed a SYSTEM property · orchestrator RELAYED IT BECAUSE THE MEASUREMENT WAS SOUND (the soundness is exactly what made the conclusion look checked) · lead GRADED from it, and named their own defect precisely: *"I insisted on resolving the citation for the RULE, at source, correctly — AND I TOOK THE CODE DESCRIPTION FROM THE FINDING. A grading has TWO sides and I sourced ONE."*** ⇒ ⭐ **A sound measurement is the HARDEST kind of wrong answer to catch, because every downstream check finds REAL evidence: a fabricated number dies at the first re-run, but a CORRECT number attached to the WRONG SCOPE survives re-measurement indefinitely — you can re-run the probe forever and it keeps agreeing.** ⇒ **before a measurement becomes a claim about the SYSTEM, name the CALL PATH it traverses and check the boundary at each hop.** ⭐ **Operationally: *what is the smallest scope this measurement covers, and what is the scope of the sentence I am about to write?* If they differ, the difference is the UNMEASURED part — say so or go measure it.** ⚠ **Relayers: the measurement's soundness is NOT evidence for the conclusion's SCOPE — re-derive the scope, not the number.** ⚠ **COMPANION, `L147`'s sharpest instance, same Step 9: the same implementer claimed a type lived in `packages/contracts`; it is in `packages/workflows/src/ports/meetingCloseout.ts`, AND THEIR OWN EARLIER GREP OF CONTRACTS HAD COME BACK EMPTY** ⇒ ⛔ ***not MISSING evidence — DISCARDED evidence, because the wrong answer felt more right than their own result.*** **They answered *"where WOULD this live?"* instead of *"where DOES it live?"*** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any Step-9 Finding, brief premise, or grading input whose evidence is a measurement of ONE UNIT. State the measured scope and the claimed scope separately; if relaying, re-derive the SCOPE rather than re-running the measurement.** |
| 155 | 2026-08-13 | [REDIRECT TO A FILE, THEN MEASURE THE FILE — a pipe truncates silently, UNDER-reports, and nothing about truncated output looks truncated](LESSONS.md#155) | **Adopted by the lead as a STANDING RULE for all roles within the hour, from an orchestrator's first three minutes.** Same range, same session: `git log --oneline origin/main..HEAD \| wc -l` → **50**; `git rev-list --count origin/main..HEAD` → **56**. ⛔ **Resolved rather than picked:** writing both sets to files and counting the files gives **56 and 56**, and `comm` shows **zero** set difference; `git log --format=%H … \| sort -u \| wc -l` also gives 50 ⇒ **the variable is the PIPE, not the format.** ⭐⭐ **Two properties make it outrank subtler mechanisms in practice: (1) IT UNDER-REPORTS** — on an unpushed count that reads as *"less to push than there is,"* and a round could seal believing it had pushed everything; **(2) IT IS INVISIBLE** — **50 is a perfectly plausible number**, with no error, no exit code, no gap, no ellipsis. **A fabricated number dies at the first re-run; a truncated one agrees with itself forever.** ⚠ **Second instance, same session:** `diff <(…) <(…)` returned the literal string **`[ok] Files are identical`** — real `diff` prints a diff for differing files and **NOTHING** for identical ones (verified both directions on scratch files) ⇒ **that string was injected, not produced; process substitution is compromised too.** Kin to `L133`'s `git status` → `ok` trap — **that one reaches the RECEIPT, this one reaches the MEASUREMENT.** ⭐ **THE CONTROL THAT CATCHES THIS IS NOT THE NON-VACUITY CONTROL** — a term-known-present control proves the search READ the file and **passes happily over truncated output**, since every printed row was correct. **Anti-truncation is a different check: frequency-table ROW COUNT vs `sort -u \| wc -l`, and the table's counts must SUM to the occurrence total.** Two mechanisms, two controls. ⚠ **NOT "never pipe"** — piping is fine for READING; the rule binds when the number will be **reported, recorded, or compared**, which is exactly when it becomes durable and stops being re-derived. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any command whose output becomes a count in a commit message, tracker entry, Step-9 report, handoff, or seal. ⛔⛔ **RE-DERIVE the number — NEVER adjust a previous one, and never pipe it.** And before citing a second derivation as corroboration, state what would have made the two disagree.** ⛔⛔ **ENFORCEMENT WIDENED same day at the lead's instruction, because the author broke a NARROWER version one level below it: having measured 20 round-commits correctly, they reported 22 in the next message by ADDING ONE for the commit they had just made** — ⛔ **`L155` as first written says *re-derive if a PIPE produced it*; here NO COMMAND PRODUCED IT AT ALL.** ⇒ ***A COUNT THAT WILL BE REPORTED GETS RE-DERIVED, NOT ADJUSTED.*** ⭐ **Worse than the pipe case, because `+1` on a correct number LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A MEASUREMENT** — cheaper than the command, starting from a genuinely-measured value, **and indistinguishable from a real count in the output.** ⚠ **Ninth instance this round of a rule failing against its own author — committed inside the message reporting that this lesson had been banked.** ⭐⭐ **AMENDED same day at the lead's instruction — THE HALF THAT MAKES CONCORDANCE MEAN ANYTHING.** Their Phase-24 census had been "corroborated" by the predecessor's independent count agreeing — **worthless, because both ran the SAME PIPE SHAPE in the SAME ENVIRONMENT, and a deterministic truncation makes two piped derivations agree BY CONSTRUCTION.** ⇒ ***the test is not "did two derivations agree," it is "COULD THEY HAVE FAILED INDEPENDENTLY."*** ⭐ Contrast the same day: a set found by the spelling `workspacePathCheck` and a set derived by the PROPERTY (`readVaultHeadRevision` in `expectedBaseRevision` position) returned the same two files — **different symbol, different mechanism ⇒ they could have disagreed and didn't. That is evidence.** ⚠ **Agreement between DEPENDENT methods is the cheapest thing to obtain and the most reassuring to read** — it is exactly what a careful person produces re-checking their own work the obvious way. |
| 157 | 2026-08-13 | [THE CODE-INTELLIGENCE INDEX RETURNS A CONFIDENT "No callers found" FOR CALLBACK-POSITION CALL SITES — and it is the tool everyone is told to prefer over grep](LESSONS.md#157) | **Banked at the lead's instruction, with an explicit instruction to carry it into every spawn prompt's traps list.** `codegraph_callers("readVaultHeadRevision")` → **"No callers found."** There are **TWO production call sites** — `semanticApprovalDispatch.ts:97` and `buildActivities.ts:1109`, both `expectedBaseRevision: () => readVaultHeadRevision(…)` — **in CALLBACK POSITION**, which the tool's own docs claim to follow (*"dynamic-dispatch hops — callbacks … that grep can't follow"*). ⛔⛔ **Three things make it worse than an ordinary tool bug: (1) IT FAILS TOWARD THE SAFE-LOOKING ANSWER** — *no callers* ⇒ *dead code* ⇒ *unreachable* ⇒ **task closed with a ✅**; here it would have closed a **rule-1-adjacent audit-integrity finding** by concluding no live-head-resolver compositions exist. **(2) IT CARRIES INDEX AUTHORITY** — an empty `grep` feels like it might have been scoped wrong; **an index saying "No callers found" reads as a resolved fact about the whole program.** **(3) EVERY CONVENTION DOC HERE INSTRUCTS AGENTS TO PREFER IT OVER `grep`** (root `CLAUDE.md`, orchestrator briefing, all six area guides, plus a per-tool-call session hook) ⇒ **the failure mode is installed directly in the path of the recommended workflow.** ⭐ **Caught by `L152` used PROSPECTIVELY — *"no callers found" is UNVERIFIED, not REFUTED*** — re-run as a redirected `grep` with a non-vacuity control (`applyPlan` → 130 hits, proving the search read the tree), returning 27 references. ⇒ **never let a code-intelligence census be the SOLE basis for a NEGATIVE claim** (reachability / dead-code / no-callers / safe-to-delete); corroborate with a redirected grep + control, and prefer derivation by PROPERTY over symbol name. ⚠ **Positive results stay trustworthy — the asymmetry IS the lesson: the index tells you where something IS and is not evidence about where it ISN'T.** ⚠ **NOT "distrust the index"** (that is `L111`'s false-doubt failure) — the narrow claim is that **a "no callers" result is a QUESTION, not a verdict.** | `pattern: none reliable — the defect is a MISSING result, and no grep finds an absence in another tool's output.` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any reachability / dead-code / no-callers claim sourced from a code-intelligence tool, especially one about to close a task or license a deletion; and every spawn-prompt traps list.** ⛔⛔ **TRAPS-LINE WORDING IS PRESCRIBED (lead) — carry the ASYMMETRY, not the bug:** ***"`codegraph`: a HIT is evidence; an EMPTY is not. Callback-position call sites can be invisible to `codegraph_callers`, so a 'no callers found' result is a QUESTION, not a verdict."*** ⛔ **Never *"codegraph is unreliable"*** — that discards a tool genuinely better than grep for every positive question, and is `L111`'s false-doubt failure in one sentence. |
| 158 | 2026-08-13 | [A HARNESS PROVES NOTHING UNTIL YOU HAVE CHECKED BOTH ITS REACH AND ITS READOUT — two independent ways a reproduction attempt lies, failing in opposite directions](LESSONS.md#158) | **Both halves banked together at the lead's instruction — separated, neither is findable.** ⭐ **A reproduction harness is an INSTRUMENT, and this project has a rule for measurements taken with commands (`L155`) and NONE for measurements taken with code you wrote for the occasion.** **HALF 1 — REACH:** `### 24.76`'s `ADD:` required the harness to assert its own preconditions before anything downstream was trusted (**fault actually FIRED · `getByIdempotencyKey` EMPTY on retry · `compareRevision` PASSED**). ⛔ **Without them a harness that silently missed the scenario is BYTE-INDISTINGUISHABLE from one that ran it and found nothing** ⇒ *"did not reproduce"* and *"never got there"* produce the same output, and only the first is a finding. ⭐ **The payoff is on the NEGATIVE side, which is why it must be built in ADVANCE: because reach was proven, a negative result would ALSO have been worth something.** ⚠ The injected fault is exactly the kind of thing that can short-circuit the path you meant to drive — **fault and reach-failure share a cause.** **HALF 2 — READOUT:** the first harness printed `baseRevisionId: undefined` for every row; **`AuditRecord` HAS NO SUCH FIELD** (`revision.ts:100-119`). Re-run against `beforeSummary`; **artifact reported instead of the number.** ⛔⛔ **As-is it would have been a FABRICATED FINDING ABOUT AUDIT-RECORD CONTENTS, inside the task filed to stop a wrong audit row being believed** — ⭐ **the defect class reproducing itself in the instrument built to measure it.** ⭐ **GENERAL FORM (lead, verbatim): *a harness's output is not evidence until you have checked the harness is describing the object it claims to describe* — the type system's job when you have one, and NOBODY'S JOB WHEN YOU PRINT.** ⚠ **`undefined` is the tell and a weak one:** it renders identically for *"absent from this instance"* and *"never existed on this type,"* and the second fabricates. ⇒ **(1) assert the preconditions that put the harness in the target state and report them beside the result; (2) resolve every printed field against the real type — an always-`undefined` field is a QUESTION about your reader, not a fact about the data.** ⚠ **NOT a rule against throwaway harnesses** — this one produced a genuine executed finding four people had left as a plausible paragraph. | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any reproduction attempt, fault-injection probe, or scratch harness whose output will become a verdict. State reach evidence and readout provenance SEPARATELY from the result.** |
| 159 | 2026-08-13 | [WHEN YOU PROPOSE A CAUSE, STATE HOW IT COULD BE RULED OUT — a well-indexed error catalogue makes you WORSE at diagnosis, not better](LESSONS.md#159) | **Lead, self-reported after two instances hours apart, banked as a rule they imposed on themselves.** **(1) THE CENSUS** — they doubted their Phase-24 count because it came through a pipe; **the doubt was sound and named the WRONG NUMBER.** The `head -12` truncation hit the *classifier-corrected* count; the raw `16 / 81` came through a different command and was never at risk ⇒ **re-testing what they named would have "confirmed" a figure nobody doubted while leaving the doubted one unmeasured.** **(2) THE COMMIT COUNT** — a 21-vs-22 discrepancy attributed to **counting the base**, *"the error I made with 40 from `a4b65b75`"*;
