| 160 | 2026-08-13 | [A CORRECT ANSWER FROM A BROKEN INSTRUMENT IS WORSE THAN A WRONG ONE — the wrong one gets caught, the right one gets the METHOD REUSED](LESSONS.md#160) | **Self-reported by providers-integrations-implementer at `#58` Part 1; their framing is the lesson.** **Two consecutive census attempts were VACUOUS and BOTH would have returned the TRUE answer:** (1) `for s in $SYMS` — ⛔ **zsh does not word-split**, so the loop ran ONCE with all 16 names as one symbol ⇒ a clean-looking "NONE"; (2) `awk '$0 ~ "\\<" S "\\>"'` — ⛔ **macOS `awk` has no `\<` word boundaries** ⇒ every symbol returned nothing, **including the positive control.** The true answer was zero external importers ⇒ ***both broken methods would have produced the correct result.*** ⛔⛔ **THEIR STATEMENT OF WHY THAT IS THE BAD OUTCOME: *"I would have reported 'no importers' from a broken matcher and been ACCIDENTALLY RIGHT — the worst outcome, since it would have validated the method."*** ⭐⭐ **MECHANISM, and it is about the FUTURE not this measurement: a WRONG answer from a broken instrument is contradicted by reality and the instrument gets fixed; a RIGHT answer is CONFIRMED by reality, so the instrument is trusted, recorded as the method, and REUSED on a question where it will be wrong.** ⇒ **the damage is in every measurement that copies the recipe.** ⚠ **This is why a non-vacuity control is not optional even when you are confident of the answer — ⭐ ESPECIALLY then, because a confidently-expected result is the one nobody re-runs.** **The control does not check the ANSWER, it checks the INSTRUMENT, and those come apart precisely when the answer happens to be right.** ⇒ **report the POSITIVE control (term known present + count) and where cheap a NEGATIVE one** (here `admitProjection` = 13 files ✓, `zzzNotARealSymbolQQ` = 0 ✓, final method `grep -rwl` per symbol). ⚠ **Two silent environment specifics to carry: zsh does not word-split** (a `for x in $LIST` loop silently runs once — second instance, different shape) · **macOS `awk` has no `\<`/`\>`.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any census, sweep, or enumeration whose result will be reported. State the control WITH the result — a control run and not reported is the same as not run, because the reader cannot distinguish them.** |
| 161 | 2026-08-13 | [UNDERSTATING A GUARD INVITES DELETION; OVERSTATING A GUARANTEE INVITES THE READER TO STOP CHECKING — both fail open, and `L153` covered only the first](LESSONS.md#161) | **Found by worker-implementer at `#51` Step 2.5 while tracing a selector rather than stopping at a field doc; their generalisation.** `copilotClaudeSynthesis.ts:449-451` and `:525` state **unconditionally** that *"the ONE served workspace reads the local gbrain; every other stays on the fixture (**WS-8 by construction**)"* — **true only of the scoping-OFF branch.** **Fifteen lines below, `:529-538` describes all three branches and says multi-served holds WS-8 *"by the mandatory per-request filter (not by construction)."*** ⛔⛔ **SAME FILE, TWO STATEMENTS, AND THE STALE ONE CARRIES THE STRONGER SAFETY PHRASING** ⇒ **a reader answering the open rule-4 task by quoting `:449-451` concludes isolation holds BY CONSTRUCTION and closes it as safe** — ⭐ **a live false-assurance positioned exactly where the next investigator lands.** ⭐⭐ **THE ASYMMETRY THAT COMPLETES `L153`: understating a GUARD** (*"this check is inert"*) **invites its DELETION** (`L153`'s territory); **overstating a GUARANTEE** (*"by construction"* where it holds by a filter) **invites the reader to STOP CHECKING** ⇒ ***both fail open, by opposite routes, and only the first was written down.*** ⭐ **OPERATIONAL FORM: what makes a reader appropriately cautious is UNDERSTATING a guarantee, never overstating one.** ⚠ **"By construction" is the highest-strength phrase available and therefore the most expensive to get wrong** — it tells the reader *no runtime condition can make this false*, terminating inquiry in a way *"enforced by X"* does not ⇒ **reserve it for properties that cannot be otherwise, and BRANCH-QUALIFY the moment a second branch exists.** ⚠ **Structural cause: the overstatement was CORRECT WHEN WRITTEN** (one branch then); a second branch was added, the prose 15 lines down moved and the field doc did not ⇒ **`L148`'s shape arriving in a SAFETY phrase rather than a residual note** — **when you add a branch, grep the file for claims quantified over the old branch count.** ⭐⭐ **AMENDED same day from the sweep this lesson triggered — THE TELL IS POSITIONAL, which turns the lesson from a thing to NOTICE into a place to LOOK.** The task named **2 sites in 1 file**; the sweep found **4 across 3**, and **the two it added were worse-positioned**: `copilotGbrainSubprocess.ts:8-14`, the **FILE HEADER**, claimed WS-8 *"BY CONSTRUCTION"* **while that file EXPORTS `createMultiServedGbrainRetrieval` and its own function doc 190 lines below already said *"by SCOPE FILTERING, not by construction"*** ⇒ **the header contradicted its own file**; and `boot.ts:334-341`, the **operator-facing CONFIG field doc**. ⇒ ***THREE OF FOUR DEFECTIVE SITES WERE THE ENTRY DOC FOR THEIR SCOPE (field doc · file header · config doc); THE ONE THAT STAYED CORRECT WAS THE INLINE COMMENT NEXT TO THE CODE.*** ⭐ **MECHANISM, mundane and therefore reliable: a new branch lands BESIDE THE CODE, so the author updates the comment they are already editing — THE ENTRY DOC IS NOBODY'S DIFF** ⇒ **the doc most likely to be stale is the one most likely to be read FIRST and weighted most heavily; the two properties compound rather than cancel.** ⇒ **when a branch is added, check the scope's ENTRY DOCS first** (file header, exported-symbol docblock, config-field doc, README) **before the inline comments, which probably moved with the change.** ⚠ **Classify by SUBJECT, not phrase: ~60 `by construction` hits in `apps/worker/src` were overwhelmingly a different subject** (redaction-safe, idempotent, traversal-safe, UI-safe, byte-equivalent) — **a phrase census is a candidate list; the subject classification is the work.** | `pin: none` · `pattern: grep -rnE "by construction" --include="*.ts" apps packages \| grep -v test` — **candidates to classify** (`L104`: this row trips it), each asking *"is there a branch where this does not hold?"* · `accepted: partially enforceable`. |
| 162 | 2026-08-13 | [AN ADVERSARIAL REVIEWER'S VALUE IS NOT EXTRA COVERAGE OF THE SAME SPACE — it is coverage of the space the author's CONFIDENCE CLOSED](LESSONS.md#162) | **Providers-integrations-implementer's observation, twice in one slice (`#58`); banked on the LANDED instance, not the pending one.** ⭐ **Their framing, last clause is the lesson: *"I picked the attack shapes I could imagine; it picked one I could not — in the exact place my confidence was highest."*** **INSTANCE 1 (landed, `code-quality-reviewer` HIGH):** having just written the hardened accessor `readOwnDataProperty`, the author **did not re-scan the file for other readers of the same field** — `isWithinDefault(level, sourceWorkspace.defaultVisibility)` was **a raw second read of the property hardened three statements earlier** ⇒ **a Proxy reporting `isolated` via `getOwnPropertyDescriptor` while returning `full` via `get` passes the new guard AND THEN SUPPLIES THE CEILING**, on the one check bounding over-exposure, **falsifying the author's own docblock claim of "the single hardened read this module uses."** ⛔ **The reviewer's FIRST move was exactly the scan the author skipped.** **INSTANCE 2 (pending, `security-reviewer`):** a `Proxy` whose `getOwnPropertyDescriptor` trap THROWS — which the primitive invokes — possibly falsifying *"closes the throw class without a `try`/`catch`."* ⚠ **Recorded as pending, NOT as evidence.** ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM: an author's enumeration of a hazard TERMINATES WHERE THEIR CONFIDENCE IS HIGHEST — not a lapse, that is what confidence IS** ⇒ **the unexamined region is not random; it is systematically the region the author just finished and feels best about**, and **they cannot locate it by trying harder.** ⇒ **dispatch an ADVERSARY, not a checker** (told to BREAK the fix, not confirm it), ⭐ **and aim it at the part the author is MOST sure of — the inverse of the instinct to review the shaky part.** ⚠ **Author corollary: when a slice hardens ONE reader of a value, the mandatory next act is a census of EVERY reader of that value in the file — the thing you just fixed is exactly what stops you looking.** ⭐ **Extends `L149` from PROSE to ATTACK-SURFACE ENUMERATION.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: reviewer dispatch on any slice hardening a guard, predicate, or parsing boundary. Name the region the author is most confident in and aim the reviewer there; require a same-file census of every reader of any value whose read was hardened.** |
| 163 | 2026-08-13 | [PRECEDENT-BY-RESEMBLANCE — a precedent can be a reason the QUESTION was settled without being a template for the ANSWER](LESSONS.md#163) | **The false wording was the orchestrator's; found by knowledge-implementer against their OWN precedent comment. Banked at the lead's instruction as a new failure shape.** `applyPlan` needed a guard for *"plan declares mutations but the diff is empty."* The implementer found `tombstone.ts:254-265` — the other KnowledgeWriter entry point — already guarding an empty-change-set condition, deliberately, with a task citation. ⭐ **A genuine and valuable find: it converted `24.77` from a design gap into a DEFECT, because the subsystem had decided the question once and `applyPlan` never got the answer.** ⛔ **Then it was relayed into two tracker entries and a message to the lead as *"guards the IDENTICAL condition."* IT IS NOT — and it diverges in exactly the two respects the slice turns on: CONDITION** (tombstone tests `changes.length === 0` **ALONE**, precisely the WIDENING `24.77` forbids) **and DISPOSITION** (tombstone SUPPRESSES; `24.77` ruled against suppression). ⇒ ⛔⛔ ***A READER COMPLETING THE PARALLEL WOULD HAVE UNDONE THE SLICE*** — arriving at the widened predicate and the suppression, both by faithfully following a precedent cited to justify the work. ⭐⭐ **THE DISCRIMINATION: a precedent can establish THAT a question was already decided without being a model for HOW — the two uses look identical in prose and are completely different in force.** ⚠ **Asymmetric in a nasty way: the precedent's VALUE (the subsystem already ruled) is what makes it persuasive, and that persuasiveness carries straight over to its DETAILS, which nobody re-checked.** ⭐ **Structural reason it survives review: a precedent citation is normally checked for EXISTENCE — does that code do roughly this? — and existence was true here. Nobody checks a precedent for FIDELITY unless they are about to copy it, and by then they are the person being misled.** ⇒ **state WHICH of the two you mean; if it is "the question is settled," say what the precedent does DIFFERENTLY and why that is sound there and wrong here.** ⚠ **Kin to `L124`** (cite by what it DID and the file it did it in) — **`L124` guards a compressed LABEL; this guards an accurate label attached to a DIVERGENT MECHANISM.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any brief, task, comment, or Step-9 report citing an existing implementation as precedent. Name the divergences, or say explicitly that you checked and there are none.** |
| 164 | 2026-08-14 | [A COUNT USED AS A COST ESTIMATE IS A CLAIM — and cost estimates are where nobody applies the measurement discipline, because they feel like CONTEXT rather than CLAIMS](LESSONS.md#164) | **The count was the orchestrator's, the ruling built on it was the lead's, falsified by knowledge-implementer at `#67` Step 2.5. Banked at the lead's instruction, ON THEMSELVES.** *"Narrowing `./*` breaks 3 live deep-importers across two packages"* was **the stated reason `### 24.78` was filed as a separate DEFERRED task rather than closed** — lead, verbatim: ***"I filed the wildcard fence SEPARATELY on that reason. My reason is falsified; the ruling may or may not survive it."*** **Measured: 2 files, and the third was NOT AN IMPORTER AT ALL** — `anti-corruption.test.ts:77` is an element of a `realImports` **STRING ARRAY**, inside a test asserting the write-surface scanner **trips on every idiomatic import form including deep subpaths.** ⇒ ⭐⭐ ***A TEST THAT DEFENDS AGAINST DEEP-SUBPATH IMPORTS, COUNTED AS A FILE COMMITTING ONE*** — population-by-spelling (grouped by the text `@sow/knowledge/`, not by *is it an import*), and **the member was the OPPOSITE of what the count claimed.** ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM, about a CATEGORY OF SENTENCE rather than a category of error: this project measures FINDINGS relentlessly and does not measure COSTS at all.** A number appearing as *"this would break N files"* / *"three tracks would need to migrate"* **reads as background colour supporting a decision, not as the decision's load-bearing premise** ⇒ **it passes review unexamined precisely because everyone is examining the finding it sits next to.** ⭐ **And the consequence is a DEFERRAL — the quietest possible outcome: a falsified cost estimate produces no wrong fix, it produces a hazard CARRIED AS FILED-AND-DEFERRED when it could have been closed.** ⚠ **Nothing re-checks a deferral's premise, because the item is *tracked*, which reads as *handled*** (`L143` entered through the cost half). ⇒ **when a count decides SCOPE, SEQUENCING or DEFERRAL, measure it as rigorously as one deciding SEVERITY.** ⭐ **Tell: if a sentence contains a number AND *"so"* / *"therefore"* / *"which is why,"* the number is a premise, not context.** ⚠ **AND A CORRECTED COUNT DOES NOT AUTOMATICALLY REVERSE THE DECISION (lead's own caution): *"breaks few importers" is a different question from "is safe to narrow."*** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any deferral, scope cut, sequencing decision, or separate-task filing whose justification contains a number. That number is a claim and it gets a method.** |
| 165 | 2026-08-14 | [THE COMMIT MESSAGE IS AUTHORED BY ONE AGENT AND LANDED BY ANOTHER — a HANDOFF WINDOW in which an approved message can be falsified, and only the lander can see it](LESSONS.md#165) | **knowledge-implementer edited an approved commit message before committing and disclosed it immediately. THIRD instance of the underlying rule failing this round; the first two were the predecessor's.** **The structure, specific to this team's split:** the **orchestrator AUTHORS** the message at Step 9, the **implementer LANDS** it at Step 10 — **and work continues in between.** **Instance:** the SHIP message answered `#66`'s Step 9 and carried *"3 files deep-import today"* twice, while the implementer's `#67` Step 2.5, sent in the interim, **had already measured it as 2 files plus a string literal** (`L164`) ⇒ **an approved message containing a claim its own recipient had disproved.** ⛔ **The standing rule is *issue a commit message AFTER the last thing that can falsify it, OR re-verify every factual claim at landing.*** ⭐⭐ **The author owns the first half and CANNOT own the second — they are not present at landing** ⇒ ***the second half is structurally the LANDER'S, and the lander is the party least licensed to change the text.*** **That tension is the lesson.** ⇒ **RESOLUTION, all three parts, any one alone being wrong: (1) do NOT ship a claim you have measured false** — it is headed for the durable record, which is re-read far more than re-derived; **(2) do NOT silently alter the author's text — add a VISIBLE note that the figure was corrected after approval, and why**, because ⭐ **silent correction destroys the evidence that the false claim existed and travelled, which is itself data** (`L148`'s erratum discipline applied to a commit message); **(3) disclose in the same breath and offer the amend** — the author may know something you do not. ⚠ **The author's half does not go away: the cheapest fix is still to write the message LAST**, and a Step-9 reply issued while another measurement is in flight **should say which measurements it is NOT conditioned on.** ⚠ **Corollary, easy to get wrong: the correct response to being corrected here is NO AMEND** — restoring the original re-introduces the falsified claim to satisfy authorship, and hiding the correction erases the evidence. **The edited message with its visible note IS the right artifact.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: `/tdd` Step 10 immediately before `git commit` — re-read the approved message against anything measured since it was written; and Step 9, where the author notes which in-flight measurements the message assumes.** |
| 166 | 2026-08-14 | [A RUN SAMPLING DURING ANOTHER TRACK'S COMMIT SEES A RED THAT BELONGS TO NOBODY — and the natural attribution is the track that just committed](LESSONS.md#166) | **worker-implementer, caught by re-running before sending an escalation they had ALREADY HALF-DRAFTED.** Mid-slice, `--project @sow/worker` returned **57 failed test files** and `turbo` **8/17** with `TS2307: Cannot find module '@sow/knowledge/markdown-vault/sections'`; the draft attributed it to knowledge's just-landed commit. **Re-run on a settled tree: 163 files / 2099 tests / 0 failed, `turbo … --force` 31/31, 0 cached.** ⛔⛔ **A TORN READ of the shared working tree while another track's commit was landing — the red belonged to NO ONE and reproduces for NOBODY.** ⭐⭐ **THE DISPROOF IS WHAT MAKES IT A FINDING RATHER THAN A SHRUG, AND IT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT "it passed the second time": `packages/knowledge/dist/markdown-vault/sections.d.ts` STILL DOES NOT EXIST, so resolution goes via `./*` → `./src/*.ts` and THE MISSING `dist` WAS NEVER CAPABLE OF BEING THE CAUSE.** ⚠ **Load-bearing distinction: *"passed on re-run"* is exactly what `L111` warns reads as FLAKY — two runs disagreeing evidences NON-DETERMINISM, never that the failing run was fictitious** ⇒ **only a structural argument separates *never real* from *intermittent*, and they demand opposite responses.** ⛔ **INVERSE OF THE KNOWN TRAP:** the documented one is *a HOLD defers the COMMIT, not the EFFECT* (their uncommitted work is in your runs); **this is your run sampling their tree MID-TRANSITION.** ⭐ **Worse in two ways: the failure is transient rather than reproducible, and the most PLAUSIBLE attribution — the track that just committed — is also the most WRONG** ⇒ **the escalation would have been specific, evidence-bearing, correctly addressed, and entirely false.** ⇒ **A CROSS-TRACK RED IS A HYPOTHESIS UNTIL REPRODUCED ON A SETTLED TREE; reproduce before attributing, and prefer a STRUCTURAL disproof to a re-run** (*could the named cause have produced this at all?*). ⚠ **Do NOT over-correct into ignoring cross-track reds** — `L136` records a documented false-positive signature *silently* skipped as its own defect. **Cite the signature and the reproduction attempt; never skip silently, never attribute unreproduced.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any test/typecheck failure in a shared checkout implicating another track, and any escalation drafted from one. Re-run on a settled tree and state the structural argument before naming a cause.** |
| 167 | 2026-08-14 | [`git commit --amend` IN A SHARED CHECKOUT OPERATES ON WHATEVER HEAD POINTS AT — and per-path verification is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to it](LESSONS.md#167) | **The round-seal incident. Self-reported by worker UNPROMPTED, and independently measured by the orchestrator from the reflog before that report arrived. Banked as a PROCEDURE GAP, not a worker error.** Worker committed `9674554e`; the orchestrator committed the round seal `614bcbdc` on top; worker then ran `--amend` to improve their own message — ⛔ **but HEAD was no longer theirs.** Result: **`1de290d9` = THE SEAL'S CONTENT WEARING WORKER'S MESSAGE**, with `614bcbdc` unreachable and surviving only in local reflog, **which expires.** ⭐ **NOTHING LOST, verified per-file not assumed** — seal text present in `git show HEAD:IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, worker's `boot.ts` intact at `9674554e`. **The damage was to the LABEL.** ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM, worker's own words: *`--amend` in a shared checkout is not an operation on YOUR commit — it is an operation on WHATEVER HEAD POINTS AT WHEN IT RUNS.* HEAD is shared mutable state, and the window between "I committed" and "I amend" is exactly long enough for a teammate to land.** ⛔⛔ **AND WHY IT IS A PROCEDURE GAP: PER-PATH VERIFICATION CANNOT DETECT IT.** The standing check is `git log --oneline -- <path>` (`L133`); **worker ran it and it correctly resolved `boot.ts` to `9674554e`** ⇒ ***you check YOUR path, it resolves correctly, and the damage is to a DIFFERENT file's commit.*** ⇒ **run `git log --oneline -1` IMMEDIATELY BEFORE `--amend`, or chain a fail-closed guard so the race is UNREPRESENTABLE** (`[ "$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)" = "<your-hash>" ] && git commit --amend …`); ⭐ **better, prefer a FOLLOW-UP COMMIT or ERRATUM — race-free by construction.** ⛔ **DISPOSITION RULING, and it generalises: NOT repaired by rewriting.** An amend **would ERASE THE EVIDENCE THE INCIDENT HAPPENED**, in the one artifact whose job is to be findable, **and would re-create the defect it fixes.** ⇒ ***corrections land IN the record, never OVER it*** — **a fresh corrective commit dissolves the circularity that "record it in the seal" creates when the SEAL is the damaged artifact.** ⭐⭐ **Worker's self-diagnosis, sharper than "I forgot": TWO SLICES EARLIER THEY REFUSED TO AMEND FOR EXACTLY THIS REASON, checked the same condition here, found HEAD clean, and proceeded — TREATING A SNAPSHOT AS A DURABLE PROPERTY** (`L141` with the author as counter-example). ⚠ **Contributing cause, the orchestrator's: a FINAL commit message revising an already-approved one created the amend pressure at all** (`L165` — the message was written three times for that slice). ⭐ **And the auto-mode classifier denying the repair amend was CORRECT and was not routed around, by worker who was certain they were right or by the orchestrator holding a clean recovery — *a guard is only worth having if it holds when the person is certain.*** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any `git commit --amend` in a shared checkout, and every spawn-prompt traps list. Confirm HEAD is yours in the same breath, or use a follow-up commit instead.** |
| 156 | 2026-08-13 | [A SELF-REFERENTIAL COUNT IS CORRECT AS OF ITS OWN WRITING — and the naive "fix" INTRODUCES the error](LESSONS.md#156) | **Banked at the lead's instruction as a new shape.** The sealed round's tracker entry reads **"16 commits · 55 unpushed"**; measured at HEAD immediately after, **17 · 56**. **Both are correct** — the seal line was written **before the seal commit that carries it existed**, and a commit cannot count itself. ⭐ **MECHANISM: the artifact is part of the population it measures**, so any count written INSIDE the thing being counted is a snapshot one step before the final state — **the gap is not an error, it is the arithmetic of self-reference.** ⛔ **THE DANGEROUS PART IS THE REPAIR:** a later reader re-measures, finds 17/56 against a recorded 16/55, and "corrects" it — **making the line disagree with every other artifact of that round** (the commit's own message, the archive entry, the handoff) and converting a *documented snapshot* into a *false current reading* that will be re-corrected again next round. ⇒ ***the record was right, the reader is right, and the edit is wrong*** — which is why it needs naming: **nothing about the situation signals "do not touch."** ⭐ **DISCRIMINATOR, cheap: *could the author have known this number when they wrote it?*** If the artifact sits inside its own population — a seal counting its own round's commits, a changelog counting its own release's changes — the answer is no, and **the recorded number is a correct snapshot, not a stale one.** ⇒ **prefer to DE-INLINE entirely** (cite the regenerating command, never the value — `L94`; ⚠ the same tracker says *"count with `git rev-list --count`"* two lines away **and then inlines 55 anyway**), or **stamp it as of-authoring** (*"55 at seal time, excluding this commit"*) so the off-by-one is self-explaining. ⚠ **Kin to `L143` with the sign FLIPPED** — there a record decayed and triage had to re-measure; **here the record is permanently accurate and refreshing it is the defect.** ⇒ **"re-measure before trusting" and "do not overwrite a snapshot" are both right; the discriminator is whether the artifact sits inside its own population.** | `pin: none` · `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` — **enforcement point: any round seal, changelog, release note, or archive entry stating a count of the change it is part of; and any later reader about to correct such a count.** |
| 168 | 2026-08-14 | [A HOLD OUTLIVES ITS REASON SILENTLY — a correctly-reasoned precondition, cancelled by a decision the holder never saw](LESSONS.md#168) | Session doc `169` held itself uncommitted behind *"commit this only after `1de290d9`'s message is restored"* — **correct when written**. The lead then ruled the repair OFF (*corrections land IN the record, never OVER it*), making the release condition **permanently unsatisfiable**, and the holder's session had already ended. ⛔ **MECHANISM: a hold is a precondition on a FUTURE state and NOTHING RE-EVALUATES IT when an unrelated decision voids it** — the ruling's author is not thinking about who is parked on that action; the holder is not present to notice. **Both parties act correctly and the artifact freezes.** ⛔⛔ **Cost had three layers and the third is the dangerous one:** untracked (the only real loss vector, `L117`) · a header instructing readers not to commit it · **an "Open follow-ups" naming the COUNTERMANDED amend as *"the only outstanding action"*** ⇒ `L153` half 2, **an instruction whose IMPERATIVE INVERTED**, in the incident's own authoritative record. ⭐⭐ **Why a careful read confirms rather than catches it: NOTHING IN THE TEXT IS WRONG — the reasoning is intact and still persuasive; the WORLD moved, not the text.** A stale fact contradicts reality and dies on re-measurement; this does not. ⭐ **[L148](#148)'s family on a different artifact** (there a FIX satisfied a recorded finding; here a RULING voided a recorded precondition) and **[L91](#91)'s inverse** — what happens when the holder *cannot* release it because their session ended. ⇒ **imposing a hold: name the DURABLE release surface AND who releases it if you are gone (a hold whose releaser is gone is a freeze). Issuing a ruling that forecloses an action: ask what is WAITING on that action.** ⚠ **Not a rule against holds** — this one was right and prevented a real mess; the defect is a hold with no transferable release path. `pattern: git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` — an untracked file surviving a round is a **candidate stale hold**, not just untidiness. `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 169 | 2026-08-14 | [AN ERRATUM ON A DOCUMENT'S PREMISE DOES NOT CORRECT ITS INSTRUCTIONS — and the instruction is the dangerous half](LESSONS.md#169) | Worker's erratum on doc `169` annotated the **GATE** (lines 7–9) exactly as ruled; it did **not** reach the amend **INSTRUCTION** at `:33` or the "Open follow-ups" entry — **26+ lines away, and a reader landing on the repair block does not scroll up.** ⭐ **Lead's own framing: *"Worker executed my ruling faithfully; my ruling was scoped to the sentence I knew about."*** One site named, three in the document. ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM: an erratum is written where the FALSE CLAIM is, because that is where the author was looking when they found it — but instructions are DERIVED from the premise and live elsewhere**, so correcting the premise leaves every derived imperative intact, unmarked, and unsupported. ⭐⭐ **THE ASYMMETRY: a premise MISLEADS; an instruction GETS FOLLOWED.** A stale premise may produce no action; a countermanded instruction has already been acted on (here: an amend that would erase the incident evidence and re-create the defect). ⇒ **the instruction is where the erratum matters MOST and is LEAST likely to be placed**, because the author found the defect by reasoning about the CLAIM, not by reading for imperatives. ⭐ **[L94](#94) at point-of-use inside ONE document — worse than L94's form**, whose channels were separate artifacts; here they are regions of one file and *"I corrected that document"* feels complete. ⚠ **[L153](#153) half 2's completion:** L153 says an inverting instruction needs authorization; **L169 says the authorization must then reach the instruction's own location.** ⇒ **re-read for IMPERATIVES after any erratum; derive the site list by READING, not from the reviewer who found the first one; annotate beside the text, preserved verbatim.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 170 | 2026-08-14 | [A DEFERMENT CONDITION CAN NAME A REMEDY THAT DOES NOT EXIST — filing an unbuildable fix makes an accepted risk look SCHEDULED](LESSONS.md#170) | **Lead, self-reported on their own approval condition.** Deferment (D) on `### 24.65` required *"file the REAL fix — pass a trusted counterpart into `denyDirectCrossWorkspaceRaw`."* ⛔ **Providers then measured, hop by hop, that NO SUPPLIER EXISTS:** `CrossWorkspaceLinkMap` has **zero dependencies** — its entire authority is what callers told it, and its own admission check is the *same non-empty-and-distinct class* as the check being strengthened ⇒ **the only obtainable counterpart is the same caller-supplied string echoed back through a `Map`.** ⛔⛔ **Lead's diagnosis, verbatim: *"I required the real fix be FILED, which felt rigorous, and never asked whether it was BUILDABLE."*** ⛔ **MECHANISM: filing a remedy tests that someone NAMED one, never that one EXISTS** — and nothing in the act of filing distinguishes a deferred fix from an impossible one. ⭐⭐ **THE COST IS A CATEGORY ERROR THAT AGES IN ONE DIRECTION:** an unfiled residual is visible as *an accepted risk*; one filed with an unbuildable remedy is **invisible as a risk AND as a blocker — it sits in the queue looking like work.** ***"Deferred" invites a future reader to simply go do it — and here the nearest buildable thing was the trap*** (a caller-echoed counterpart = `L147` manufactured deliberately, which "would read as coverage forever after"). ⚠ Nothing re-checks a deferral's premise because the item is *tracked*, which reads as *handled* (`L164`). ⭐ **Kin [L102](#102)** (phantom obligation); **distinct from [L131](#131)** — L131's condition can be satisfied by worthless activity, **L170's cannot be satisfied at all**, and nobody finds out until someone tries to build it. ⇒ **ask what would SUPPLY it, not just what the remedy is; if nothing does, record "unfixable at this layer" + "what would have to be true" — NOT a task.** ⚠ **Not an argument against filing remedies — the defect is treating the filing as the check.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 171 | 2026-08-14 | [A PER-PACKAGE ERROR COUNT IS A COUNT OF VANTAGE POINTS, NOT OF DEFECTS](LESSONS.md#171) | `24.72` Leg A's declared break is **ONE site** (`commitKnowledge.ts(97,13)` TS2322); the per-package run reports **FOUR packages**, each the SAME error at the SAME location, because each typechecks workflows' source transitively. ⛔ **Reporting "4 packages" would have inflated the consumer leg fourfold — and it would have looked like a measurement.** ⛔ **MECHANISM: a monorepo typecheck reports per-OBSERVER, and the natural unit to report is the one the tool prints** ⇒ **population-by-spelling in a new unit, grouped by *who noticed* rather than *what is wrong*.** ⭐ **Tell is decisive and cheap: same file, same line, same code ⇒ one defect** — deduplicate on `(file, line, code)` before any number leaves your session. ⚠ **Same run, opposite direction:** the first `turbo … --force` reported `Tasks: 14 successful, 16 total` — **it HALTED DEPENDENTS**, so its blast radius was **incomplete by construction** ⇒ **one run over-counted the defect and under-counted the surface simultaneously, and neither announces itself — 4 looks thorough, 14/16 looks like a pass rate.** ⇒ **report `(file, line, code)` triples, never a package count; and say when a build tool short-circuited — a halted run's coverage is a claim about the RUNNER, not the tree.** `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 172 | 2026-08-14 | [MEASURE YOUR OWN COMPLIANCE — do not assert restraint](LESSONS.md#172) | `24.72` carried a hard constraint: no rule number in any commit message, comment, or test name (the grading was the lead's). **Every prior slice would have satisfied that by SAYING so. This one MEASURED it — `0` hits for `rule \d\|safety rule` across 59 added lines and the new test file, reported as a number.** ⛔ **MECHANISM: a compliance claim about your OWN output is the one you are least able to check by introspection, because you check against your INTENT rather than the bytes** (`L149` aimed at self-review). ***"I did not write a rule number" and "no rule number appears" are different claims***, and only the second is verifiable by anyone else. ⭐⭐ **Disproportionately cheap, because such constraints are almost always a grep over your own diff** — no rule number · no `TODO` · no secret literal · no `console.log` · no deleted symbol · no forbidden phrase. **One command on the diff you already have, converting an assertion into evidence**, versus a reviewer taking your word for something they'd have to read every line to falsify. ⚠ **Asymmetric the bad way: a false compliance claim is invisible to review** (the reviewer reads for defects, not for your restraint) **and survives into the durable record — which is why the constraint existed.** ⇒ **grep your own diff and REPORT THE COUNT, especially when it is zero — zero is the only result that looks identical to not having checked.** `pattern: git diff --cached \| grep -cE '<forbidden token>'` · `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 173 | 2026-08-14 | ["DO NOT PIN THE DEFECT" AND "DO NOT BUILD ON THE DEFECT" ARE DIFFERENT FAILURES — and only the first was written down](LESSONS.md#173) | **The implementer's distinction, sharper than the constraint they were given.** `24.72` said *do not close this by pinning either throw* (`L69`). Leg A then broke 4 of `### 24.77`'s tests, whose harness asserted `.rejects.toThrow(...)`. ⛔ **They looked like a violation and were not: they CONSUMED the throw as setup scaffolding; they never ASSERTED it as intended behaviour** ⇒ the constraint was never breached, and repairing them made the harness **strictly stronger** — pinning a contract instead of depending on a defect. ⭐⭐ **PINNING** a defect ⇒ the suite asserts the broken behaviour is correct ⇒ **the fix reads as a regression** and the next person backs it out *with a failing test's confidence* (`L69`, gate inverted into a lock). **BUILDING ON** a defect ⇒ the suite merely *uses* it ⇒ **the fix legitimately breaks the test and breaking it is CORRECT.** ⇒ **OPPOSITE responses: a pinned defect means the test was WRONG and must be re-aimed; a built-on defect means the test was RIGHT and its scaffolding moved.** ⛔ **Conflating them deletes a good test in one direction and keeps defending the defect in the other.** ⚠ **The tell is what the assertion is ABOUT, not what it mentions** — both mention the broken behaviour, **which is why a grep cannot separate them and a skimming reader will not.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 174 | 2026-08-14 | [A CORRECTION DOES NOT PROTECT ITS OWN AUTHOR — you re-commit the retracted claim in a different region of the same file](LESSONS.md#174) | **TWO INSTANCES, ONE DAY, TWO AGENTS.** The implementer's Step-8 comment asserted *"nothing caught the fault… it escaped as an untyped rejection"* — the exact claim `L154` retracts — ⛔⛔ **while the same file already carried THEIR OWN correction of it** (`writer.ts:275`, written that morning: *"AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS COMMENT CLAIMED IT DID"*), **266 lines away.** The orchestrator committed the identical claim the same day, in a brief whose own §16 section cites `L154`. ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM: a correction lives at the site of the ORIGINAL error; a later edit happens in a DIFFERENT REGION** — ⭐⭐ **and the author does not re-read their own correction, because KNOWING WHAT IT SAYS is exactly what prevents re-reading it.** ⇒ ***a correction is written for future readers, and its author is the one person who will never be a future reader of it.*** ⭐ **[L94](#94)'s CEILING** — even a correction reaching every channel fails to protect its writer, who does not consult channels they authored; **neighbour of [L149](#149) one step on: the author of a CORRECTION is the likeliest to RE-COMMIT the corrected error.** ⭐⭐ **DETECTION IS THE REUSABLE HALF, in the implementer's words: *"Reading your erratum is not what caught it; grepping my own diff for the phrase is"*** — **[L172](#172) applied to a retracted CLAIM rather than a forbidden token. ⇒ add the retracted PHRASE to your own Step-9 self-grep, permanently: the correction is for readers, the grep is for you.** Sweep the whole file, not the region you edited; **expect the only match to be the negating correction itself.** ⚠ **Reviewer corollary: a file containing an explicit correction of X is a HIGHER-risk site for a fresh assertion of X — the correction's presence reads as immunity and is the opposite.** `accepted: partially enforceable` ⭐ **AMENDED 2026-08-17 — one more hop: a CORRECTION can introduce a new defect of the SAME FAMILY, and it is the hardest to catch because it arrives with a reviewer's endorsement attached.** A fix for an over-claim prescribed a census returning ZERO on the one file with a deliberate bypass. **A correction is new content and carries the full evidentiary bar.** |
| 175 | 2026-08-14 | [A COMMENT THAT DESCRIBES OTHER FILES DECAYS FROM EVERY DIRECTION AT ONCE — and none of them touch the file it lives in](LESSONS.md#175) | One block enumerating nine sites in **two other files** carried **THREE decays from three unrelated directions**: someone else's comment-only insertions **moved all nine pointers, none dangling — every one resolving to plausible text** (one onto *the other author's new note about the sites*); **the block author's OWN later slice, in a THIRD file, falsified its behavioural prose**; and the enumeration was **grouped by SPELLING**, nine members that were never one population. ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM is EXPOSURE, not care: a comment's decay surface scales with how much of the world it describes and INVERSELY with how often its own file is edited.** It never appears in the diff of the change that falsifies it · nobody editing the described file sees it · **its author edits it least, because as prose it is *finished*** ⇒ ***three decay sources, ZERO signals in the file carrying the claim.*** ⭐ **Contrast a note AT the site: it is in the diff of every change to that site, so the falsifying edit and the claim arrive in the same review.** ⭐⭐ **REMEDY IS PLACEMENT, NOT DILIGENCE — *a note at the site cannot be moved away from it; this one can, and was.*** ⚠ Where a cross-file block is genuinely needed: **anchor by SYMBOL and POPULATION** (`### 24.71`), **declare site-level notes AUTHORITATIVE over it**, and **name the population's defining property so membership is re-derivable rather than trusted.** ⚠ **Not an argument against cross-file docs** — that block was load-bearing; it is an argument that such a block is a **known-decaying artifact** and must be written to be re-derivable, not merely correct at authoring. ⭐ **[L145](#145) pointed outward** (L145's comment over-claims about the code beneath it; this one under-defends against code it cannot see), **and composes with [L174](#174)** — the author is at once the least likely reader and a live source of falsification. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 176 | 2026-08-14 | [A CONDITION NAMES THE MOST OBSERVABLE PROXY FOR ITS EVENT, NOT THE EVENT — and a trigger can fail silently in BOTH directions](LESSONS.md#176) | **THREE instances, ONE session, THREE authors, none careless.** `### 24.76`'s trigger named a **TEXTUAL** change for a **SEMANTIC** condition (the catch was never edited; its COVERAGE shrank when a throw became a returned `err`) · `#43`'s fold named a **SESSION-SCOPED** home for a **DURABLE** need · `#74` named a **NUMBER** for an **AUTHORSHIP** event (doc 170 landed — in another area's chain; acting would have written a false successor link). ⛔⛔ **MECHANISM: a condition names the most OBSERVABLE proxy for its event, because observability is exactly what makes it feel checkable AT THE MOMENT YOU WRITE IT** — *"doc 170 lands"* is easy to verify; *"worker's next doc lands"* requires knowing the chains. ⛔ **And the proxy is right MOST of the time, which is what lets it survive review.** ⇒ ⭐⭐ ***the authoring test is NOT "is this checkable" but "CAN THIS BE CHECKED AND BE WRONG AT THE SAME TIME?"*** ⭐⭐ **THE PAIR is the half neither instance shows alone: `24.76`'s trigger FIRED AND LOOKED SILENT; `#74`'s LOOKED FIRED AND WAS SILENT** — both invisible from the wording, both cured by *what EVENT does this name, and can I observe that event directly?* ⭐ **[L118](#118)'s family aimed at CONDITIONS not states, and worse for it:** L118's proxy gives a wrong ANSWER someone can contradict; **a proxy in a TRIGGER governs whether an obligation is LIVE, so the failure produces NO answer — nobody acts, or someone acts on a false premise, and neither emits a signal.** ⚠ **Sibling of [L131](#131)**: L131's condition is satisfiable by worthless activity; this one names the wrong observable entirely. ⇒ **state the EVENT in the terms that make it true; if the honest form is harder to check, write it anyway and add the check.** ⚠ **All three authors had read the governing rule — one that same session. Knowing it does not protect you; checking does.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 177 | 2026-08-14 | [A SIGNAL WHOSE CONSUMER IS MORE EPHEMERAL THAN THE STATE IT REPORTS — everything is wired, and the record still does not outlast the fault](LESSONS.md#177) | `### 24.72` Leg A made a post-commit record fault typed and distinguishable. **On two of three production compositions the SAME typed failure has completely different remediation value:** `sourceIngestion` → `deps.health.surface` → `materializeHealthItem` ⇒ **a durable `HealthItem` an operator can act on tomorrow**; the approval path → `Result<void, FailureVariant>` → tRPC → **the approving user** ⇒ **a toast.** ⛔⛔ **NOT [L106](#106), and the difference is the point:** L106 is produced-and-DROPPED; **here the signal reaches a consumer perfectly well — the human who clicked approve. Nothing is dropped, nothing unwired, no component misbehaves.** ⭐⭐ **THE DEFECT IS A LIFETIME MISMATCH: the Markdown is DURABLE so the inconsistency PERSISTS, while the signal is transient and gone when dismissed** ⇒ ***the state outlives its only notification***, and no durable record says a committed mutation lacks its audit row. ⛔ **WHY IT SURVIVES A WIRING REVIEW: *"is it wired?"* is the WRONG QUESTION and it returns YES** — a reachability trace, `/wired`, an L106 audit all pass. ⇒ **the right question is *does the RECORD outlast the FAULT?*, and nothing in a call-path trace asks it, because LIFETIME IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE GRAPH.** ⭐ **Cheap tell: if remediation requires a human who is ALREADY LOOKING, the notification is the wrong shape for a durable fault** — a synchronous error suits *"your action failed, retry"* and not *"the system is now inconsistent."* ⚠ **Corollary, flagged by its own author: a fix's benefit can be COMPOSITION-DEPENDENT while its commit message is not.** Leg A's *"makes the fault observable and identifiable"* is true on the composition traced and false on one that was not; it never claimed universality and **will be read that way** beside a verdict saying *"it routes."* ⇒ **state which composition you measured, or measure them all.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 178 | 2026-08-14 | [A NON-VACUITY CONTROL AND AN APPLICABILITY CONTROL ARE DIFFERENT THINGS — and the second is the one nobody runs](LESSONS.md#178) | ⛔⛔ **NON-VACUITY: *did the instrument RUN?* · APPLICABILITY: *is this instrument VALID FOR THIS INPUT?*** ⇒ ***a non-vacuity control passes happily on an inapplicable instrument.*** **Every mechanism banked so far is an instrument FAILING; this is one WORKING PERFECTLY on the wrong input, and nothing in its output says so.** **Instance:** a diff-free comment-only checker, built to re-verify proofs computed on a lossy `git diff`, **had a positive control (it detected a known real change) and NO applicability control** — and produced two false alarms: a Markdown file run against a **TypeScript** comment-stripper, and ⭐⭐ **a check that cannot distinguish REFORMATTING from a logic change** (prettier reflow + trailing commas), where **normalising whitespace to compensate CORRUPTS STRING LITERALS — that file's fixtures include a deliberate three-space `"   "`.** ⇒ ***the test file most likely to need the check is the one the check corrupts.*** ⛔⛔ **Reflexively: both runs would have produced FABRICATED FINDINGS AGAINST SHIPPED WORK, from the audit commissioned to catch fabricated findings** — and a false finding against a landed commit is worse than the defect it hunts, because it arrives defended by a measurement. ⇒ **state the class of input your instrument is VALID for, and pick a control that would FAIL outside it; a positive control proves it fires, an applicability control proves you may READ the result.** ⚠ **Honest limit: both were caught by READING, which does not scale** — so an automated check whose applicability can only be established by hand is one you may run and **must not report unread**. ⭐ **[L160](#160) one step earlier** — L160 is a broken instrument that happens to be right; this is a working instrument pointed at input it cannot describe. `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 179 | 2026-08-14 | [WHEN TWO CENSUSES DISAGREE, THE DELTA IS THE FINDING — reconciling by picking a winner discards exactly the interesting set](LESSONS.md#179) | A name-keyed census found **3** `WriteSuccess` constructors; a compiler enumeration found **2**. ⛔ **The orchestrator had instructed the compiler REPLACE the name search** — *"a construction site is type-dependent, so the enumeration is COMPLETE."* **It is not: `as WriteSuccess` is an ASSERTION, not an assignment-checked construction, so a required field flags none of them** — true population **12 casts across four further files**, including `as unknown as WriteSuccess`. ⇒ ***the name-keyed census was the MORE complete one and was retracted in favour of the incomplete one.*** ⭐⭐ **The implementer's framing, adopted over "use the union": THE TWO METHODS FAIL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, SO THEIR DISAGREEMENT IS THE SIGNAL** — name-keyed **over**-includes and FOUND the casts; the compiler **under**-includes and EXCLUDED the mentions ⇒ **the delta is exactly the interesting set.** `3 vs 2` was **a discrepancy demanding an explanation, and the explanation was the finding.** ⛔ **The error, self-reported: *"I had both results in hand and reconciled them by picking a winner"* — and it recurred because the SAME shape appeared earlier in that task (`75`-vs-`3`) where picking a winner HAPPENED to be right.** ⭐ **[L160](#160) aimed at a RECONCILIATION METHOD rather than an instrument: a correct answer from a bad reconciliation is worse, because the reconciliation gets reused.** ⇒ **compute the delta, classify every member, name which method missed it and why; if you cannot explain the discrepancy you do not yet know the population.** ⚠ **Corollary: agreement is evidence only if the methods COULD have disagreed (`L155`); disagreement is useful only if you EXPLAIN rather than RESOLVE it.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 180 | 2026-08-17 | [A COUNT SOURCED FROM WHAT YOU PERSONALLY SAW IS A SAMPLE, and every careful person reports it as the population](LESSONS.md#180) | ⛔ Three people reported **3**, **5** and **6**; measured population **50 of 90**. Nobody miscounted — the unit was *briefs-I-recently-authored*. `L118` arriving through RECENCY rather than an instrument. **Name a count's UNIT and SOURCE; "instances I have seen" is a CANDIDATE.** `accepted: not mechanically enforceable` |
| 181 | 2026-08-17 | [THE DANGEROUS INSTRUMENTS FABRICATE *ABSENCE* — they return the reassuring answer](LESSONS.md#181) | Instrument **six**: turbo replayed 20 cached results in 55ms and reported green. **Seven**: `npx tsc` returns `TypeScript: No errors found` for EVERY invocation, and is **INTERMITTENT** — one clean probe is not clearance. `028`'s five fabricate findings; these fabricate absence. `pattern: \bnpx\s+(tsc\|vitest\|eslint\|turbo)\b` |
| 182 | 2026-08-17 | ["FILED SEPARATELY" IS WRITTEN WHEN THE AUTHOR IS LEAST LIKELY TO CHECK](LESSONS.md#182) | Three instances in one session of a split naming a task nobody created. **Ask whether it was FILED, not only whether it is BUILDABLE** — the phrase describes an intention and reads as a completed action. `accepted: candidate plan-lint rule` |
| 183 | 2026-08-17 | [A DERIVED NUMBER SILENTLY INHERITS ITS SOURCE'S UNIT DEFECT — subtracting two counts does not produce a count](LESSONS.md#183) | `17 − 15 = 2` "unidentified skippers" was arithmetically perfect and semantically void: 17 was a TOKEN count, 15 a BY-TYPE count. Truth was **one skipper and one non-member**. ⛔ **Subtraction LAUNDERS the unit error out of view** — a reader seeing `2` cannot ask what it counted. `accepted: publish both operands with their units` |
| 184 | 2026-08-17 | [THE CHECK THAT MATTERS IS THE ONE ON THE CLAIM YOU ALREADY BELIEVE](LESSONS.md#184) | `max(40)` "also kills ghp-style" was TRUE and worth **1-of-5**; measuring it before ruling kept a credential-rejection rationale off a rule-7 surface. ⛔ **Every discipline in this ledger triggers on DOUBT, and a believed claim generates none.** **Trigger: an "also"/"additionally" in a rationale you are about to approve.** `accepted` |
| 185 | 2026-08-17 | [EVIDENCE OFFERED IN SUPPORT OF A MEASUREMENT IS NOT ITSELF MEASURED](LESSONS.md#185) | A rigorous 64→17 sweep, then a **textual hit recorded as a writer** inside the very comment correcting a false producer credit, under a "measured, not assumed" banner. ⭐ **`L182`'s WHY — the artifact in front of you does not FEEL like the deliverable.** Three instances, one day, three roles. `accepted` |
| 186 | 2026-08-17 | [BEFORE EDITING A SPEC TOWARD WHAT SHIPPED, ASK WHETHER IT DESCRIBES OR CONSTRAINS](LESSONS.md#186) | ⛔ **A report is IMPROVED by matching reality; a CONTRACT is DESTROYED by it** — amend it and nothing is in breach, the same end state as the code having been right all along. **A doc edited to match a defect makes the defect UNFINDABLE.** Distinct from `### 24.54`: there a LINT demanded the widening, here DILIGENCE did. `accepted` |
| 187 | 2026-08-17 | [DEFEND A DECISION WHERE IT WILL BE UNDONE, NOT WHERE IT WAS MADE](LESSONS.md#187) | `24.84`'s deferral survived its session's death because the reason sat in the ENTRY, not on the dead `#73` pointer. ⭐ **Distinct from `L51`: that is DURABILITY, this is LOCATION — durable and un-encountered is indistinguishable from absent.** `accepted` |
| 188 | 2026-08-17 | [AN ARTIFACT THAT CARRIES A PREMISE IS AN INSTRUMENT](LESSONS.md#188) | An owner packet, a brief, a brief-refresh and reviewer prompts all shipped with false premises in one round. ⭐ **Three overclaims in one slice, every one PROSE or a NAME, none logic: the logic survives mutation, the SENTENCE ABOUT IT is what fails.** Route a premise-carrying artifact to someone who can FALSIFY it before it ships. `accepted` ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-08-17: the DOCBLOCK — the one type its rule named but never evidenced — now has a witness, and it is the worst: EXECUTED and it leaked. A source comment is trusted MOST and reviewed LEAST; proximity reads as provenance.** |
| 189 | 2026-08-17 | [BEFORE CONCLUDING A SUITE PASSES UNDER A CHANGE, PROVE THE CHANGE WAS IN EFFECT](LESSONS.md#189) | 511/511 green proves nothing until the change is shown live in that environment. ⭐ **A NEGATIVE result carries an applicability burden a positive one does not** — *"no impact"* is a claim about an ENVIRONMENT. **Build the control, use it, report it, DELETE it.** `accepted` |
| 190 | 2026-08-17 | [A SEARCH INSTRUMENT ANSWERS "WHERE DOES THIS TOKEN APPEAR?"](LESSONS.md#190) | Three witnesses in one day: a comment saying *"NOT written here"* counted as a writer · `17−15=2` over a token denominator · 14 literals-a-shape-rejects reported as 14 tests-that-break (got an area STAFFED). ⛔ **Re-derivation cannot catch it — the number was never wrong.** Report the PREDICATE, not just the unit. `accepted` |
| 191 | 2026-08-17 | [A CLAIM CHANGE OWES A CLAIM SWEEP](LESSONS.md#191) | A brief naming a location answers *"where is the claim I know about?"*, never *"where does this claim LIVE?"* — a rewritten doc block left `auditOf`'s docblock 11 lines away asserting the opposite. ⭐ Counter-example that WORKS: a census opening *"re-run rather than trusting the numbers"* defuses its own staleness ⇒ record the COMMAND, not the value. `accepted` ⛔ **AMENDED 2026-08-17: the trigger is broader and INVISIBLE — a FIXTURE change is a claim change; one data swap falsified 3 sentences, none in the diff. Grep the fixture's DESCRIPTION, not its value.** |
| 192 | 2026-08-17 | [AN ASSERTION OVER A HEURISTIC GATE IS DECORATIVE](LESSONS.md#192) | Unless the fixture TRIPS the heuristic. 3 pins asserted `isRedactionSafe`===true with a neutral sentinel matching none of its 3 regexes ⇒ could not fail for the reason they existed. `### 24.99`'s defect at test scope. Prove by mutation; state honest scope when a pin cannot be made discriminating. `pin: packages/knowledge/test/gcl-visibility-gate.test.ts` |
| 193 | 2026-08-17 | [MEASURED THE ARTIFACT I HAD OPEN, NOT THE ONE THE CODE WILL USE](LESSONS.md#193) | Orchestrator + implementer, same mechanism opposite directions, one message apart; each caught the other's, neither their own. ⛔ Re-derivation cannot catch it — the artifact was never wrong, it was never the one in play. Check dirty + generated (`git diff HEAD --stat -- <path>`); name WHICH WORLD. `accepted` |
| 194 | 2026-08-17 | [DELETING A FALSE ASSURANCE REPAIRS THE DOC AND LEAVES THE DEFECT](LESSONS.md#194) | ⭐ The overclaim was simultaneously the defect AND the only map to it. Inverse of *doc-edited-to-MATCH*: both edits are locally correct, both destroy the finding. Test is not *"is it true?"* but ***"what does it POINT AT, and does the pointer survive my fix?"*** Prefer true-by-construction; else demote and keep. `accepted` |
| 195 | 2026-08-17 | [A RETAINED-STRUCK RULING KEEPS ITS STATUS LINE](LESSONS.md#195) | Striking a block does not tense-shift `STATUS: RE-PUT, UNDECIDED` — an impl read it as live and nearly reopened a settled owner ruling. Live answer sat ~4.8k chars ABOVE the dead one. ⛔ Retain the REASONING, strip the STATE. Provenance and status want opposite things. `pattern: grep struck blocks for STATUS:/PENDING/UNDECIDED/AWAITING` |
| 196 | 2026-08-17 | [PIN A FIXTURE'S PREMISE AS THE CONJUNCTION IT IS](LESSONS.md#196) | A fixture chosen for slug-valid AND credential-shaped, pinned only by OUTCOME, degrades silently when either conjunct stops holding. ⭐ Pinning each conjunct makes the test fail toward a DECISION (re-decide `### 24.55`) instead of a REPAIR (swap in a passing fixture). Assertions should equal reasons-it-was-chosen. `pin: packages/knowledge/test/gcl-projection.test.ts` |
| 197 | 2026-08-17 | [A CORRECTED CLAIM MUST CARRY ITS PAST FAILURE AND ITS DIRECTION](LESSONS.md#197) | The corrected answer alone selects neither audit: once-too-NARROW invites "still incomplete", once-too-BROAD invites "still over-claims" — opposite checks. ⭐ Residual risk points the way the original error did, and only the record preserves that vector. `L153` half 1 aimed at docblocks; pairs with `L194`. `accepted` |

True ground is the OPEN-BY-CONSTRUCTION id set — and that ground has an owner and could itself expire. `accepted` |
| 199 | 2026-08-17 | ["I VERIFIED IT" FROM A SUBAGENT IS A CLAIM, NOT A MEASUREMENT](LESSONS.md#199) | security-reviewer "verified" a brief citation; code-quality-reviewer read it differently; `sed` settled it — the reviewer was wrong while saying "verified". ⭐ Two instruments that COULD disagree, and did, is the only reason anyone checked. Re-read any subagent-cited file:line before it enters a durable artifact. `accepted` |

Re-run the accused package isolated before attributing. `accepted` |
| 201 | 2026-08-17 | [`git show -U0 --` DUPLICATED ITS OWN DIFF](LESSONS.md#201) | One-file one-hunk commit emitted TWICE; counts exactly 2× (112/8 vs true 56/4). ⛔ This is the surface comment-only proofs are gated on, and `028` records the same family OMITTING ⇒ fails BOTH directions. Use `--numstat`, never count +/- from a diff body. ⭐ NOT grep — awk reproduced it; blaming the known-bad tool would have hidden it. `pattern` ⛔ **AMENDED same-day: orchestrator could NOT reproduce (1 hunk, 56/4, `--numstat` agreeing) ⇒ INTERMITTENT, which is WORSE — a clean probe is a third data point for non-determinism, never a refutation. "I checked, it's fine here" is the unsafe sentence.** ⛔ **EXTENDED 2026-08-18: `diff` said *"Files are identical"* for a provably-different pair (cmp + 2 shas disagreed), once, non-reproducing over 5 retries ⇒ the wrapped-tool class is NOT only `git`. Reassuring output from a wrapped tool needs a second surface (`cmp`/`shasum`).** |
| 202 | 2026-08-18 | [A LIVE HYPOTHESIS MAKES EVERY ANOMALY LOOK LIKE CONFIRMATION](LESSONS.md#202) | `--name-only` listed an unmodified file minutes after "git is wrapped" was banked — right shape, right tool, right day. ⛔ BENIGN: stat-cache, byte-identical to HEAD. A false data point inside a TRUE hypothesis is the worst kind. ⭐ The bar for adding to a hypothesis must rise as it strengthens. `accepted` |
| 203 | 2026-08-18 | [THE NUMBERED-DOC CONVENTION ASSUMES SERIALIZED CLOSE-OUTS](LESSONS.md#203) | 3 impls closed concurrently, all computed `max+1`=176. ⛔ A max+1 counter is a read-modify-write with no lock. ⚠ The track-prefix fix is scoped to MULTI-TRACK, but the real condition is CONCURRENT WRITERS — single-track team mode has them and is exempted. Allocate numbers in the dispatch. `accepted` ⛔ **AND THE REMEDY REPRODUCED THE DEFECT: assignment sent as a CORRECTION raced a commit already in flight (`9b838321`) ⇒ a correction issued into a running concurrent operation IS a concurrent write. A COMMITTED number is a stable ID — the assignment adapts to it, never the reverse.** |
| 204 | 2026-08-18 | [AN OPTION LIST IS AN ARTIFACT THAT CAN CARRY A DEFECT](LESSONS.md#204) | An orchestrator `ADD:` offered "throws or cuts maximally" as equivalent; `throw` would have escaped uncaught on `applyPlan` — no `try` anywhere, §16 never-throw, sole-writer path, rule 1. ⛔ Step 2.5 is understood as review of the implementer's TESTS; the REVIEWER's reply lands in the slot with the least scrutiny, and enumeration implies vetting. ⭐ Its only reviewer is its executor. ⇒ State the CONSTRAINT the options are filtered against, not just the options. ⚠ Executing side: an option list is a HYPOTHESIS, not a grant — check each branch against the target file's documented constraints. The implementer reading the docblock beats the reviewer remembering the architecture. `accepted` |
| 205 | 2026-08-18 | [A SUCCESS EXIT IS AMBIGUOUS BETWEEN "IT WORKED" AND "IT NEVER APPLIED"](LESSONS.md#205) | `rm -f` exited 0 without deleting — stale cwd, relative path resolved to nothing, and `-f` is DEFINED to make a missing target a success. ⭐ PROMOTED to subsume the mutation rule rather than sit beside it: RED is self-proving, GREEN/zero is ambiguous, and that is a fact about exit codes generally — `sed` matching nothing, a patch on a stale copy, a write under an unexpected symlink. ⛔ FAILURE is informative and SUCCESS is not, so the reassuring outcome carries no information AND is the one nobody re-checks. ⇒ Verify the effect with a DIFFERENT instrument than the one that reported success; re-running the same command is not a second instrument. ⚠ cwd persists between calls here. `accepted` |
| 206 | 2026-08-18 | [THE CROSS-TERRITORY DISCRIMINATOR IS NOT "WHOSE FOLDER"](LESSONS.md#206) | Two cross-territory edits in one round, handled two ways, BOTH correct: a `packages/policy` note authored verbatim by the orchestrator and transcribed, vs two `packages/workflows` fixtures fixed by the party whose change broke them (`L121`). ⇒ ⭐ Requires REASONING about the target's logic ⇒ owner does it, or it is authored verbatim and transcribed. MECHANICALLY FORCED by your own change and assertion-preserving ⇒ the breaking party does it. ⛔ "Whose folder" gives the same answer to both and separates nothing. ⚠ BOTH owe disclosure at the owner's next slice boundary — an unannounced cross-territory commit and an improvised one are indistinguishable from inside the diff. `accepted` |
| 207 | 2026-08-18 | [A COMPILER ENUMERATION IS ONLY AS WIDE AS THE COMPILATION YOU RAN](LESSONS.md#207) | `### 24.98` made a field required so `tsc` would enumerate construction sites; that enumeration stopped at the UNION boundary, which is why `### 24.103` exists. ⛔ Then `24.103`'s own `tsc` ran INSIDE `packages/knowledge` and reported "10 sites, zero in tests" — repo-wide it is 12, with 2 in `packages/workflows` test files. ⇒ the instrument fixing a boundary-limited enumeration was boundary-limited one scope up. ⭐ The `--listFiles` non-vacuity proof could not help: it showed the compilation READ 54 test files, not that it was pointed at the right tree (`L178`). ⛔ The orchestrator then "confirmed" it with a hand table ALSO scoped to `packages/knowledge` — two instruments sharing a boundary, agreement carrying no information, relayed upward as concordance. ⇒ REPORT THE BOUNDARY BESIDE THE COUNT; before citing two counts as corroborating, state what each could have returned that the other could not. ⚠ Every whole-program instrument has a program boundary you chose and it never appears in the output. `accepted` |
| 208 | 2026-08-18 | [A REGEX SAFETY CUT MUST BE PROVEN AGAINST LINE TERMINATORS — the failure is fail-OPEN](LESSONS.md#208) | `^(.*?\b<region>\b)[./].*$` without `s`: `.` does not match a line terminator and `$` (no `m`) matches only at end of input ⇒ a key containing `\n`/`\r`/U+2028/U+2029 makes the pattern FAIL and the `?? path` fallback returns the path VERBATIM. ⛔ Fail-OPEN, invisible to every single-line fixture — which is every fixture written by hand. ⭐⭐ And `isRedactionSafe` returned TRUE on the leaked value (a codename matches no credential pattern, as `audit-signal.ts` warns about itself) ⇒ this cut IS the control, nothing is behind it — the strongest evidence yet for `L103`. ⭐ Found by BUILDING THE INPUT AND RUNNING IT; reading the pattern does not surface the flag. ⇒ fixture all four terminators + both dialects + trailing; state each flag and why; ⛔ and make the FALLBACK fail-closed — `?? path` is the defect independent of the flags. `pin` + `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 209 | 2026-08-18 | [A GUARD WRITTEN IN THE FUTURE TENSE ABOUT AN ALREADY-TRUE CONDITION CLOSES THE QUESTION IT EXISTS TO OPEN](LESSONS.md#209) | ⛔ A landed `visibility.ts` residual named its own contingency responsibly — *"IF the id set were EVER architecturally fixed to a closed set ... NO SUCH CHANGE IS PROPOSED"* — and **the condition was already met when that sentence was written** (`### 24.111`: the sole production create path derives the id from a closed 3-value enum). ⇒ ***a reader who dutifully checks the contingency reads "no such change is proposed" and STOPS.*** ⛔ **Worse than a rotted pointer: every citation RESOLVES, so the reader gets a clean read and a wrong conclusion — nothing snags.** ⭐ The author was being MORE diligent than average; the defect is entirely in the TENSE, and a hypothetical never presents as a claim so it never recruits scrutiny. **Do: write every contingency in the tense of its CURRENT truth value + the measurement establishing it; if unmeasured, say "not checked as of <date>".** Kin `L106`, `L195`. `pattern:` warn-grep future-tense contingency markers · `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 210 | 2026-08-18 | [A QUOTED HEREDOC FEEDING ANOTHER LANGUAGE MAKES `\n` A LITERAL BACKSLASH-N](LESSONS.md#210) | ⛔ `<<'PY'` suppresses shell interpolation (the reason you use it), so `\n` passes through untouched; if the inner language does not interpret it in that position either, **NEITHER layer consumes it and it lands in the source file** — which produced a `TS1127` that failed a whole package and took three sessions plus a ruled-out line-separator hypothesis to find, **because the symptom is a parse error and nothing points at the WRITER**. ⭐ **A heredoc that produces a corrupt file EXITS 0.** **Do: verify the BYTES of any file written through a nested writer, never the exit code; build a needed literal escape in code (`chr(92)+"n"`) so no layer can eat it.** `pattern:` grep the written file and account for every hit · `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 211 | 2026-08-18 | [A SIDE-BY-SIDE READ OF TWO NEAR-IDENTICAL LISTS *CONFIRMS* RATHER THAN CATCHES](LESSONS.md#211) | ⛔ `packages/policy` and `packages/domain` credential lists diverged by **ONE FLAG on ONE pattern** (`CREDENTIAL_PREFIX` lost `/i`) while **the sibling pattern in the same file KEPT its `/i`** ⇒ ***two of three matched, the reader's sample confirmed "these are the same", and they stopped before reaching the one that differed.*** ⭐ **A human comparing two long near-identical lists is running a SAMPLING procedure that terminates early on agreement — the opposite of what a divergence hunt needs, and the more similar the lists the faster it terminates and the more confident it feels.** **Do: never establish list parity by reading — extract mechanically and `diff`; delete the copy where possible, pin parity where not.** Kin `L46`. `pattern:` mechanical diff of extracted lists · `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 212 | 2026-08-18 | [VERIFY A MUTATION BY ITS *EFFECT* AS WELL AS BY ITS DIFF](LESSONS.md#212) | ⛔⛔ `029`'s remedy for an ambiguous GREEN mutation is *prove it applied, by diff* — **and `diff` is on this project's unreliable-instrument list.** It reported *"Files are identical"* for a provably different pair (non-reproducing; recorded as a NON-admitted candidate on `### 24.122`, `L202` applied by the implementer it would have benefited). ⇒ ⛔ **the structural point survives regardless: `diff` failed toward a FABRICATED ABSENCE — the SAME direction as the failure the check screens for, so a broken instrument and a real finding are indistinguishable AT the check.** **Do: a GREEN mutation needs two independent signals — the diff (it changed) AND the effect (something observably responded); a RED outcome is self-proving and needs neither.** ⚠ Generalises: any check whose failure mode points the same way as the condition it screens for cannot screen for it alone. `pin:` /tdd Step 6 · `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 213 | 2026-08-18 | [RECORD THE BASE, DERIVE THE COUNT — never store a derivable quantity in an artifact that mutates when you store it](LESSONS.md#213) | ⛔ The tracker's snapshot-of-NOW line carried *"37 commits · 216 unpushed"*, both measured immediately before the commit that carried them ⇒ **both off by one the instant they landed.** ⭐⭐ **A count recorded in the document whose commit changes the count is UNFIXABLE BY CONSTRUCTION — every correction is itself falsifying**, so no value written there can be true when read; care cannot reach it. ⛔ **Worse than an absent number: a missing figure prompts measurement, a stale-but-plausible one invites RESTATEMENT** — the behaviour behind three separate errors in one hour. **Do: record the BASE (ref/hash/date) + the deriving COMMAND, never the quantity.** ⚠ Generalises to test totals, file counts, "N of M passing" in any doc that is part of the population it counts. ⭐ Its punchline is three layers deep — `### 24.122` (a task about an instrument that MISCOUNTS COMMITS) was filed in a message that miscounted commits, on a line that cannot count commits correctly. Kin `L180` (wrong POPULATION; this is wrong TIMING). `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 214 | 2026-08-18 | [A DONE-WHEN THAT CANNOT BE SATISFIED WITHOUT CROSSING AN OWNER GATE IS A BLOCKED TASK WEARING A COMPLETABLE ONE'S CLOTHES](LESSONS.md#214) | ⛔ `### 24.123`'s Done-when required *"measured against a real vault population"* — **which IS a scan over real vault content, and that is a standing owner gate.** ⇒ ⭐⭐ **the MORE DILIGENT the implementer, the more certainly they cross it**: someone treating a Done-when as binding walks through, because the Done-when is the artifact they were told is authoritative and the gate lives in a handoff they were not told to read. ⛔ **Nothing connects the two AT THE POINT OF ACTION.** Third instance in one round of a requirement whose satisfiability depends on another decision's state ⇒ a CLASS. **Do: (1) MARK the state on the checkbox line (`BLOCKED-ON-<what>`), never annotate it in the body — a body note is read after the reader already decided the task is actionable; (2) NAME the honest path that does not cross the gate, else the task gets satisfied by an invented substitute reported as the real thing.** ⚠ Fails toward ACTION, unlike most stale-state defects. Kin `L92`. `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 215 | 2026-08-18 | [AN ASSERTED ABSENCE ACROSS A SESSION BOUNDARY IS AN INFORMATION-AVAILABILITY DEFECT, NOT A DISCIPLINE FAILURE](LESSONS.md#215) | ⛔⛔ **Three instances in one evening from three different seats:** the LEAD relayed *"UNRECORDED, file it"* about a finding filed 5 minutes earlier · the ORCHESTRATOR reported both Step-9s unreviewed from `182 §7c` when `§1` had been refreshed later in the same doc · an IMPLEMENTER reported three items *"still unfiled"* that were filed, in a commit crediting them as the finder. ⭐⭐ **None was careless — each asserted something true of what they could SEE.** ⇒ ***nobody can observe what landed in a session they were not in, so a negative claim about project state is a claim about a population the speaker structurally cannot survey.*** ⛔ **"Be more careful" cannot reach it: an absence feels identical from inside whether it is real or merely unobserved**, and it is the CALMING direction. ⚠ More parallel sessions ⇒ worse. **Do: GREP FIRST with a positive control, or say "I have not checked."** Kin `L180` (its positive form; the negative form is worse because a confident absence CLOSES the question). `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 216 | 2026-08-18 | [A GUARD'S PLACEMENT IS WORTHLESS UNLESS IT RENDERS ON THE SURFACE THE EDITOR ACTUALLY SEES — and a guard must never carry a census](LESSONS.md#216) | ⛔ **Measured against the TS language service: `//` comments NEVER appear in quick info; only JSDoc does.** So a `//` fence at `toUiSafeEgressStatus` would have left the EXISTING *"mirrors `systemHealth.ts`'s `toUiSafeEgressStatus`"* as the **only** hover text — at the moment of the dangerous edit, to the reader the fence targets, and it is the sentence the fence contradicts. ⭐ **The misleading text as SOLE SURVIVOR of the surface the guard defends — worse than never writing the guard, because the reader leaves CONFIRMED in the wrong belief.** `L187`'s rendering half: right file and line are not enough if the tooling does not show it. ⛔ **Second half — the same fence said *"this repo has THREE `redactString` implementations"*: a guard carrying a count rots on someone else's schedule and ROTS TOWARD DELETION** (a verifier counts four, sees three, and now has documentary evidence the guard is stale — about the clause establishing reachability). ⭐⭐ **GENERAL RULE, and it was never about counts: A GUARD'S CLAIMS MUST BE FALSIFIABLE *IN PLACE*, OR NAME WHERE TO CHECK THEM.** ⛔ **Subsumes `029`'s rotted-pointer finding — a cited line number, a claimed call-site total, an "only two producers" and a package census are ONE defect, and all fail toward DELETION.** **The decidable test: a count you can check by READING THE SENTENCE is fine; one you must LEAVE THE FILE to check is not** — the same fence's *"REACHABLE BY THREE ROUTES"* was correctly KEPT (it enumerates its own three in that sentence, self-verifying in place). ⇒ ***the test is not "is it a number" but "can the reader falsify it without leaving the guard?"*** **Keep unverifiable-in-place counts in the tracker, where a number is a measurement.** Kin `L152`. `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 217 | 2026-08-18 | [THE SHELL'S CWD PERSISTS BETWEEN TOOL CALLS — every repo-relative path is an unchecked assumption](LESSONS.md#217) | ⛔ Three instances, one session: a `git commit` refused (*"pathspec … did not match"*) because cwd had persisted to `apps/worker`, and an `rm -f` that **exited 0 without deleting anything** for the same reason. ⭐⭐ **The two outcomes are the lesson and they are opposite: the pathspec form FAILED SAFE — refused rather than guessing, nothing lost; `rm -f` FAILED SILENT — exit 0, no deletion, no message.** ⇒ ***one root cause, loud on one command and invisible on another, so the ones that bite are the ones with a permissive flag*** — and `-f` exists precisely to suppress the complaint that would have surfaced it. **Do: pass the root explicitly every time (`git -C <root>`, absolute paths, or a leading `cd`); treat a `0` exit from `rm -f`/`mkdir -p`/`cp -f` as NO information and verify the EFFECT** (`L212` via the filesystem). ⚠ The harness reports cwd persistence nowhere, so drift accumulates silently and the eventual reveal gets blamed on the command. `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 218 | 2026-08-18 | [NAMING YOUR OWN UNCERTAINTY IN AN INSTRUCTION IS WHAT BUYS THE CONSTRUCTION](LESSONS.md#218) | ⛔ An orchestrator attached an `ADD:` — *prove stripping cannot destroy a match* — **while writing that their own reasoning said "probably not"**. The implementer **CONSTRUCTED the counter-example** instead of executing it as written, and **both approvals died** (`### 24.120`). ⭐⭐ **Their account points at the SENDER: the hedge is what made building the input the obvious next move — an `ADD:` stated CONFIDENTLY would most likely have been executed as written, fixture and all, asserting the false thing with a green suite and two approvals behind it.** ⛔ **So the rule is NOT "implementers should construct" — that credits the receiver for what the sender caused. An instruction resting on unverified reasoning MUST SAY SO INSIDE THE INSTRUCTION.** ⭐ *"Prove X"* states a conclusion and invites a demonstrating fixture; *"Prove X — my reasoning says probably, and I have not run it"* states a task and invites an attempt to break it. ⛔ Inverts `029`'s orchestrator amendment: **both directions of the chain are governed by the writer, not the reader.** Kin `L202` (`202` is why you don't check your own claim; `218` is how you get someone else to). `accepted: partially enforceable` |
| 219 | 2026-08-18 | [A SCRATCH FILE IN A COLLECTED TEST PATH IS NOT LOCAL](LESSONS.md#219) | ⛔ Four untracked probes reded `@sow/domain#typecheck` (`TS2532`), and **`@sow/domain` is upstream of nearly everything**, so the graph was red for every concurrent session. ⭐⭐ **Invisible to exactly one person — its author**, who knows the files are theirs and temporary, so the cost never lands where the knowledge is. ⛔⛔ **And it appears in NOBODY's diff: the pathspec discipline that protects every commit here defends nothing, because the file is never staged** — a shared-tree hazard with no commit-side surface. ⚠ One session spent **four independent checks** to attribute it before it could report its own work honestly. **Do: scratch harnesses live OUTSIDE collected paths; if inside, delete the moment the measurement is read — the window IS the hazard — and verify by LISTING, not by `rm -f`'s exit code (`L217`).** ⭐⭐ **Addendum: the lead attributed another session's red to these files pre-measurement, struck it, and a real witness appeared later on a different gate. The strike STANDS — *a conclusion that happens to be right does not retroactively repair the method that produced it.*** Kin `L217`, `L109`. `accepted: partially enforceable` |
